> - **Physics-consistent imagination ≠ physics-consistent action** — this is the gap to test; if it's small, the direction collapses. → H5's Pearson $\rho$ between $\sum P_i$ and SR is the go/no-go before scaling.
> - **Reward hacking** — [[2512.00425|NewtonRewards]] documented it on the generation side; the action-side analog (model freezes) is likely. → Static-output detection (σ drop > 2×) + [[2509.15194|EVOL-RL]] novelty diversity defend (tied to H5).
> - **'Physics-as-a-differentiable-loss' is already done generically** — [[2603.14469|PIPER]] enforces a Lagrangian-consistency residual inside the actor's objective (+20–45% sample-efficiency), so the loss *mechanism* is not the contribution. → Pin the wedge to the *verifiable per-law predicate SET* (training-set-independent invariants) and run H1's head-to-head vs PIPER's generic residual on OOD Safe-SR; if the predicate set does not out-extrapolate PIPER, A3 reduces to it.

### A4 — Multi-Actor World-Action Models: Decomposing Ego- from Exogenous-Caused Change

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | A — Architecture & Training |
| **Thesis** | A manipulation or navigation scene can hold more than one causal actor — a human collaborator, a second robot — but this doc's own joint $p(o',a\mid o,l)$ is well-posed only if the robot is the sole source of $o'$-change. The field assumes any scene change the robot didn't cause is noise to subtract, not a second process worth predicting correctly. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2605.12090\|WAM-Survey]] (survey), [[2604.22748\|Agentic-World-Modeling-Survey]] (survey), [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] (benchmark), [[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]] (method), [[2603.15359\|NavThinker]] (method), [[2409.00215\|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | exogenous-branch causal-consistency Pearson **ρ > 0.7** (B1's own bar) on second-actor probe trials, where a standard single-actor WAM's implicit exogenous prediction sits at B1's negative-control floor (near-chance); [[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]]'s reported Drift-Energy cut of up to **90%** ([[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]]) reframed and quantified as the reversed correct-exogenous-prediction metric's miss-rate on genuine second-actor probes; [[2603.15359\|NavThinker]]'s **59.46%** SR / **55.00%** SPL on Social-HM3D as the existence-proof anchor that action-conditioned joint ego+partner prediction is achievable, ported from a navigation-RL reward term into a manipulation WAM's own $p(o',a\mid o,l)$ objective |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: A1's own formal framing states the joint's data pairs "each future observation with the action that caused it" — well-posed only when the robot's action is the sole source of $o'$-change; B1's own validity apparatus is built to punish the opposite finding, scoring a WAM near-chance when "only the scene, not the action, is perturbed"; [[2608.04653|CoCo-WorldModel]]'s Drift-Energy objective — the field's own anti-hallucination fix — explicitly penalizes any action-independent motion (cut up to **90%**), so the sharpest tool against imagination-drift doubles as a tool against ever getting a second actor right.
- **Today's answers**: the field already predicts a second body when it has to — [[2603.15359|NavThinker]] couples an action-conditioned world model to a policy that predicts "the future evolution of scene geometry and human trajectories" together, hitting **59.46%** SR on Social-HM3D — but folds the human's future into ONE undifferentiated $o'$ conditioned only on the robot's own action, with no ego/exogenous split and no validated exogenous sub-score, and the coupling lives in navigation-RL reward shaping, not a manipulation WAM's $p(o',a\mid o,l)$. [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]] estimates a real human co-carrier's 6-DoF intent online (task time **83%** of admittance control, human impulse **−24%**) — but via a classical particle filter feeding an impedance-control law, entirely outside any learned WAM, exactly the "separate perception module feeding a planner" pattern that never reaches a training objective.
- **The opening**: [[2606.02486|AHEAD]] already names the failure mode in manipulation terms — "VLA models... fail when objects move independently during task execution" — and wraps a frozen VLA in a lightweight latent WM that catches a projectile 19/30 times where baselines score 0/30; but the "independent" mover is physics (a ball, a conveyor), not an agent with adaptive intent, and the wrapper never touches B1's causal-consistency score. Between NavThinker's coupling, AHEAD's independent-motion framing, and CoCo-WorldModel's ready-made (but inverted) counterfactual machinery, the pieces for a decomposed, validated exogenous branch already exist — nobody has assembled them into one $p(o',a\mid o,l)$ that admits a second actor.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A manipulation or navigation scene can contain more than one causal actor. The joint $p(o',a\mid o,l)$ this doc's own Formal Framing defines is only well-posed when the robot's action $a$ is the *sole* source of $o'$-change — a property of single-actor worlds, not a property of embodied worlds in general.
- **Assumption being challenged**: A1's own thesis is single-actor by construction — the joint is natural only "given the current view and the instruction," with no term for a second actor's own contribution. B1's validity apparatus holds the same assumption from the evaluation side: its action-vs-scene negative control is *designed* to score a WAM near-chance when the scene, not the action, changes — correctly flagging hallucination on single-actor benchmarks, but on a genuine second-actor task it would flag a WAM that got the *partner's* motion right as if it had hallucinated. [[2608.04653|CoCo-WorldModel]]'s own Multi-Step Counterfactual Consistency loss enforces this at training time — a zero-action rollout is *penalized for any predicted motion at all* — treating exogenous change as a defect to cut (up to 90%) rather than a second stream worth being right about. [[2607.09185|CD-LAM]] names the same pattern one level down, in the latent itself: "action-relevant dynamics entangled with action-irrelevant visual factors (e.g., background, non-interacted objects)," debiased away by construction rather than modeled. [[2603.15359|NavThinker]] and [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]] show the assumption is not universal in the wider field — but neither folds the second actor into the WAM's *own* joint objective, so within this doc's Cluster A/B corpus the single-actor assumption stands unchallenged.
- **The bet**: On tasks with a genuine second causal actor — a human collaborator, a second independently-controlled robot — a WAM trained with an explicit ego/exogenous decomposition (an action-conditioned branch $p(o'_{\text{ego}}\mid o,a)$ plus a branch conditioned on the partner's own recent trajectory $p(o'_{\text{exo}}\mid o,\tau_{\text{partner}})$) clears B1's own causal-consistency bar — Pearson **ρ > 0.7** — on the exogenous branch specifically, where a standard single-actor WAM's implicit exogenous prediction sits at B1's negative-control floor (near-chance) on the same trials, because the standard model was never trained or scored on getting a second actor's future right — only, in cases like [[2608.04653|CoCo-WorldModel]], trained to suppress it as drift.

**Related research papers.**

Nine systems that touch a second body's motion somewhere in the pipeline — as noise to eliminate, as an external perception input to a planner, or folded into one undifferentiated future without a validated branch — none decomposing a WAM's own joint into a validated ego-caused and exogenous-caused stream. The axis is *whether the second body is genuinely uncontrolled by the policy (not a second effector of the same policy), and where its predicted future goes*:

| System | Second-body treatment (controllable by the policy? where does its future go?) | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] | — (sets the wall) — no second body in the benchmark at all, single-actor by construction | interpolation transfers, extrapolation **0%** (reach/push only) | not a second-actor benchmark; the wall it measures is morphology, not causal-actor count — A4's own wall has no benchmark yet |
| [[2607.09185\|CD-LAM]] | uncontrolled — background/non-interacted objects entangled as *noise*, debiased out of the latent by construction | zero-transition response **0.527→0.043** | treats exogenous visual factors purely as contamination to suppress via contrastive/calibration losses, never as a second signal worth predicting correctly |
| [[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]] | uncontrolled — the training loss (MSC2/ASC2) explicitly penalizes any action-independent motion, by construction | Drift Energy cut up to **90%** on BAIR/RoboNet | the field's sharpest anti-hallucination objective doubles as an anti-second-actor objective — A4's own bet is the reversed miss-rate on this exact metric |
| [[2605.29360\|MiraBench]] | uncontrolled, scene-fixed — Optimism-Bias Detection flags over-confident WMs, never a second agent's own trajectory | de-biasing gap **48.7%→12.8%** | scene-fixed probes vary environment state, never condition on a second actor's own independently-generated action |
| [[2605.27589\|What-If-World]] | uncontrolled — six physical primitives (friction, force) span Environment + Interaction domains, no Agent-vs-Agent axis | no SOTA model clears **52%** paired-causal | scene-counterfactual by prompt edit, never conditions on a second actor's own policy |
| [[2603.15359\|NavThinker]] | uncontrolled, genuinely independent (pedestrians) — folded into ONE undifferentiated $o'$ conditioned only on the robot's own action | **59.46%** SR / **55.00%** SPL, Social-HM3D | proves action-conditioned joint prediction of a real second actor works — in navigation-RL reward shaping, not a manipulation WAM's own $p(o',a\mid o,l)$, with no ego/exo decomposition or validated exogenous sub-score |
| [[2409.00215\|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]] | uncontrolled, genuinely independent (human co-carrier) — external classical estimator (particle filter), not folded into any learned WAM | task time **83%** of admittance control, human impulse **−24%** | the sharpest evidence a second actor's intent can be estimated online without force sensors — but as a perception module feeding a control law, never a training objective inside a WAM's joint loss |
| [[2606.02486\|AHEAD]] | uncontrolled, but *physics not agent* (rolling ball, conveyor, projectile) | **19/30** projectile catching (vs **0/30** baselines) | names "objects move independently" as the exact failure mode A4 targets — but the wrapper is a frozen-VLA add-on trained on physics extrapolation, not an agent with adaptive intent, and never touches B1's causal-consistency score |
| [[2507.15597\|Being-H0]] | not a partner — a human demonstrator, offline, no concurrent execution | **25%** teleop data matches 50–100%-data baselines | treats the human purely as a trajectory *source* to imitate, never a body co-present with the robot during execution |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (an ego/exogenous decomposition beats a single-actor WAM on getting a second actor's future right), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — An ego/exogenous decomposition clears B1's causal-consistency bar on the exogenous branch where a single-actor WAM sits at the negative-control floor.**
   - *Prediction*: a WAM with a separate branch conditioned on the partner's recent trajectory ($p(o'_{\text{exo}}\mid o,\tau_{\text{partner}})$) reaches Pearson **ρ > 0.7** against real second-actor rollouts, while a standard single-actor WAM's implicit exogenous prediction on the same trials sits at B1's negative-control floor (near-chance) — the standard model was never trained to be right about a future it didn't cause.
   - *Test*: build a minimal second-actor task suite (a scripted or teleoperated partner layered onto an existing LIBERO/RoboTwin-style task); before scoring anything, audit the suite itself — measure and report the fraction of frames where the partner is occluded from the robot's onboard camera by the robot's own body, gripper, or the manipulated object, broken out separately for the contact/handoff phase; a suite built without this check would report a falsely optimistic exogenous-branch score on frames where the partner was never actually visible. Then train a decomposed WAM and a standard single-actor WAM on matched data; score each branch's ASR+COD against real second-actor rollouts.
   - *Row*: NavThinker (the existence proof the coupling is achievable) / CoCo-WorldModel (the single-actor baseline whose training actively suppresses the exogenous signal).
   - *Falsifier*: the decomposed WAM's exogenous branch does not clear ρ > 0.7, or a standard single-actor WAM already matches it → the decomposition buys nothing over an undifferentiated joint.
2. **H2 — B1's negative control reverses on second-actor tasks: scoring correct exogenous prediction flips today's WAM rankings.**
   - *Prediction*: re-scoring a [[2608.04653|CoCo-WorldModel]]-style Drift-Energy-minimizing WAM on second-actor probe trials for CORRECT exogenous prediction — rather than penalizing any action-independent motion — flips its ranking against a WAM never trained to suppress drift: the Drift-Energy leader scores worst on the reversed metric, because its training objective drove exogenous prediction toward zero regardless of whether a second actor was actually moving.
   - *Test*: take a WAM trained with CoCo-WorldModel-style MSC2/ASC2 losses; run it on second-actor probe trials; compute both the original Drift-Energy metric and a reversed exogenous-accuracy metric (does the predicted $o'$ correctly show the second actor's actual motion); compare the two rankings.
   - *Row*: CoCo-WorldModel (the loss to re-score) / MiraBench (the optimism-bias filter machinery this reversal adapts).
   - *Falsifier*: Drift-Energy-suppressing WAMs still score well on the reversed metric → the loss only suppresses motion when none is present, and B1's negative control does not need redesigning for second-actor tasks.
3. **H3 — A3's physics predicates need a third-body term on genuine two-actor tasks.**
   - *Prediction*: A3's closed-system momentum predicate P1 ($|\Delta p_{\text{total}}| < 0.05\,p_{\max}$ off-contact, a 2-body budget) registers a spurious violation on a genuine co-manipulation task, because a second actor imparts force on the shared object outside the robot's own action; extending P1 to a 3-body budget (robot + object + partner) recovers predicate validity without adding a false-violation rate on single-actor tasks.
   - *Test*: run A3's 5 physics predicates on [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]]-style co-carrying rollouts; compare the 2-body momentum predicate's violation rate against a 3-body-extended version using the partner's measured force/motion.
   - *Row*: Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation (the real 2-actor force-sharing task).
   - *Falsifier*: the 2-body predicate's violation rate on co-manipulation tasks is statistically indistinguishable from single-actor tasks → 2-body suffices in practice and the third-body term is unnecessary overhead.
4. **H4 — Co-training the exogenous branch beats bolting it onto a frozen policy.**
   - *Prediction*: jointly training the ego and exogenous branches (à la A1's co-evolution) recovers more of H1's gain than [[2606.02486|AHEAD]]-style bolting a frozen-wrapper exogenous predictor onto an already-frozen single-actor policy, because the ego branch's own action-conditioning must be trained aware of the exogenous branch to avoid misattributing the partner's motion to its own action.
   - *Test*: compare a jointly-trained ego/exo decomposition against an AHEAD-style frozen-VLA-plus-separately-trained exogenous wrapper, holding data and backbone fixed; report the ASR+COD gap between the two on H1's suite.
   - *Row*: AHEAD (the frozen-wrapper baseline this H tests against).
   - *Falsifier*: the bolted-on wrapper matches the jointly-trained decomposition → co-training is unnecessary and A4 reduces to an AHEAD-style add-on.
5. **H5 — Folding exogenous prediction into the joint loss beats a classical external estimator under mutual coupling, once sensing modality is controlled for.**
   - *Prediction*: on tasks where the partner's motion is contingent on the robot's own action (mutual coupling — [[2603.15359|NavThinker]]'s own framing: "a robot's actions can influence how humans move"), a WAM with the exogenous branch folded into the same joint training objective beats [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]]'s classical particle-filter-plus-impedance-control pipeline on task SR and human-effort reduction *when both systems are fed the same sensing modality*, because the external module cannot correct the WM's own action-conditioned imagination when the coupling runs both ways — not because it happens to run on a sensor that never occludes.
   - *Test*: on a co-carrying or handover task with genuine mutual coupling, run a sensing-modality-matched comparison — either (a) a camera-only WAM vs. a camera-only re-implementation of Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation's particle-filter-plus-impedance-control pipeline, or (b) a WAM fed the same OptiTrack/mocap-tracked object-motion signal Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation uses vs. that pipeline as published — holding the base policy fixed; report SR and human impulse/effort reduction for the matched pair. Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation's own KH note confirms its particle filters observe only OptiTrack-tracked object kinematics ("crucially, force-torque sensor data is not used for observation"), never a camera view of the human — so comparing a camera-fed WAM directly against its published numbers, without matching modality, cannot separate "folded-in beats external module" from "the external module happened to use a sensor that doesn't occlude."
   - *Row*: Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation (the classical external-module baseline, and the occlusion-immune OptiTrack modality this control matches against) / NavThinker (the mutual-coupling framing this borrows).
   - *Falsifier*: under a matched sensing modality, the classical external module still matches or beats the folded-in decomposition → mutual coupling doesn't require joint training, and a separate perception module is sufficient. (If only the unmatched camera-vs-mocap comparison favors the classical module, that is a sensing-modality artifact, not evidence against folding exogenous prediction into the joint loss.)
6. **H6 — The exogenous branch's causal-consistency score collapses under gripper/object occlusion in the contact window, and a camera-agnostic proxy signal recovers it.**
   - *Prediction*: in H1's suite, during the final reach-to-contact/handoff phase — where the gripper or manipulated object crosses the onboard camera's line of sight to the partner — partner-visibility drops below **30%** of frames, and the exogenous branch's ASR+COD causal-consistency score collapses from **ρ > 0.7** (measured on the unobstructed 80% of the trial) to below **0.3** in the occluded window — B1's card fixes no exact numeric threshold for its negative-control floor, only "near-chance," so **0.3** is H6's own operational reading of that floor, chosen in the weak/negligible-correlation range; a camera-agnostic proxy signal (wrist-mounted view, third-person view, or shared-object motion tracking à la [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]]'s OptiTrack-based estimator, which tracks the object/human directly rather than through the robot's onboard viewpoint) recovers **ρ > 0.5** in the same occluded window.
   - *Test*: instrument H1's suite with per-frame partner-visibility logging from the robot's onboard camera; isolate the contact/handoff phase; compute the exogenous branch's ρ separately for unoccluded and occluded frames, once for an onboard-camera-only branch and once for a camera-agnostic proxy branch trained on the same rollouts.
   - *Row*: Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation (the occlusion-immune sensing modality the proxy branch borrows) / NavThinker (unoccluded-by-construction pedestrian tracking — the contrast case where this collapse would not appear, since nothing in navigation blocks the camera's view of pedestrians the way a gripper blocks a handoff).
   - *Falsifier*: the exogenous branch's ρ does not measurably drop in the occluded window relative to the unoccluded window → onboard-camera occlusion during contact is not actually load-bearing for the exogenous branch's failure mode, and the camera-agnostic proxy buys nothing H1's suite doesn't already have.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **No benchmark has a second-actor split** — [[2505.14986|AnyBody]], [[2306.03310|LIBERO]], [[2504.13059|RoboTwin]], and [[2512.11891|VLSA]]'s SafeLIBERO are all scoped to a single controlled actor, so the direction can't be measured without first building a scripted or teleoperated second-actor task suite — a benchmark-infrastructure cost, not just a modeling one. → Script a minimal second-actor variant on top of an existing suite (H1's Test), including the occlusion-fraction audit, before running any other hypothesis; treat suite construction as the direction's own first deliverable.
> - **The exogenous branch could just be learning physics, not an agent** — [[2606.02486|AHEAD]]'s "independently moving objects" are a ball and a conveyor, not an adaptive partner; an exogenous branch that only extrapolates simple kinematics would collapse the decomposition into AHEAD's existing wrapper rather than a genuine second-actor model. → H1's suite must include a partner that *reacts* to the robot's own action (mutual coupling, per H5), not a scripted or physics-only mover, or the bet is unfalsifiable against AHEAD.
> - **Reversing B1's negative control could break it where it currently works** — the vast majority of this doc's benchmarks have no second actor, and B1's action-vs-scene negative control is correctly calibrated there. → The fix must be *conditional*: score exogenous-prediction accuracy only when a second actor is genuinely present (H2), never a blanket reversal of B1's existing single-actor validity gate.
> - **Multi-actor training data is scarce** — genuinely independent (not co-controlled) partner data, of the kind [[2409.00215|Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation]] collects, sits outside the OXE-style single-actor corpora this doc's other WAMs train on. → Budget new data collection (human-robot co-manipulation logs, or a scripted-partner sim suite) as part of H1's suite construction, not an afterthought.
> - **A sensing-modality confound could masquerade as an architecture result** — comparing a camera-fed WAM's exogenous branch against Intent-Aware-Co-Manipulation's OptiTrack-fed pipeline without matching sensing modality risks crediting the architecture for what is really a sensor-capability gap (H5), and risks silently building H1's suite on an onboard camera that goes blind exactly during the contact window that matters most (H6). → Run H5's matched-modality control and H6's occlusion audit before drawing any "folded-in beats external module" conclusion; an unmatched, unaudited comparison is not evidence either way.

---

## Cluster B — Evaluation, Robustness & Deployment: From Trained to Deployed

*Everything that stands between a trained policy and reliable deployment: measuring whether imagination and action are causally bound (B1), recovering when it fails with memory (B2), and not forgetting what it knew under continual fine-tuning (B3).*

### B1 — Joint Policy/World-Model Evaluation: Causal Consistency Between Imagination and Action

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Evaluation, Robustness & Deployment |
| **Thesis** | Measure the world model and policy on one joint causal-consistency axis, not two separate ones (WM quality via FVD/PSNR, action quality via SR). A WM and a policy are causally bound only when the action taken in an imagined future matches the executed one — a property visual fidelity cannot see. The field assumes the visual fidelity of WM-generated futures predicts policy success. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2605.12090\|WAM-Survey]] (survey), [[2604.22748\|Agentic-World-Modeling-Survey]] (survey), [[2310.06253\|Objective-Mismatch-MBRL-Survey]] (survey), [[2606.05773\|PiL-World]] (method), [[2606.04463\|OSCAR]] (method), [[2512.10675\|Veo-Robotics]] (method), [[2605.06311\|VISER]] (benchmark), [[2605.29360\|MiraBench]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | The *audited* causal-consistency metric, not raw ρ alone — raw fidelity-mediated ρ is already cleared by [[2607.01060\|RoboWorld]] (0.989), [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]] (0.984), and [[2606.05773\|PiL-World]] (0.94), none of which carry a validity wrapper, and now not even a *calibrated, cross-transfer-validated* correlation alone — [[2608.11174\|VIScore]] ships that apparatus too, on sim-MPC planning success. Target: ASR + COD clears real-SR Pearson **ρ > 0.7** (separate axes: ρ < 0.4) *and* survives an action-vs-scene negative control (near-chance when only the scene, not the action, is perturbed) *and* [[2605.29360\|MiraBench]]'s optimism-bias filter (its paired action-counterfactual probes — Physics Adherence + Action-Following Fidelity + Optimism-Bias Detection, scene-fixed, 16,704 human decisions — reproduce its documented 48.7%→12.8% de-biasing gap) *and* holds under cross-lab variance reduction (H5); the wedge is specifically an *action*-counterfactual object regressed against *real-fleet* SR, not a validated metric in the abstract |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: current protocols score WM quality (FVD/PSNR) and action quality (SR) *separately*, so a joint model can score high on each while imagination and actions are causally disconnected — and [[2310.06253|Objective-Mismatch-MBRL-Survey]] gives the MBRL substrate showing predictive WM loss doesn't correlate with downstream return.
- **Today's answers**: the correlation *is* recoverable once you measure it right — [[2605.06311|VISER]] hits sim-real Pearson **r = 0.92** with controlled fidelity; [[2606.05773|PiL-World]] closes the imagined-vs-real SR gap **63.2% → 12.0%** (Pearson **0.94**); [[2606.04463|OSCAR]] tracks real-fleet rankings at **r = +0.852** (MAE **1.73 pp**); [[2607.29302|Boundless World Model]] now pushes the hardware-correlation number itself to **r = 0.908** by scoring failure-inclusive rollouts rather than success-only. But [[2603.22078|WAM-vs-VLA-Robustness]] shows world-action models win on visual perturbation while running **4.8×** slower — a cost worth paying only if imagination helps action quality, which separate metrics can't certify. And the "raw ρ alone" wedge B1 stakes its differentiator on is narrower than it looked a paragraph ago: [[2608.11174|VIScore]] now ships exactly the kind of validity apparatus (cross-method transfer, cross-task transfer, run-level disjoint calibration) that used to be B1's own unique claim — just aimed at sim-MPC planning success, never real-fleet SR, and at latent empowerment, never a perturbed action $a'_t$. B1's remaining differentiator is those two specifics, not "any calibrated correlation."
- **The opening**: B1's actual diagonal — an *action*-counterfactual metric correlated with real-fleet SR — is open: the closest external attempt, [[2605.27589|What-If-World]], varies only a *scene* detail by prompt (no SOTA video model clears **52%** on paired causal metrics) and never scores against deployed policy SR, while [[2605.28527|VLA-Value-Probing]] (R² ≈ 0.55, **+17.67 pp** value-guided) hints the success signal may already live in the policy. The nearest off-card lane, "Is the Future Compatible?" (arXiv 2605.07514, not yet in KH; abstract verified), reads *action-state consistency* off *executed* actions and "systematically separates successful and failed rollouts" (its success-prediction AUC is full-paper, not independently confirmed), so the action-*counterfactual* wedge is whitespace only if it beats this recover-and-match baseline. The durable deliverable is therefore not a higher ρ (already 0.79–0.99 across [[2606.05773|PiL-World]] / [[2606.18610|SC3-Eval]] / [[2604.22152|dWorldEval]] / [[2512.10675|Veo-Robotics]] / [[2607.01060|RoboWorld]] (0.989), overwritable by whoever has the GPUs) but an **optimism-bias-immune audit standard** — a metric is trustworthy only on probes the WAM provably rendered as the intended failure (MiraBench, arXiv 2605.29360, abstract verified, reports scale does *not* reliably improve action-following and optimism bias is *pervasive*; its 48.7%→12.8% figure is full-paper, not independently confirmed) — so B1's primary deliverable is the **causal-consistency metric *plus* its validity wrapper** (action-vs-scene negative + optimism-bias filter + cross-lab variance reduction), not the ρ number alone.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A world model and a policy are truly linked only when the actions taken in *imagined* futures match the actions taken in *executed* ones. If you never measure that link directly, the "world-model quality" score and the "policy SR" score can both climb while the combined skill goes nowhere — each metric gets gamed on its own.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "fidelity predicts success" — that is now *established premise*, not a contrarian wedge: [[2604.21686|WorldMark]] (fidelity and consistency unrelated), [[2601.17067|Video-Generation-as-World-Models-Survey]] (SOTA only ~24% on causal-understanding), and [[2605.27589|What-If-World]] (looks-real overstates causal ability by **52.2 pp**) all say it in their abstracts. The narrower open claim is that the *action*-counterfactual link — vary $a'_t$, not a scene prompt — predicts real-fleet SR where both single-video fidelity *and* scene-counterfactual rubrics ([[2605.27589|What-If-World]], which never scores policy SR) do not.
- **The bet**: The deliverable is the causal-consistency metric's validity wrapper, not the raw correlation. ASR + COD over *action* counterfactuals must predict real-fleet SR at Pearson **ρ > 0.7** (the easy part: [[2607.01060|RoboWorld]] 0.989, [[2606.18610|SC3-Eval]] 0.984, and [[2606.05773|PiL-World]] 0.94 already clear it with no wrapper at all), and it only counts as *causally* validated once it also survives an action-vs-scene negative control (near-chance when only the scene is perturbed) and [[2605.29360|MiraBench]]'s optimism-bias de-biasing filter (its documented 48.7%→12.8% gap) under cross-lab variance reduction (H5). Clearing ρ > 0.7 alone does not establish the metric.

**Related research papers.**

Forty-seven evaluation systems that measure something — WM fidelity, controllability, counterfactual divergence, closed-loop correlation, real-fleet ranking, sim-as-proxy, offline-error proxy, or statistical validity — none scoring *action*-counterfactual binding against real SR. The axis is *what the eval measures and whether it certifies the imagination↔action link*:

| System | What it measures | Joint causal axis? | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2605.27589\|What-If-World]] | scene-counterfactual divergence under paired prompt interventions | counterfactual, but scene not action | no SOTA model >**52%** paired-causal; single-video overstates by **52.2 pp** | the closest external attempt — but varies a *scene* prompt not the *action* $a'_t$, scores a physics rubric not real-fleet SR, reports no ρ-to-SR |
| [[2603.22212\|Omni-WorldBench]] | interaction-centric WM via counterfactual probes | WM-only | first counterfactual WM eval | scores the WM alone — never the action it should drive |
| [[2603.23497\|WildWorld]] | Action Following + State Alignment | WM-only (large-scale) | 108M Monster Hunter frames | game-domain WM metrics, no policy-success binding |
| [[2510.10125\|CTRL-WORLD]] | controllability for manipulation | WM-only | controllability score | controllability ≠ causal binding to executed actions |
| [[2603.22078\|WAM-vs-VLA-Robustness]] | robustness grid (WAM vs VLA) | separate axes | **4.8×** latency cost | measures both axes separately — the cost-vs-benefit it can't resolve |
| [[2605.21800\|stable-worldmodel]] | reproducible OOD WM harness | WM planning-only | [[2603.19312\|LeWM]] 94% / [[2411.04983\|DINO-WM]] 92% [[2109.00137\|IBC]], decays under occlusion | the perturbation substrate — planning SR, not action-causality |
| [[2605.06311\|VISER]] | sim-real visual-fidelity correlation | fidelity, not causality | **r = 0.92**, 1,000+ PBR objects | existence proof the correlation is recoverable — but visual, not causal |
| [[2606.05773\|PiL-World]] | closed-loop imagined-vs-real SR | closed-loop correlation | Pearson **0.94**, gap 63.2→12.0% | correlates closed-loop, but evaluates rather than certifies causal binding |
| [[2606.04463\|OSCAR]] | virtual vs real-fleet policy ranking | correlation via conditioning | **r = +0.852**, MAE **1.73 pp** | ranks via skeleton conditioning, not an action-causality metric |
| [[2604.22152\|dWorldEval]] | discrete-diffusion WM, *actions as primary tokens* + progress token | closest to action-causal | Pearson **~0.9–0.92** vs real (LIBERO/RoboTwin/real) | treats action as the primary signal (not an auxiliary video condition) and self-detects success — the nearest thing to an action-causal evaluator, but still scored on imagined-frame fidelity, not the counterfactual link |
| [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]] | unified dynamics model as evaluator via three consistencies — central is *forward-inverse* (predict-frames-from-actions ∧ recover-actions-from-frames jointly) | action-grounded, inverse-consistency as a test-time signal | in-dist Pearson **0.984** / MMRV **0.022**; reproduces real failure modes at avg rate **0.56** (beats 3 prior video-WM baselines) | the new ceiling of the action-grounded evaluator family beside [[2604.22152\|dWorldEval]] (actions-as-primary-tokens) and [[2601.04137\|WoW-World-Eval]] (IDM-executability) — makes inverse-dynamics consistency a training objective + early-termination uncertainty signal, the strongest "ground in action, go beyond fidelity" yet — but it checks consistency on the action the policy *actually took* (recover-and-match), not a *counterfactual* $a'_t$ perturbation, and reports one aggregate ρ, not the joint-ASR+COD-vs-separate-axes contrast B1's diagonal needs |
| [[2506.00613\|WorldGym]] | action-conditioned generative WM as policy-evaluator | closed-loop correlation | Pearson **r = 0.78** vs real, SR within **3.3%**, preserves rankings | GPT-4o scores success on *rendered* video, so the correlation is fidelity-mediated — exactly the gap a causal metric must close |
| [[2606.27375\|ABC]] | open *simulation* eval proxy (ABC-Sim) for real BC policies, with offline-metric proxies | sim-as-proxy correlation | strong sim-to-real correlation reported; ABC-VLA inference **47.8→17.5 ms**; 3,553-h open dataset | the reproducible *open-stack* sim proxy beside [[2605.20774\|VLA-REPLICA]] / [[2506.18123\|RoboArena]] — an end-to-end open data+training+eval system whose ABC-Sim approximates real SR cheaply, useful for the cross-lab variance reduction H5 needs, but the proxy is a *simulator* reading task success, not the action-counterfactual causal link |
| [[2602.08971\|WorldArena]] | 16-metric WM benchmark across 3 functional roles | fidelity vs action, separately | EWMScore↔perceptual **r = 0.825** but ↔action-planning **r = 0.360** | the benchmark form of WorldMark's finding — perceptual score barely predicts action utility, yet no single causal axis ties them |
| [[2601.04137\|WoW-World-Eval]] | 5-ability suite + IDM-executability Turing test | executability, not fidelity | high-fidelity video models fail IDM (Kling **9.88%**) while real-trained pass (WoW-wan **40.74%**) | the executability Turing test decouples looks-real from acts-right — but reads it off generated frames, not the imagination↔action consistency itself |
| [[2605.25874\|WBench]] | multi-turn interactive video-WM | WM-only | 289 cases, 22 sub-metrics; nav drops 33 pts Turn-1→4+ | the substrate B1 adds an action-causality axis to |
| [[2604.21686\|WorldMark]] | looks-real vs physically-consistent | decoupling diagnostic | fidelity and consistency *unrelated* | proves FID is the wrong predictor — but offers no replacement metric |
| [[2601.17067\|Video-Generation-as-World-Models-Survey]] | causal-understanding vs perceptual realism (taxonomy) | decoupling diagnostic | SOTA only ~**24%** on causal understanding | establishes the fidelity≠causality premise — a survey-level diagnosis, not a metric |
| [[2602.08025\|MIND-Bench]] | closed-loop memory + action accuracy under action-space shift | WM closed-loop, separate | memory +**4%** consistency but action accuracy *drops* with memory | shows fidelity/memory and action-accuracy diverge, but scores them on separate axes |
| [[2512.00836\|Counterfactual-Model-Error]] | how to estimate WM error under counterfactual scenarios | statistical (counterfactual) | plausible-only evaluation is *systematically biased* | the statistical caution for B1's counterfactual probes — bias-corrected estimation, not the metric itself |
| [[2605.28527\|VLA-Value-Probing]] | value signal from a frozen policy | policy-internal | R² ≈ 0.55, **+17.67 pp** value-guided | the signal may live in the policy, not a separate WM — needs a metric |
| [[2605.26379\|LeJEPA-World-Model]] | when a latent encodes the true world | theory | oracle planning R² > 0.999 iff isotropic-Gaussian | the theory of *when* B1's latent is trustworthy, not a benchmark |
| [[2608.11174\|VIScore]] | planning-relevant latent-WM quality (veracity × influence × sobriety), validated with run-level disjoint checkpoints, cluster-bootstrap resampling, and isotonic calibration | diagnostic, but sim-MPC planning success, not real-fleet SR | pooled Spearman **+0.91** / calibration error **7.0** on held-out checkpoints vs raw empowerment's **+0.49**/**15.6**; transfers to **4** held-out WM methods (**+0.75**) and an unseen MAZE task (**+0.87**) | a validity-and-generalization apparatus in the same category B1 claims as its own differentiator — cross-method and cross-task transfer, not just a single-lab correlation — but its counterfactual object is latent *empowerment*, not a perturbed action $a'_t$, and its yardstick is sim CEM/MPC planning success (PushT/Reacher/Two-Room/MAZE), never real-hardware or real-fleet SR, so it sharpens rather than closes B1's action-counterfactual-to-real-SR diagonal |
| [[2606.05159\|X4Val]] | variance-reduced policy eval | statistical layer | **38.4%** variance cut from non-paired data | the statistical-validity layer a joint metric needs, not the metric |
| [[2605.29710\|PhAIL]] | time-to-success CDF + KS test | statistical rigor | **80%** detection at N=25-30 vs binary at N=30 | adds statistical rigor, but still binary success, not causal binding |
| [[2606.04233\|Manipulation-Benchmark-Audit]] | significance of SR claims | meta-eval | 0.09B [[2304.07193\|DINOv2]]+MLP hits **99.0%** LIBERO-Spatial; only **19.8%** of claims significant | shows current SR certifies the wrong thing — the motivation, not a fix |
| [[2510.03827\|LIBERO-PRO]] | minor-perturbation robustness suite | memorization probe | **>90% → ~0%** under small changes; identical memorized trajectories under object swap | the most damning evidence SR rewards memorization not control — a perturbation probe, not a metric that certifies the link |
| [[2602.22579\|VLA-Metamorphic-Testing]] | metamorphic relations on input transforms | execution-level diagnostic | **3,527** failures symbolic oracles miss (motion/manipulation) | surfaces execution-quality failures a success/fail oracle cannot see — task-agnostic, but still pass/fail per relation, not imagination↔action binding |
| [[2509.18953\|Eva-VLA]] | gradient-free black-box robustness search | adversarial stress | failure **4–23.5% → >80%** (3D pose / light / patch); adversarial training recovers it | the adversarial-search baseline B1's joint metric must include — exposes brittleness, but offers no certification axis |
| [[2510.04354\|SureSim]] | prediction-powered real/sim inference | statistical layer | **−20–25%** real-hardware effort | the statistical layer for the joint metric, not the causality axis |
| [[2505.19017\|WorldEval]] | action-conditioned WM generates execution video, Policy2Vec injects the policy's latent actions, LLM scores success + flags unsafe policies | closed-loop correlation, action-conditioned via Policy2Vec | Pearson **0.79** vs real SR, beats real-to-sim on MMRV + correlation, detects unsafe policies pre-deployment | sits beside [[2506.00613\|WorldGym]] (r 0.78) — conditions the WM on the policy via Policy2Vec but still reads success off *generated frames* with an LLM, so the correlation is fidelity-mediated, not the action-counterfactual link [[2604.22152\|dWorldEval]] approaches; its unique unsafe-policy detection certifies hazards, not the imagination↔action binding |
| [[2512.10675\|Veo-Robotics]] | action-conditioned Veo2-finetuned video-WM as a full-spectrum policy evaluator: nominal SR ranking + OOD-axis decomposition (generative scene-editing) + safety red-teaming | closed-loop correlation, fidelity-mediated | nominal Pearson **r = 0.88** (MMRV **0.03**), OOD-background **r = 0.91**; predicted unsafe behaviors replicated on real hardware, validated vs **1600+** real evals across 8 GROD checkpoints | the SOTA frontier-video evaluator beside [[2506.00613\|WorldGym]] (r 0.78) / [[2505.19017\|WorldEval]] (r 0.79) — strongest correlation plus uniquely adds OOD-axis decomposition and safety-probing the cited roster lacks, but reads success off *generated frames* (fidelity-mediated), so it strengthens "the correlation is recoverable" evidence without scoring the action-counterfactual link B1's diagonal needs |
| [[2607.01060\|RoboWorld]] | fast AR video-WM (Step-Forcing) + task-progress VLM judge as policy evaluator | closed-loop correlation, fidelity-mediated | Pearson **r = 0.989** / Spearman **0.970** vs RoboArena rankings (8 policies), ~**100** H100-h, **15.31** FPS | the new ceiling of the fidelity-mediated evaluator family beside [[2512.10675\|Veo-Robotics]] (0.88) / [[2506.00613\|WorldGym]] (0.78) — pushing ρ to 0.989 *reinforces* the card's pivot rather than closing the gap: a higher raw ρ is exactly the overwritable number B1 says is not the deliverable, since it still reads success off *generated frames* via a VLM judge, not the action-counterfactual link |
| [[2606.29898\|CI-MSE]] | offline action-space error on VLM-identified *critical intervals* (+ temporal-ensembling / action-chunking / DTW) | offline-proxy correlation, no imagination | Spearman **ρ = −0.87** vs rollout SR across 27 policies (raw MSE −0.61), holds under object/skill OOD | the offline-metric lever distinct from every WM-rollout row — correlates a *supervised* critical-interval error with SR (no world model), the cheap offline proxy the cross-lab-variance side (H5) can ride, but it reads recorded expert error, never the imagination↔action counterfactual |
| [[2607.14439\|Active Robot Policy Evaluation]] | frames policy assessment as sequential experimental design: Bayesian active testing (Deep Ensembles / GP surrogates, BALD/PSD acquisition) selects the most informative real-world trial next | statistical layer, not causal | matches random-testing RMSE with **20–40 fewer trials** out of 100; **50–65 fewer** trials to match predictive-log-likelihood | the H5 variance-reduction lever the card lacked at the *trial-selection* stage (vs [[2606.05159\|X4Val]]/[[2510.04354\|SureSim]]'s post-hoc estimators), cuts the real-world rollout budget an action-counterfactual metric needs, but selects trials for uncertainty reduction, not action-counterfactual binding itself |
| [[2607.26789\|CheckVLA]] | execution-time verifier: an action-conditioned world model predicts future observations from *committed* actions, a conformally-calibrated risk trigger flags prediction-observation discrepancy, latency-aware suffix rewriting replans on trigger | closest to action-causal, but on *executed* not counterfactual actions | **36.1%** RoboCasa365 SR (**+8.5 pp** over periodic replanning); verifier recall **77.9%** at 5% FWER (vs 48.6% observation-only); 25.0% of episodes show low policy-uncertainty but high WM-risk, concentrating 48.4% of all failures | a live deployed instance of B1's first principle: checks imagined-vs-actual on the action *taken*, with a statistical FWER guarantee, but like [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]] it recovers-and-matches the executed action rather than perturbing a counterfactual $a'_t$, so it doesn't yet close H1's diagonal |
| [[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]] | *trains* a WM to be action-counterfactual, rather than measuring one after the fact — Multi-Step Counterfactual Consistency rolls out original/inverse/zero action branches and penalizes cycle-inconsistency + zero-action drift, Action-Spatial Counterfactual Consistency enforces mirror-equivariance between scene and action | trains the counterfactual link H1 wants to *measure* | Drift Energy (action-independent motion) cut up to **90%** on BAIR/RoboNet; VP$^2$ visual-planning SR **73.1%** (**+3.0 pp** over iVideoGPT); introduces **ARC**/**DE** metrics for action-response fidelity | the training-side complement no row supplies: its inverse-action and zero-action rollout branches are literally B1's counterfactual object ($a'_t$ = inverse or zero, not just "different") operationalized as a *loss*, and its ARC metric is a ready-made action-response score H1's harness could regress against real SR — but it never validates ARC/DE against real-fleet SR, so it strengthens the counterfactual-training case without closing H1's diagonal itself |
| [[2607.15207\|BadWAM]] | adversarial "World-Action Drift Attack": imperceptible visual perturbations desynchronize the *executed* action from a still-plausible *imagined* future, via black-box query-based optimization | attack, not a metric — inverts B1's premise rather than measuring it | closed-loop SR drops **>50%** for both action-only and imagination-preserving variants; perturbations transfer across WAM variants, non-adaptive defenses insufficient | the first existence proof, from an attack rather than a benchmark, that visual-fidelity-of-imagination is *not* a safety/correctness signal — a WAM can dream right and act wrong; strengthens B1's premise but supplies no metric of its own, and belongs in the Risks adversarial roster too |
| [[2607.02642\|GigaWorld-1/WMBench]] | dedicated WM-as-evaluator benchmark + a purpose-built 5B autoregressive-diffusion WM, scored by a 4-step closed-loop protocol and a "World Model as Evaluator Score" | closed-loop correlation, fidelity-mediated | **2,989** paired real/WM trajectories, **324K** human-annotated segments; GigaWorld-1-Plus WMES **0.6834** vs Cosmos-Predict2.5 **0.6123** / Wan 2.2 **0.5948** | same family as [[2506.00613\|WorldGym]]/[[2505.19017\|WorldEval]]/[[2512.10675\|Veo-Robotics]]/[[2607.01060\|RoboWorld]] — the largest, most purpose-built evaluator-benchmark yet, but adds no action-counterfactual axis: scale, not the diagonal B1 needs |
| [[2607.29302\|Boundless World Model]] | action-aligned trajectory-replay data pipeline + dual-path (cross-attention + AdaLN) action interface feeding a stateful autoregressive video-WM evaluator and data engine | closed-loop correlation, fidelity-mediated | WorldArena fidelity **63.51** EWMScore (**+3.81** over Ctrl-World); failure-inclusive closed-loop correlates **r = 0.908** with hardware (MAE **14.67**) vs **r = 0.158** for success-only; as a data engine lifts physical-robot MOTIF success **50.67%→71.00%** | the sharpest hardware-correlation number in the table (r = 0.908) — but the gain comes from being *failure-inclusive*, not action-counterfactual: it still reads success/failure off generated frames, so a higher correlation from a richer label set is not the same axis as perturbing $a'_t$ |
| [[2608.13552\|PlayWorld-Bench]] | agent-driven, long-horizon closed-loop interaction protocol — a multi-modal Agent Player adapts action sequences online (Keep/Stop/Extend/Correct/End), a Gemini VQA rubric scores geometry/interaction/out-of-sight/insight-evolution dimensions | interactive closed-loop, but rubric-scored not action-counterfactual | best model **2.12/5** overall; rubric tracks **600** human pairwise judgments at Spearman **ρ = 0.933**; trajectory-validity diverges sharply from quality (SANA-WM **80.4%** valid, only **1.48/5** rubric) | a genuinely *interactive*, long-horizon evaluator — closer to B1's causal spirit than any single-step fidelity metric, and its validity-vs-quality divergence is fresh evidence for the fidelity≠causality premise — but the scoring is a VQA rubric over generated frames, not a perturbed action $a'_t$ regressed against real-fleet SR, so it still measures WM quality, not the imagination↔action link |
| [[2606.04811\|Dream.exe]] | converts generated video into *executable* robot trajectories via a 5-step video→3D-trajectory pipeline, then scores physical success in a MuJoCo simulator | executability, not fidelity — a different decoupling mechanism | "negligible correlation" between visual quality and physical task success; up to **21.4%** binary success on simple RoboCasa365 tasks, weak on multi-stage | independent confirmation of the fidelity≠causality premise via a concrete video→trajectory→physics-sim pipeline, distinct from [[2601.04137\|WoW-World-Eval]]'s IDM-Turing-test and [[2604.21686\|WorldMark]]'s rubric — but tests generic video-generation models against physics executability, not a policy's own action-counterfactual |
| [[2607.02403\|ACID]] | inverse-dynamics-model "realizability" check on predicted intermediate states, folded into the planning objective as a *plan-time* cost term inside MPC/CEM | forward-inverse consistency as a **plan-time cost** — a third role beside [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]]'s training objective and [[2607.26789\|CheckVLA]]'s execution-time verifier | **+14.0%** SR (Reacher, PLDM), **-2.3%** ATE (NWM navigation) across 4 WMs / 6 tasks; matches full-budget CEM with **~10×** fewer samples | like SC3-Eval and CheckVLA, the IDM recovers-and-matches the *conditioned* action rather than perturbing a counterfactual $a'_t$ — a third instantiated role for forward-inverse consistency in the corpus, but still doesn't close B1's actual diagonal |
| [[2605.06222\|FFDC-WAM]] | compares WAM-predicted future visual tokens against real observations via a lightweight causal-attention (FFDC) verifier, adaptively resizing the action chunk and triggering replanning on deviation | closest to action-causal, but on *executed* not counterfactual actions | real-hardware Astribot S1 SR **45%→80%**; adaptive chunk sizing cuts compute on predictable phases | same execution-time imagination-vs-real family as [[2607.26789\|CheckVLA]], but a different confidence-based chunk-adaptation trigger (not a conformal FWER guarantee) and validated on **real hardware**, not sim only |
| [[2605.22446\|Pre-VLA]] | *preemptive* pre-execution filter — PPO-critic-derived safety constraints screen candidate action chunks before they run, and truncate unreliable WM imaginations | closest to action-causal, but pre-execution not counterfactual | LIBERO SR **+6.83 pp** (**30.79%→37.62%**); F1 **0.8303**, invalid-action false-pass rate **0.0200** | preemptive (before execution) rather than [[2607.26789\|CheckVLA]]'s post-hoc discrepancy trigger — a different point in the verification loop (screen vs. catch), but still scores the action about to be taken, not a counterfactual $a'_t$ |
| [[2607.09185\|CD-LAM]] | zero-transition / camera-shift counterfactual-sensitivity of a latent action model, corrected via three debiasing objectives (embodiment-centric reconstruction, action-centric contrastive, latent calibration) | counterfactual, but scene/camera shift not action — the weaker sibling of [[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]] | zero-transition response **0.527→0.043**, camera-shift response cut **3.6–8.3×**, **12×** less robot data for adaptation | structurally similar to [[2608.04653\|CoCo-WorldModel]]'s ARC/DE counterfactual-sensitivity measure, but its counterfactual object is camera/background shift, not an inverse/zero *action* branch — a step further from B1's actual $a'_t$ object, and targets latent-action controllability, not real-SR correlation |
| [[2604.01985\|World-Action-Verifier]] | forward-inverse asymmetry (state-plausibility subgoal generator + sparse inverse-dynamics action-reachability check), used to guide *data collection* for WM training | no — a data-acquisition application of forward-inverse consistency, not an evaluation or planning axis | **2×** sample-efficiency gain; **+22%** avg reward across 6 manipulation tasks from WAV-improved WMs | the weakest/lowest-confidence addition here: overlaps heavily with [[2606.18610\|SC3-Eval]]'s forward-inverse mechanism, but applies it to data acquisition rather than evaluation or planning — not clearly a new axis for B1's evaluation thesis |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (a joint causal-consistency metric predicts real SR where separate axes don't), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — An *action*-counterfactual metric beats both FID and *scene*-counterfactual rubrics at predicting real SR.**
   - *Prediction*: a metric that samples $a'_t$ (the action, not a scene prompt), generates $\hat s'_{t+1}$, and requires $\|\hat s_{t+1} - \hat s'_{t+1}\|$ to scale monotonically with $\|a_t - a'_t\|$ (on [[2304.07193|DINOv2]] features) predicts real-fleet SR better than FID/FVD *and* better than [[2605.27589|What-If-World]]'s scene-prompt APEO rubric on the same models.
   - *Test*: compute the action-counterfactual metric on [[2605.29360|MiraBench]]'s paired probes (which perturb a nominal successful action into a physically-interpretable failure-inducing $a'_t$ while holding the scene fixed — the action-counterfactual structure the metric needs), alongside FID and a [[2605.27589|What-If-World]]-style scene-counterfactual score on the same fixed WAM set; regress each against real SR ([[2603.13966|vla-eval]] reference harness). [[2608.04653|CoCo-WorldModel]]'s ARC/DE pair is a ready-made candidate for the action-response term, already computed on inverse/zero-action counterfactuals — worth including as a fourth arm alongside FID and the scene-counterfactual rubric, since it was designed to train the link H1 only measures.
   - *Row*: What-If World (scene-counterfactual, no SR correlation) / WorldMark (fidelity-vs-consistency decoupling).
   - *Falsifier*: FID or the scene-counterfactual rubric predicts real SR as well as the action-counterfactual metric → varying the *action* buys nothing over varying the scene, and B1's diagonal collapses.
2. **H2 — The metric only earns trust once it survives its validity wrapper, not from clearing raw ρ > 0.7 alone.**
   - *Prediction*: ASR (action success in imagination) + COD (counterfactual outcome deviation) *together* predict real SR at Spearman ρ > 0.7 — the easy part, since fidelity-mediated proxies already clear it ([[2607.01060|RoboWorld]] 0.989, [[2606.18610|SC3-Eval]] 0.984, [[2606.05773|PiL-World]] 0.94) while separate L1/L2/L3 sub-scores ([[2604.22748|Agentic-World-Modeling-Survey]]) stay below ρ < 0.4. The metric only counts as *causally* validated if it also (a) scores near-chance under an action-vs-scene negative control (perturbing only the scene, holding the action fixed, should not register as causally bound) and (b) holds ρ > 0.7 after [[2605.29360|MiraBench]]'s optimism-bias de-biasing filter, not just on raw scoring.
   - *Test*: layer the suite on [[2306.03310|LIBERO]] + [[2510.13626|LIBERO-Plus]] + [[2603.28301|LIBERO-Para]] (~40k pairs per WAM); compute raw ρ, then re-compute under the action-vs-scene negative control and the MiraBench optimism-bias filter; cross-check against [[2506.18123|RoboArena]] + [[2605.20774|VLA-REPLICA]] real SR before vs after the wrapper.
   - *Row*: RoboWorld (ρ = 0.989 raw, fidelity-mediated, no wrapper) / SC3-Eval (0.984 raw) / PiL-World (0.94 raw) / MiraBench (optimism-bias filter source).
   - *Falsifier*: the audited ρ (post-wrapper) collapses relative to the raw ρ, or the negative control fails to register near-chance → the wrapper subtracts signal rather than certifying it, and a raw fidelity-mediated number a competitor already owns (RoboWorld's 0.989) is just as trustworthy, killing B1's differentiator.
3. **H3 — The speed-quality Pareto justifies the 4.8× WAM cost only when imagination helps L3.**
   - *Prediction*: re-running [[2603.22078|WAM-vs-VLA-Robustness]]'s config grid with the joint metric shows the **4.8×** latency cost buys a measurable L3-Evolver gain, isolating *when* imagination is worth paying for.
   - *Test*: re-score the ~12-config grid on the joint metric; plot L3 gain vs latency.
   - *Row*: WAM vs VLA Robustness (separate axes, 4.8× cost).
   - *Falsifier*: the joint metric shows no L3 gain from the extra cost → imagination doesn't help action quality and the cost is unjustified.
4. **H4 — The joint metric exposes shortcut-solvable benchmarks current SR rewards.**
   - *Prediction*: on benchmarks where a 0.09B [[2304.07193|DINOv2]]+MLP probe already hits **99.0%** ([[2606.04233|Manipulation-Benchmark-Audit]]), the joint causal metric separates genuinely WM-bound policies from shortcut solvers that the SR number cannot distinguish.
   - *Test*: apply the joint metric to the shortcut-probe vs real WAMs on LIBERO-Spatial; report metric separation.
   - *Row*: Manipulation Benchmark Audit (shortcut-solvable) / MetaFine (binary inflates capability).
   - *Falsifier*: the joint metric scores the shortcut probe as high as a real WAM → it certifies the same wrong thing as SR.
5. **H5 — The joint metric's predictive validity survives variance reduction across labs.**
   - *Prediction*: under [[2606.05159|X4Val]] / [[2510.04354|SureSim]] variance reduction, the joint-metric → real-SR correlation holds at ρ > 0.7 cross-lab on [[2605.20774|VLA-REPLICA]]'s reproducible protocol, so the signal is real, not a single-lab artifact.
   - *Test*: estimate the correlation with variance-reduced surrogates across the [[2605.20774|VLA-REPLICA]] / [[2506.18123|RoboArena]] real-fleet split.
   - *Row*: X4Val (variance reduction) / SureSim (prediction-powered).
   - *Falsifier*: the correlation collapses under variance reduction or across labs → it was a sampling artifact.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Raw ρ alone is not a differentiator**: [[2607.01060|RoboWorld]] (0.989), [[2606.18610|SC3-Eval]] (0.984), and [[2606.05773|PiL-World]] (0.94) already clear ρ > 0.7 with no validity wrapper, so a bare correlation number does not establish B1's contribution. → Always report the audited (post-wrapper) ρ alongside the raw ρ; the wrapper (negative control + MiraBench de-biasing filter + cross-lab variance reduction, H2/H5) is the actual deliverable, not the correlation itself.
> - **Calibrated, cross-transfer-validated correlation is now also taken, not just raw ρ**: [[2608.11174|VIScore]] ships run-level disjoint calibration and validated transfer to held-out WM methods *and* an unseen task — the generalization apparatus B1's wrapper was meant to be the differentiator for. → State B1's remaining wedge precisely: an *action*-counterfactual object (not latent empowerment) regressed against *real-fleet* SR (not sim-MPC planning success) — the two specifics VIScore does not attempt — rather than "a validated metric," which is no longer a novel claim on its own.
> - **Metric noise** — feature-space similarity embeds blind spots. → Pair with explicit physical predicates from A3; cross-validate against [[2605.06311|VISER]]'s measured sim-real correlation.
> - **Sample size** — counterfactual probes may need 100+ rollouts per task instance. → Use [[2603.13966|vla-eval]]'s **47×** speedup to amortize the rollout cost; add [[2606.05159|X4Val]]'s variance reduction (H5).
> - **Selection bias** — the benchmark may flatter current WAMs. → Include adversarial ([[2604.05498|JailWAM]], [[2606.03556|VLA-Patch-Attack]] at **90.7%** attack SR, [[2607.15207|BadWAM]]'s imagination-preserving drift attack dropping closed-loop SR **>50%** while the imagined future stays plausible, [[2605.02900|Safety-in-Embodied-AI-Survey]] attack layers) + physics-violating ([[2603.23376|ABot-PhysWorld]] rejects) baselines.
> - **Optimism in the scoring model** — an LLM/VLM judge systematically over-rewards suboptimal behavior ([[2606.01036|Bad-Behavior-Rewards]], Preference Ordering Accuracy 0.72–0.77 simple → 0.52–0.62 complex), exactly the failure mode H2's optimism-bias filter targets. → Calibrate with dense per-timestep failure labels (+10% on complex tasks per [[2606.01036|Bad-Behavior-Rewards]]) as part of the validity wrapper (H2), not an afterthought.
> - **The metric itself must clear the deployment's real-time floor** — B1's joint causal-consistency metric is only useful if it is cheap enough to run inside the control loop, not just accurate offline. [[2505.04769|VLA-Concepts-Survey]] names the **3–5 Hz** AR ceiling a contact-rich policy must clear; single-lever fixes already reach it ([[2507.05116|VOTE]] **38.6×** speedup at **98.0%** LIBERO, [[2512.20188|Fast-Slow-WB-VLA]] **32.3 Hz** at **90%** SR on a real whole-body robot), so floor-reachability is settled — whether *composing* multiple efficiency levers (architecture × decoding × precision × data, per [[2510.24795|Efficient-VLA-Survey]] and [[2603.28489|Video-Gen-as-WM-Survey]]) beats the best single lever is a real open question, but it is a secondary, likely non-transferable optimization on top of the floor, not part of B1's causal-consistency claim itself. → Report the joint metric's own per-step compute cost against the 3–5 Hz floor before trusting it in a deployed loop, and don't let a co-designed efficiency stack substitute for measuring the causal-consistency link; [[2603.19131|Embodied-Efficiency]]'s finding that SR-preserving compression still degrades embodied smoothness (**+19.5%** jerk-L2 at **−2.7%** max SR loss) is the concrete reminder that a cheap metric can still certify a policy that moves badly.

### B2 — Non-Markovian Long-Horizon Memory + Failure Recovery Loops for Real-World Deployment

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Evaluation, Robustness & Deployment |
| **Thesis** | Make a trained VLA's memory survive what a fielded robot actually experiences — a real process kill and relaunch, not just a new episode inside one unbroken run. Every published "cross-episode" memory system (HELM, ReMem-VLA, BATON, PhysMem) has only ever been tested within one continuous deployment, and the field's own benchmarks (RoboMemArena, RMBench, RoboMME) never test the restart boundary. Cross-episode recognition and *cause-attributed* recovery — recognize "I have failed this same way before" and route to the diagnosed fix, not uniform rollback — are necessary machinery, but insufficient on their own: they are what this direction bets can be engineered to survive a restart, not the bet itself. The target variable throughout is *non-Markovian dependence* (does the optimal action need information absent from the current observation?), not raw episode length — "long-horizon" alone is the wrong proxy. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2605.10921\|RoboMemArena]] (benchmark, H1+H2's test substrate), [[2603.01229\|RMBench]] (benchmark, H1+H2's test substrate, formalizes the horizon ≠ memory-complexity distinction H2's confound-control test runs on), [[2505.12224\|RoboFAC]] (benchmark, H3's test substrate), [[2604.18791\|HELM]] (method, H1/H2/H3's shared baseline), [[2603.12942\|ReMem-VLA]] (method, H1's sharpest disconfirming precedent — hard-resets at episode boundaries by design), [[2602.20323\|PhysMem]] (method, H1's nearest real candidate — already combines detect+diagnose+recover+cross-episode memory, restart untested), [[2608.16889\|BATON]] (method, H2's SOTA-to-beat on RoboMemArena), [[2606.03385\|GTP-FA]] (method, H3's real-Franka cause-attribution reference), [[2604.07034\|KITE]] (method, H3's RoboFAC-native diagnosis-quality floor) — every anchor is the literal empirical substrate a specific Hypothesis's Test or Row runs against, not a general-field citation |
| **Key targets** | **Primary (the restart gate)**: on an enforced kill-reload-relaunch replay of [[2605.10921\|RoboMemArena]]'s and [[2603.01229\|RMBench]]'s repeated-failure trajectories, a restart-engineered memory clears [[2604.18791\|HELM]]'s episode-local floor by ≥10 pp strategy-switch rate — every target below only counts once it clears this gate, not just within one continuous run. **Conditional on clearing that gate**: ≥+5 pp SR on RoboMemArena's cross-episode repeated-failure subtasks over [[2608.16889\|BATON]]'s own reported SOTA (**57.7%** TSR / **78.8%** CSR, already beating the benchmark's ground-truth-memory oracle by CSR — the real bar on this benchmark, not HELM's LIBERO-Long/CALVIN numbers, which never touched RoboMemArena); recovery SR lift from cause-attribution ([[2606.03385\|GTP-FA]] **11.2→76.8%**; [[2505.12224\|RoboFAC]]'s 3-level failure taxonomy + closed-loop correction SR **47.5→61.25%** real-world; [[2604.07034\|KITE]]'s **+36pp** failure-detection / **+33pp** failure-localization gain on RoboFAC itself, the diagnosis-quality floor cause-attribution must clear); oscillation incidents **−50%** via state-machine integration |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the integrated loop now exists — but it is *episode-local* and *cause-blind*. [[2604.18791|HELM]] wires an episodic memory + a memory-conditioned state verifier + a recovery harness into one frozen-VLA loop and hits **+23.1 pp** on LIBERO-Long, yet its memory keeps only the most-recent checkpoint per subgoal (no cross-episode carryover) and its harness rolls back *uniformly* without attributing the cause. So the two faces that turn a recovery loop into *learning* — recognizing "I've failed this way before" and choosing the fix for the *cause* — are exactly what the integrated systems skip.
- **Today's answers**: [[2604.18791|HELM]] is the integrated loop (memory + memory-conditioned detection + rollback, **81.5%** LIBERO-Long, ships LIBERO-Recovery at **54.2%** RSR) but episode-local and uniform-rollback; [[2602.13086|UniManip]] is the only system claiming all four faces — detect, diagnose, recover, memory (**93.75%** zero-shot) — but via *agentic LLM orchestration*, not a trained policy (and [[2606.19980|ENPIRE]] now closes the *real-world fleet-scaled* agentic self-improvement loop at up to **99%** real SR, [[2606.19419|RATS]] adds an intrinsic-motivation play curriculum with skill+failure memory, and [[2607.08448|Harness-VLA]] beats RATS itself **82.4% → 43.8%** on perturbed LIBERO-Pro via a Global cross-task memory over a frozen VLA primitive — all three sharpening H5's agentic-vs-trained contrast, none a trained policy); [[2606.03385|GTP-FA]] adds grasp-vs-planning *diagnosis* (real Franka **11.2% → 76.8%**) but as a standalone step; [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]] shows **68.9%** of subtasks need historical info; SOMA (arXiv 2603.24060, not yet in KH) adds cause-*typed* attribution *and* an offline memory-consolidation module that distills across executions, but via an **Attribution-Driven LLM Orchestrator over a *frozen* policy** — not a *trained* VLA — so it pre-empts the *orchestrated* form of cross-episode failure-memory and leaves the **trained-policy** cross-episode loop as the surviving wedge (whether its memory is recurrence-keyed needs a full-paper read, per the review's de-risk). The raw-memory-architecture ceiling has also moved since the card's own RMBench baseline was written: [[2608.05042|BridgeVLA++]] now hits **96.0%** RMBench overall (vs the cited Mem-0 baseline's **52.8%**/**28.5%**) — but that gain is *within-rollout* spatio-temporal context, not cross-episode recurrence, so it raises the memory-quality floor H2's mechanism must clear without touching H1's restart claim or H3's cause-attribution claim at all.
- **The opening**: [[2603.09030|PlayWorld]]'s self-play proposer/executor captures failure modes absent from human data (Pearson **0.8766** predicted-vs-real SR, **+65%** real SR), supplying the repeated-failure data a *cross-episode* memory would index — the missing piece is a trained-VLA loop that remembers across episodes and routes to cause-attributed recovery. Lead with the endorsed, lowest-risk core: cause-attributed recovery already beats uniform rollback ([[2606.03385|GTP-FA]] **11.2 → 76.8%** real-Franka, H3), which stands even if the cross-episode-memory face (H2) is pre-empted — so H3 is the safe spine and cross-episode memory the higher-variance extension.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle (persistence ≠ deployment survival)**: an engineered memory store *existing* — a retrieval index, a bank of embeddings, a growing failure buffer — is not the same claim as that memory *surviving what a fielded robot actually experiences*: process crashes, OTA updates, redeploys, a robot powered down overnight and back on in the morning. Every benchmark this direction's evidence rests on — [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]], [[2603.01229|RMBench]], [[2603.04639|RoboMME]] — scores memory within one continuous, never-killed process. A memory architecture is unproven at the boundary that actually matters for deployment until it is tested there, and nobody has tested it there. This is not a manipulation-specific blind spot: [[2607.21571|Sequential-EQA]] runs the *milder* version of the same test in embodied question answering — carrying memory across consecutive questions within one still-running session, no restart at all — and finds most memory architectures (lightweight geometric, dense episodic-semantic, implicit-latent) gain under **3%** from it; only structured 3D spatial memory clears a real margin (**+33.3%** accuracy, real-quadruped validated). If even within-session carryover is this fragile across architectures, the untested restart boundary is a deeper hole than a single-domain gap.
- **First principle (horizon ≠ memory complexity)**: episode length and history-dependence are orthogonal axes, not the same variable — RMBench's own Task Memory Complexity metric formalizes this: a task is M(0) (memory-free), M(1), or M(*n*) by the *minimum past information its optimal action needs*, independent of step count. A long chain of locally-Markovian subtasks (each solvable from the current observation alone, e.g. many BEHAVIOR-1K-style task chains) needs no cross-step memory at all; a single-step task like RMBench's own "Put Back Block" is short *and* non-Markovian. [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]]'s **68.9%**-of-subtasks-need-history figure is itself a non-Markovian-dependence measurement in disguise — it is not evidence that "long tasks" need memory, it is evidence that a *subset* of subtasks, of any length, do. So B2's mechanism should be scored against non-Markovian dependence directly, not against raw horizon as a proxy for it.
- **First principle**: An *episode-local* memory cannot recognize recurrence — it has thrown the prior attempt away by the next episode, so "I have failed this same way before" is unrepresentable; and a recovery that does not attribute the *cause* (grasp vs planning vs perception) picks a fix at random and oscillates. Cross-episode memory and cause-attributed recovery are therefore the two faces an integrated loop *must* add to count as learning, not the four-way composition (which [[2604.18791|HELM]] already shows composes) — but as machinery *for* restart survival, not a substitute for testing it.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not that "nobody integrates the four faces" — HELM integrates three (memory + memory-conditioned detection + recovery) and [[2602.13086|UniManip]] claims all four — and not merely that a trained VLA's episode-boundary memory beats episode-local uniform rollback, which is the modest claim this direction already had solid evidence for. The sharper, contested claim: that *any* published "cross-episode" memory system — including HELM, [[2603.12942|ReMem-VLA]], [[2608.16889|BATON]], and [[2602.20323|PhysMem]] (the closest real candidate, already combining detect+diagnose+recover+cross-episode memory) — actually survives a genuine process restart, as opposed to merely spanning episode boundaries inside one still-running deployment. The field treats "the memory object outlives an episode boundary" and "the memory object outlives the process" as the same claim. They are not, and nobody has tested the difference.
- **The bet**: *Primary* — a failure-signature memory engineered for restart survival (disk-backed, keyed to failure cause, reloaded on relaunch) clears HELM's episode-local floor by ≥10 pp strategy-switch rate on RoboMemArena's and RMBench's repeated-failure trajectories even after an enforced kill-reload-relaunch between the failure and the retry, while every currently-published "cross-episode" system — HELM itself, ReMem-VLA's hard-reset design, PhysMem as currently built — degrades to at-or-below that floor once the restart is imposed, because none has a mechanism built to survive it. *Necessary-but-insufficient precondition* — that same memory, in the easier within-deployment setting nobody has yet falsified, also lifts SR on RoboMemArena's repeated-failure subtasks by ≥+5 pp over [[2608.16889|BATON]]'s own **57.7%** TSR (the real bar on this benchmark — [[2604.18791|HELM]]'s +23.1 pp LIBERO-Long never touched RoboMemArena), raises recovery SR via cause-attributed diagnosis over uniform rollback (the [[2606.03385|GTP-FA]] **11.2→76.8%** attribution gain), and cuts oscillation incidents ≥50% via state-machine integration — a result that only counts once it also clears the restart gate above.

**Related research papers.**

Sixty-one systems spanning the loop — a few now integrate three or four faces, but episode-locally and with cause-blind rollback. The axis is *how much of the detect-diagnose-recover-with-memory loop it integrates, and whether the memory spans episodes and the recovery attributes cause*:

| System | Loop face | Integrated? | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2604.18791\|HELM]] | memory + memory-conditioned detection + recovery | yes — 3 faces, one frozen-VLA loop | **+23.1 pp** LIBERO-Long (**81.5%**), **54.2%** LIBERO-Recovery RSR | the integrated loop B2 reframes from — but memory is *episode-local* (most-recent checkpoint only) and recovery is *uniform rollback*, no cause-attribution, no cross-episode recurrence, no oscillation metric |
| [[2602.13086\|UniManip]] | detect + diagnose + recover + memory (agentic) | yes — all 4 faces | **93.75%** zero-shot, **82.5%** cluttered | claims all four — but via *zero-shot LLM orchestration* over a scene graph, not a *trained* policy; no LIBERO-Long SR, no oscillation |
| [[2606.19980\|ENPIRE]] | agentic real-world self-improvement (Environment / Policy-Improvement / Rollout / Evolution closed loop, fleet-parallel) | yes — full loop (agentic, real-world) | up to **99%** real (pin-insertion, zip-tie); 1→8 robots cuts convergence 1.5 h→~40 min; transfers autoresearch experience to new tasks | the *real-world fleet-scaled* agentic four-module loop beside [[2602.13086\|UniManip]]'s scene-graph orchestrator — coding agents build the safety/reset/verify interface and refine policies on physical robots, the strongest agentic-self-improvement foil to the trained-VLA H5 bet; but the loop lives in an LLM coding agent, not amortized into a trained policy, and recovery is per-task not cross-episode-recurrence-keyed |
