| [[2603.13528\|Counterfactual-Failure-Synthesis]] | counterfactual-rollout failure-data engine | data engine | **120K+** verified pairs, **46%** real closed-loop recovery | the second data engine — synthesizes failures by perturbing actions into a WM rather than self-play, with paired recovery labels, but the loop still has to consume them |
| [[2606.21406\|Human-Video-Dynamics]] | recover→correct via human-video dynamics+value models (DGAC turns failed rollouts into corrective supervision) | recover + data engine (self-improve) | real **85.3%** (**+9.3 pp** over RISE); DGAC ablation **−22.6 pp** (62.7%); transfers to a *new embodiment* Franka **36.7→70.0%** | a third recover-cell data engine distinct from the self-play ([[2603.09030\|PlayWorld]]) and counterfactual-WM ([[2603.13528\|Counterfactual-Failure-Synthesis]]) engines — retrieves a progress-aligned successful reference and *synthesizes* the corrective action via human-video-pretrained dynamics, no teleop; cross-embodiment transfer of the corrector itself, but recovery is episode-local with no cross-episode recurrence memory or cause-attribution |
| [[2607.19745\|EgoRecovery]] | recover via a *fourth* data engine: co-trains on robot + egocentric human recovery demonstrations, distilling a compact "corrective intent" (low-frequency DCT of future EE motion) that a gated FiLM head applies only when a Recovery Gate fires | recover + data engine (human-recovery-demo) | **85.0%** avg recovery SR from off-nominal starts (vs **71.2%** direct human-recovery-mix, **0%** nominal-only robot baseline); human recovery data collected **10.5×** faster (516.5 episodes/operator-hour) | the fourth data-engine variant beside self-play ([[2603.09030\|PlayWorld]]), counterfactual-WM ([[2603.13528\|Counterfactual-Failure-Synthesis]]), and dynamics-retrieval ([[2606.21406\|Human-Video-Dynamics]]): the corrective signal comes from *humans demonstrating recovery*, not synthesized, and the gate prevents nominal-execution interference; still episode-local, no cross-episode recurrence memory or cause-attribution |
| [[2602.02895\|DEFT-FailActive]] | failure-conditioned trajectory-generation data engine (samples per-joint actuation-failure "embodiments," constructs RRT/IK trajectories under them, trains a diffusion policy via start-goal inpainting + limit clamping) | data engine (mechanical-failure, not task-failure) | **74.51%** constraint satisfaction vs **36.85%** classical planners (**+37.7 pp**); OOD generalization near-parity (**73.61%** vs **78.33%** ID); real Franka drawer+erasing tasks **1.00/1.00** vs optimization baseline **0.00/0.35** | a fifth data engine, but a different failure *type* than every other row — actuator/joint damage (locked/degraded joints), not grasp or planning error; the generate-failure-conditions-then-train-recovery pattern transfers, the mechanism doesn't: no vision, no VLM, no cause-attribution of a *manipulation* failure |
| [[2506.06677\|RoboCerebra]] | long-horizon memory benchmark | — (measures) | **2,972** sim-steps/traj (~6× prior), 100 task variants, scores memory + reflection + plan-efficiency | the shared long-horizon, memory-intensive suite H5's trained-loop-vs-orchestrator comparison lacks — but a benchmark, not a loop component |
| [[2404.00756\|Recover]] | detect + diagnose + recover (online cyclical, neuro-symbolic) | yes — 3 faces, one online loop | OntoThor rules **100%** detection (vs **~50%** LLM verification), **~70%** recovery rate, **93%** of safety issues recovered | the symbolic full-loop contrast to [[2604.18791\|HELM]]'s uniform rollback — an ontology gives explicit cause-understanding HELM lacks, but it is hand-built symbolic (not a trained VLA) and episode-local, no cross-episode recurrence memory |
| [[2602.12405\|Self-Refining-VLM-Failure]] | detect + diagnose (one VLM, entropy-gated) | detect+diagnose | **+25.6%** detection over fine-tuning under shift; iterative refinement **+42.4%** reasoning (Round 0→1) | bridges detect→diagnose in one self-refining VLM under heterogeneous supervision — supplies the diagnosis signal H3 routes recovery on, distinct from [[2410.00371\|AHA]] (diagnosis only), but no recovery generator or memory |
| [[2503.15386\|CCDP]] | recovery via failure-steered diffusion (success-only data) | recover-only | **99%** Door Opening / **96%** Button Pressing vs **70%** / **10%** Diffusion Policy, no failure labels | a composable-diffusion recovery distinct from [[2604.18791\|HELM]] rollback / [[2602.20057\|AdaWorldPolicy]] LoRA — steers actions away from failure regions, but no diagnosis of which cause and no cross-episode memory |
| [[2605.09410\|RePO-VLA]] | trained-VLA (π0.5) recovery — RAI+VCR reuse success/failed/recovered trajectories | recover-only (trained policy) | recovery **15→43%** sim (randomized), **+55pp** adversarial real-bimanual | a *trained-VLA* recover-cell whose Trajectory-Slicing-with-History-RESET (TSHR) is a direct **foil to H2**: it deliberately *resets* failure history (the opposite of cross-episode memory) to disentangle correction from failure-context, and still has no recurrence-recognition or cause-attribution — sharpening the card's argument that cross-episode failure-signature memory is the unexplored face |
| [[2603.09292\|See-Plan-Rewind]] | progress detection + data-free Rewind recovery | detect+recover | **91.8%** LIBERO (**+5.0%** over MolmoAct, **+8.2%** long-horizon); OOD degradation only **18.8%** on LIBERO-Plus | spatially-grounded 2D-subgoal progress + a recovery trained on synthesized reverse trajectories (no failure data, no aux model) — distinct from [[2503.15386\|CCDP]] and [[2604.18791\|HELM]]'s checkpoint rollback, but recovery is episode-local with no repeated-failure memory |
| [[2604.21232\|ReCAPA]] | predictive hierarchical correction (action/subgoal/trajectory) | recover (predictive) | **0.75** SR AI2-THOR, lowest Error-Propagation-Rate / highest Propagation-Attenuation | anticipatory correction across three levels to arrest cascading failures — distinct from [[2510.09459\|FIPER]] (predictive detection that stops at the alarm); predicts AND corrects, but within-episode, no cross-episode recurrence memory |
| [[2503.15202\|VLM-BT-Failure-Handling]] | detect + identify + correct via Behavior-Tree state-machine | detect+diagnose+recover (state-machine) | **100%** task SR on the REFLECT benchmark + real YuMi (vs **31.25%** pre-execution-only) | the explicit Behavior-Tree state-machine that H4's oscillation-reduction bet measures — proactive verify + reactive monitor + correct in one structured policy, but no memory of repeated failure and hand-built BTs, not a trained loop |
| [[2512.02787\|ViFailback]] | diagnose + recover + learn (visual-symbol annotation) | yes — 3 faces + real failure-data engine | ViFailback-8B **93.70%** closed / **72.64%** open bench; +**π0.5** real SR **+20.6% → +23.8%** | covers diagnose→recover→learn plus a scalable real-world failure-annotation contribution (5,202 trajectories, 58,128 VQA) distinct from the self-play ([[2603.09030\|PlayWorld]]) / counterfactual ([[2603.13528\|Counterfactual-Failure-Synthesis]]) data engines — but an external VLM supervisor, episode-local, no cross-episode recurrence memory |
| [[2510.12710\|Reflective-Self-Adaptation]] | recover → continual-update (failures AND successes) | continual-update (H6) | **83.6%** avg LIBERO (Spatial/Object/Goal/Long); removing success-pathway collapses to **0%** (reward hacking) | self-improves at test time by mining BOTH observed failures and high-quality successes — distinct from [[2506.21669\|SEEA-R1]] (recovery→training only), sharpening H6's 'verified recovery becomes a training example' face, but no cross-episode memory indexing *repeated* failure |
| [[2602.21198\|Reflective-Test-Time-Planning]] | reflection → self-supervised parameter update (double-loop) | recover → continual-update (H6/H5) | **33.65%** Long-Horizon Household vs 3DLLM-Mem **11.13%**; **19.5%** zero-shot HM3D transfer | converts verbal reflections into LoRA gradient updates (reflection-on-action) — the reflection-to-gradient recovery-as-learning loop, an *embodied-LLM* datapoint sharpening H5's trained-loop-vs-orchestrator contrast, but an LLM-orchestrated three-model loop, not a trained policy with cross-episode memory |
| [[2606.17463\|WeaveLA]] | action-side latent memory across repetitions | memory-only (H2-adjacent) | RoboMME **19.0% → 24.7%** (6-task); SWINGXTIMES (N=3) **0% → 47.8%**, **4% → 30%** | event-driven action-side memory that transfers sub-task completion info ACROSS REPETITIONS — the closest existing analog to cross-episode/cross-repetition *action* memory, distinct from the perception-memory row ([[2605.10993\|ECHO-VLA]]) B2's H2 needs, but within a single rollout, not across episodes, and no recovery loop |
| [[2606.09827\|MemoryVLA++]] | past memory + future imagination | memory-only | **+26 pp** memory-dependent / **+28 pp** imagination-dependent real-robot; **73.1%** zero-shot LIBERO-Plus | supersedes the cited [[2508.19236\|MemoryVLA]] on the memory axis by adding world-model imagination — strengthens the memory substrate the loop indexes failures into, but memory alone, no detection or recovery, and no cross-episode failure recurrence |
| [[2604.11306\|Hierarchical-Episodic-Memory]] | lifelong episodic memory with learned forgetting | memory substrate (answers the Risk) | **−45%** memory size, **−35%** query compute; QA **+70%** after feedback; real Armar-7 | gives lifelong humanoids a learned relevance-based FORGETTING mechanism — directly mitigates the card's 'cross-episode memory growth + retrieval latency' Risk, but no detect/diagnose/recover loop wired on top |
| [[2605.29341\|WorldMemArena]] | memory benchmark — four-stage lifecycle | — (measures *where* memory fails) | 461 multi-session multimodal tasks; retrieval identified as the critical bottleneck | scores memory through write/maintain/retrieve/use inside an Action–World Interaction Loop to pinpoint WHERE memory fails — a failure-point-diagnostic benchmark distinct from [[2605.10921\|RoboMemArena]] (need-for-history), [[2603.04639\|RoboMME]] (memory-type), and [[2506.06677\|RoboCerebra]], but scores memory alone, no recovery axis |
| [[2502.10550\|MIKASA]] | memory benchmark — RL-agent angle | — (measures) | PPO-MLP w/ full state **100%** on all 32 tasks; online/offline RL + π0.5 collapse to near-zero / **10%** under partial observability | a tabletop memory suite (Object/Spatial/Sequential/Capacity) showing online/offline/VLA agents all fail long-horizon spatio-temporal memory, real-robot validated — the RL-agent memory axis the VLA/MLLM-recall benchmarks ([[2605.10921\|RoboMemArena]]/[[2603.04639\|RoboMME]]) don't cover, but no recovery loop |
| [[2607.06256\|Semantic Handoff Diagnosis]] | diagnose (compositional handoff, agentic) | diagnose-only (trace-backed) | BEHAVIOR-1K mean progress **19.5%** despite high isolated-skill SR; failures split into target-grounding (37) / control-execution (58) / next-skill-readiness (35) | names a *new cause type* the grasp/plan/perception taxonomies ([[2606.03385\|GTP-FA]] / [[2505.12224\|RoboFAC]]) miss — the *semantic handoff* failure where a skill meets its local postcondition but leaves the state unfit for the next skill; a handoff-aware-postcondition diagnostic, but agent-orchestrated and episode-local, no recovery generator or cross-episode memory |
| [[2605.01195\|TAIL-Safe]] | detect + recover via task-agnostic runtime safety criteria (visibility/recognizability/graspability fused by a Lipschitz-continuous WeightNet) + a reach-avoid Safety Q-value defining a control-invariant safe set, with a Nagumo-tangentiality gradient controller steering back in | detect+recover (task-agnostic, no VLM) | **100%** SR on Franka Candy-Picking/Pick-and-Place vs **20–25%** vanilla-IL under runtime perturbation; **0.63–0.85** recovery actions/episode at **2.8 ms** latency | a mechanistically distinct detect+recover cell: no VLM judge, no learned failure classifier, just Lipschitz-continuous safety criteria fused into an invariant-set Q-function, complementary to [[2503.15202\|VLM-BT-Failure-Handling]]'s state-machine, but no memory of repeated failure and no cause-attribution |
| [[2607.10350\|ABot-AgentOS]] | detect + diagnose + recover + memory via a deliberative Agent-OS layer (reasoning, tool-use, verification) over low-level controllers, with a Universal Multi-modal Graph Memory and a lifelong self-evolution mechanism | yes, all 4 faces (agentic) | **+11.99 pp** TSR / **+10.84 pp** GCR over single-controller ReAct on EmbodiedWorldBench; graph memory beats baselines across **five** separate memory benchmarks (LoCoMo/OpenEQA/Mem-Gallery/NExT-QA/EgoLife), improving further under self-evolution (e.g. LoCoMo 87.5→88.7) | a fourth agentic all-four-faces system beside [[2602.13086\|UniManip]]/[[2606.19980\|ENPIRE]]/[[2606.19419\|RATS]], its distinguishing evidence is *cross-domain* memory generalization (five heterogeneous benchmarks, not one robotics-specific suite) plus a self-evolution loop that keeps improving them; strengthens H5's agentic comparator, but still an orchestration layer over controllers, not a trained policy, and memory generalization is not cross-episode failure-recurrence recognition |
| [[2607.01111\|FAR]] | trained-policy loop combining explicit *failure attribution* (IQL-style Q/V identifies the specific failure-inducing action chunk) with test-time recovery (Failure-Contrastive Preference Adaptation + action perturbations) and a continual-improvement loop that folds verified recoveries back into advantage-weighted actor/critic updates | yes — diagnose(attribution)+recover+continual-update, real-hardware validated | **+17.6%** avg SR over standard Diffusion Policies in sim; real xArm validated; higher data efficiency (fewer environment resets to match SR) | lands on B2's own cause-attributed-recovery-then-learning axis — fuses H3's diagnosis face (which action chunk caused the failure) with H6's recovery-becomes-training-example face in one trained loop, real-hardware confirmed; but the failure-contrastive adaptation is test-time/session-local, not indexed by a cross-episode failure-signature key, so it still doesn't add cross-episode recurrence memory |

**Hypotheses & tests.** H1 is the primary claim — restart survival, the direction's revolutionary bet. H2–H6 are the within-deployment machinery H1 requires; each only counts once it also clears H1's restart gate. Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis, with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — Failure-signature memory that survives a genuine process restart beats memory that only spans one continuous deployment.** [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]]'s own protocol never kills the process between the failure and the retry, and every "cross-episode" system checked (including [[2603.12942|ReMem-VLA]], which hard-resets its recurrent state at episode boundaries, "guaranteeing strict isolation and preventing any cross-episode state leakage") has this same convention baked in. H1 asks whether cross-episode recognition (H2) survives the boundary nobody has tested: the policy process actually terminating and relaunching, with memory reloaded from disk rather than held in RAM across the whole run.
   - *Prediction*: on RoboMemArena's and [[2603.01229|RMBench]]'s repeated-failure trajectories, replayed with an *enforced process restart* between the failure episode and the retry (kill the policy process, reload its failure-memory store from disk, relaunch), a system whose memory is engineered to survive the restart (e.g. a disk-backed store keyed to failure signatures, in the spirit of [[2602.20323|PhysMem]]'s resonance-checked principle memory but wired for restart survival) clears [[2604.18791|HELM]]'s episode-local floor by ≥10 pp strategy-switch rate on the second encounter; every architecture whose "cross-episode" claim was never restart-tested (HELM itself, ReMem-VLA's hard-reset design, PhysMem as currently built) drops to at-or-below HELM's baseline once the restart is imposed, because none has a mechanism to survive it.
   - *Test*: two conditions on the same repeated-failure trajectory set — (a) no-restart control, matching every cited system's existing protocol; (b) enforced restart between failure and retry (process kill → disk reload → relaunch). Compare second-encounter SR and strategy-switch rate between conditions, for both a restart-engineered memory and an as-published baseline.
   - *Row*: HELM (episode-local floor, no restart in any cited protocol) / ReMem-VLA (hard-reset by design, the sharpest disconfirming precedent) / PhysMem (nearest real mechanism — detect+diagnose+recover+cross-episode memory already present, restart never tested).
   - *Falsifier*: the restart condition matches the no-restart control for both the restart-engineered memory and the baseline → the process boundary is not actually load-bearing, and the field's within-deployment testing convention already captures what matters — H2's within-deployment framing is sufficient on its own and H1 adds nothing.
2. **H2 — Cross-episode memory, keyed to a specific failure's *cause*, recognizes recurrence that neither an episode-local loop nor a subtask-transition memory catches.** [[2608.16889|BATON]] is now the real bar on this benchmark, not HELM — HELM's own numbers are LIBERO-Long/CALVIN/LIBERO-Recovery, never RoboMemArena. BATON already beats the benchmark's own ground-truth-memory oracle on CSR (**78.8%** vs **64.8%**) via hierarchical subtask exploration and transition-aware language memory — but its memory is keyed to *subtask handoff readiness*, not to a *specific prior failure's cause*; it never claims "I have failed this exact way before, here is the diagnosed fix." This hypothesis only counts once it also clears H1's restart gate.
   - *Prediction*: a memory that carries failure *signatures* (not just subtask-transition contracts) across episodes, keyed to cause, raises SR on RoboMemArena's repeated-failure subtasks ≥+5 pp over BATON's own **57.7%** TSR, specifically on the subset of failures that recur with the *same* underlying cause across separate attempts — the axis BATON's transition-memory was never built to test.
   - *Test*: replay repeated-failure episodes through a cause-keyed cross-episode memory vs BATON's transition-aware memory vs HELM's episode-local memory; report SR and strategy-switch rate on the second+ encounter, stratified by whether the recurring failure shares a cause or just a subtask boundary. To isolate history-dependence from raw episode length (the horizon ≠ memory-complexity first principle above), additionally run the same three-way comparison on RMBench's M(1) vs M(0)/M(*n*) task splits at matched step-count, so any SR gain is attributed to non-Markovian dependence and not to longer episodes giving cross-episode memory more surface area to help.
   - *Row*: BATON (subtask-transition memory, the real SOTA to beat) / HELM (episode-local memory + verifier, the mechanism baseline).
   - *Falsifier*: cause-keyed memory adds no SR over BATON's transition-memory on same-cause recurrences, **or** the gain on RMBench disappears once M(1)/M(*n*) tasks are matched for step-count against M(0) controls → the cause-attribution axis buys nothing beyond subtask-boundary tracking (or worse, the mechanism was only ever tracking episode length, not history-dependence), and BATON's mechanism already suffices.
3. **H3 — Cause-attributed recovery beats uniform rollback at matched detection.**
   - *Prediction*: inserting [[2606.03385|GTP-FA]]-style grasp-vs-planning attribution between detection and recovery raises recovery SR over HELM's uniform return-to-checkpoint rollback, because the fix is chosen for the *cause* (the GTP-FA **11.2→76.8%** attribution gain is the reference).
   - *Test*: hold HELM's memory + detector fixed; swap uniform rollback for diagnosis-conditioned recovery; compare recovery SR on [[2505.12224|RoboFAC]]'s failure suite (9,440 erroneous trajectories + 78,623 QA pairs, 3-level task-planning/motion-planning/execution-control failure taxonomy + corrective annotations + sim+real recovery-SR test sets) so cause-attributed vs uniform recovery are scored on the same labeled failures.
   - *Row*: GTP-FA (diagnose, real-Franka attribution gain) / KITE (diagnose, RoboFAC-native detection/localization floor) / HELM (uniform rollback).
   - *Falsifier*: diagnosis-conditioned recovery matches uniform rollback → cause attribution doesn't improve recovery and HELM's uniform fix is enough.
4. **H4 — State-machine integration cuts the oscillation an undiagnosed loop produces.**
   - *Prediction*: a detect→recover loop without cause-attribution oscillates (re-applies the wrong fix); explicit state-machine mode transitions cut oscillation incidents ≥50% with no SR loss — a metric HELM never reports.
   - *Test*: run undiagnosed-compose vs diagnosis-gated state-machine; count oscillation incidents and SR.
   - *Row*: CycleVLA (proactive correction) / FailSafe (detect+recover).
   - *Falsifier*: undiagnosed composition shows no oscillation → the state-machine overhead buys nothing.
5. **H5 — A trained-VLA loop matches the agentic-orchestrated all-four-faces system at lower latency.**
   - *Prediction*: a *trained* cross-episode-memory loop matches [[2602.13086|UniManip]]'s zero-shot LLM-orchestrated four-face SR on long-horizon tasks while running at a fraction of its per-step orchestration latency, because the loop is amortized into weights, not re-planned by an LLM each step.
   - *Test*: head-to-head trained-loop vs UniManip on [[2506.06677|RoboCerebra]] (long-horizon, memory-intensive — 2,972-step trajectories, 100 task variants, with an explicit memory-utilization + reflection protocol); report SR and per-step latency.
   - *Row*: UniManip (agentic four-face) / LiLo (modular recovery).
   - *Falsifier*: the trained loop cannot match the orchestrator's SR → the four faces need an LLM planner, not a trained policy.
6. **H6 — Each verified recovery becomes a cross-episode training example without eroding base skills.**
   - *Prediction*: writing verified recoveries into cross-episode memory *and* back as training examples ([[2506.21669|SEEA-R1]] pattern) improves the policy continually, *if* writes are gated behind a recovery-success check and protected against poisoning ([[2605.02900|Safety-in-Embodied-AI-Survey]]) — bridging B3.
   - *Test*: run 100 recovery-update cycles; measure new-task gain and old-task retention with vs without B3 subspace protection.
   - *Row*: SEEA-R1 (recovery→training) / VASO (verification-gated).
   - *Falsifier*: cross-episode recovery updates erase base skills or fail to improve → the loop is unstable without B3.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **No existing benchmark tests the restart boundary** — [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]], [[2603.01229|RMBench]], and [[2603.04639|RoboMME]] all score memory within one continuous deployment; building the kill/reload/relaunch harness is real infrastructure work with no existing protocol to reuse. → Stage H1 behind H2: only build the restart harness once H2's within-deployment result is confirmed, so the more expensive test targets a result already known to hold in the easier setting.
> - **Composed-budget latency gate** — the loop is not one model: cross-episode retrieval + the diagnosis model + the recovery state machine each cost time, and only their *sum* against the deployment's **3–5 Hz** control-rate ceiling decides deployability — a per-component "fast enough" can still blow the composed budget. → Budget the three components *together* per step (retrieval + diagnosis + state-machine), report the composed control-rate, invoke recovery only on the verifier firing, and co-design against B1's efficiency risk; compress memory to per-failure-signature keys ([[2604.18791|HELM]]'s CLIP-indexed store, generalized across episodes).
> - **Calibration-under-drift of the recurrence key** — the cross-episode "have I failed this way before?" match is only as good as its failure-signature key, which can drift under pose / lighting / background change, so the recurrence detector silently mis-fires (false matches trigger the wrong cached fix; missed matches lose the recurrence). → Instrument the signature-matcher's calibration *under drift*: measure recurrence-recognition precision/recall across a pose/lighting-perturbed replay of the same failure ([[2510.13626|LIBERO-Plus]]-style perturbations), and gate H2's SR claim on the matcher staying calibrated, not just on clean-replay recurrence. [[2608.04574|SpatialSTALE]]'s VLM-agent study puts a number on the cost of skipping this: unfiltered stale spatial memory more than *doubled* an agent's death rate over no memory at all (74.4% vs 28.0%), while explicit staleness auditing halved it back down — concrete field evidence that an unaudited recurrence key is a net safety liability, not a neutral risk, and that auditing (not more memory) is the load-bearing fix.
> - **Undiagnosed-loop oscillation** — without cause-attribution the loop re-applies the wrong fix and oscillates. → Diagnosis-gated state-machine transitions are the mitigation the bet measures (H4's ≥50% oscillation reduction); H3 isolates the diagnosis contribution.
> - **Memory as attack surface** — [[2605.02900|Safety-in-Embodied-AI-Survey]] flags memory poisoning, amplified when memory persists across episodes. → Gate cross-episode memory writes behind a recovery-success check (H6); treat the failure buffer as untrusted input.

### B3 — Continual Policy Learning Without Catastrophic Forgetting

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | B — Evaluation, Robustness & Deployment |
| **Thesis** | Protect selective weight subspaces during continual policy fine-tuning instead of defaulting to data-replay. In an over-parameterized policy, the directions a new skill needs and those an old skill occupies are *mostly disjoint*, so forgetting is a subspace-overlap problem, not a storage one. The field assumes replaying old trajectories is the price of retention. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2508.07407\|Self-Evolving-AI-Agents-Survey]] (survey), [[2404.14387\|LLM-Self-Evolution-Survey]] (survey), [[2506.21872\|Continual-RL-Survey]] (survey), [[2510.20685\|C-Nav]] (benchmark), [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]] (benchmark), [[2605.15735\|UAM]] (method), [[1612.00796\|EWC]] (method), [[2602.21919\|Learning-in-the-Null-Space]] (method), [[2603.08763\|SPREAD]] (method, evaluated directly on [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]]'s lifelong suites) |
| **Key targets** | ≥+9.7 pp old-task SR over Data Replay ([[2510.20685\|C-Nav]] **42.61% vs 32.9%** [[2109.08238\|HM3D]]); manipulation retention on [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]]'s lifelong suites (130 tasks across SPATIAL/OBJECT/GOAL/LONG with built-in FWT / NBT / AUC for sequential continual learning) — [[2603.08763\|SPREAD]]'s policy-validated shared-subspace bar: **FWT 81.0% / AUC 73.0% / NBT 8.0%** on LIBERO-OBJECT, **NBT 9.0%** on LIBERO-GOAL (vs M2Distill's **NBT 20.0%**); replay-free manipulation transfer ([[2605.29562\|VLA-Pro]] real **5.8% → 65.0%**, unseen **+207%** [[2504.13059\|RoboTwin]]); embodiment tax **<5%** ([[2605.15735\|UAM]]); ≤−3 pp new-task SR vs full fine-tune; [[2605.06175\|VLA-GSE]] 81.2% zero-shot LIBERO-Plus (shared-subspace SVD-split baseline); [[2606.15685\|SCE]] NBT 0.0 (real-world zero-negative-backward-transfer bar) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a fielded policy is fine-tuned again and again (new objects, new corrections from B2's loop), and every fine-tune erodes prior skill — yet forgetting shows up elsewhere in this doc only as a *risk* (B2 names it the hazard its recovery-update loop must avoid), never as a first-class objective.
- **Today's answers**: replay-free beating data-replay on action policies is now *consensus* — [[2601.09512|CLARE]] is exemplar-free continual VLA that beats experience replay by up to **15%** AUC with near-zero NBT (**−0.80% to 1.85%**) at only **1.7–2.3%** params/task, and [[2603.09298|CORAL-LoRA-Experts]] reaches **99.3%** LIBERO with no forgetting by construction. But both win by *per-task adapter expansion* (adding parameters per skill), not B3's distinctive single *protected shared* subspace; [[2510.20685|C-Nav]] (replay-free **42.61% vs 32.9%** [[2109.08238|HM3D]]) and [[2605.15735|UAM]] (**<5%** embodiment tax) are the same expansion-or-dual-path reflex.
- **The opening**: the *mechanism* B3 conjectures is now theoretically grounded but unrun on policies — [[2603.02224|Subspace-Geometry-Forgetting]] proves the principal-angle Geometric Forgetting Law $F=\alpha(1-\cos^2\theta_{\min})+\beta$ (r=**0.994** on synthetic) but only on Split-CIFAR100 / Sequential GLUE, never robot policies, and [[2602.06043|Shared-LoRA-Subspaces]] is the unified-shared-subspace alternative (up to **100×** params / **281×** memory saved, backward transfer CoLA 56.00→59.81) but LLM-only. Nobody has tested either on action policies.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A large policy has far more weights than any one skill needs. The handful of weight directions a new skill leans on barely overlap with the ones an old skill already uses — so forgetting happens only where they *do* overlap, in a small set of shared directions, not because the model ran out of room. The fix is therefore to *protect those shared directions*, not to re-show the old data.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "replay is the safe default" — [[2601.09512|CLARE]] and [[2603.09298|CORAL-LoRA-Experts]] already beat replay replay-free, so that is consensus. The open claim is sharper: that a *single protected shared subspace* beats *per-task adapter expansion* on action policies — that the principal-angle Geometric Forgetting Law ([[2603.02224|Subspace-Geometry-Forgetting]]) holds for *policies*, so protecting the high-overlap directions in one shared subspace matches expansion's retention at a flat (non-growing) parameter budget. CLARE/CORAL bet expansion is necessary; [[2602.06043|Shared-LoRA-Subspaces]] bets a unified subspace suffices but only proves it for LLMs.
- **The bet**: On a robot-policy task sequence, consecutive fine-tunes overlap below the Geometric-Forgetting-Law threshold (principal-angle $\cos^2\theta_{\min}$ predicting forgetting at r > 0.5), and protecting that single shared subspace beats the *flat*-budget policy-side incumbent ([[2603.08763|SPREAD]]'s NBT 8.0% LIBERO-OBJECT / 9.0% LIBERO-GOAL) and matches per-task-expansion retention (CLARE's near-zero NBT) at that same flat parameter budget, while holding the embodiment tax <5% ([[2605.15735|UAM]]) and new-task SR within −3 pp of full fine-tune — making forgetting a geometry problem, not a storage or expansion one.

**Related research papers.**

Fifty-two systems that handle continual learning — by subspace protection, per-task adapter expansion, dual-path architecture, replay-content scheduling, latent-dynamics recurrence, modality-incremental fusion, or weight merging — most borrowed from classification and few validated on action policies. The axis is *how it protects old skills and whether it uses a single shared subspace (vs per-task expansion) and is policy-validated*:

| [[2601.09512\|CLARE]] | per-task LoRA adapters + autoencoder routing | yes (exemplar-free) | **+15%** AUC over ER, NBT **−0.80 to 1.85%**, **1.7–2.3%** params/task | proves replay-free > replay on VLA — but wins by per-task *expansion*, not one protected shared subspace; no overlap analysis |
| [[2603.09298\|CORAL-LoRA-Experts]] | frozen base + per-task LoRA experts + language routing | yes | **99.3%** [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]], no forgetting by construction | no-forget-by-construction on policies — but again expansion + isolation, not shared-subspace protection |
| [[2603.02224\|Subspace-Geometry-Forgetting]] | principal-angle Geometric Forgetting Law | (theory) | $F=\alpha(1-\cos^2\theta_{\min})+\beta$, r=**0.994** synthetic | proves the law H1 conjectures — but on Split-CIFAR100 / GLUE, never robot policies |
| [[2602.06043\|Shared-LoRA-Subspaces]] | single continually-refined shared subspace (SVD) | yes | **100×** params / **281×** memory saved, backward transfer | the unified-shared-subspace alternative to expansion — but LLM/vision only, not action policies |
| [[2605.15735\|UAM]] | dual-stream (Semantic + Dorsal Expert) | yes | **>95%** VLM retention, **<5%** embodiment tax | architectural, no continual-*task-sequence* eval |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (replay-free subspace protection beats replay on action policies), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — The Geometric Forgetting Law holds on policies across *both* an orthogonal and a correlated continual stream — and the overlap matrix is the primary deliverable.**
   - *Prediction*: the durable artifact is the **policy-overlap-matrix diagnostic** itself (principal-angle / Fisher overlap predicting which task pairs forget), publishable even if the protection scheme (H2) doesn't win. It must hold on two streams, not one: **(a) orthogonal stream** — a diverse [[2306.03310|LIBERO]] task sequence where overlap stays below ~0.3 for most pairs and $\cos^2\theta_{\min}$ predicts forgetting at r > 0.5; **(b) correlated stream (external validity, promoted to a first-class step)** — *repeated re-fine-tunes of one skill family* (e.g. successive variants of the same grasp/place skill, the realistic deployment case where B2's recovery updates keep hitting the same skill), where overlap is *high* and the law's prediction must still hold (forgetting tracks the high overlap) for the geometry account to be valid in the regime that actually occurs in the field.
   - *Test*: measure principal-angle / Fisher overlap across (a) [[2306.03310|LIBERO]]'s diverse lifelong 130-task stream (NBT/AUC substrate) *and* (b) a constructed correlated stream of repeated re-fine-tunes of one skill family; fit the Geometric Forgetting Law on *both*; build the overlap matrix predicting which pairs forget on each, and report the law's r on the correlated stream separately.
   - *Row*: Subspace Geometry Forgetting (the law) / EWC (subspace-importance prior).
   - *Falsifier*: the law doesn't transfer on the correlated stream (high overlap but principal angle fails to predict forgetting, or protection is impossible because the high-overlap directions *are* the new-skill directions) → the geometry account holds only in the easy orthogonal regime and fails exactly where deployment lives; or overlap > 0.5 dominates the orthogonal stream too → subspaces aren't disjoint on policies and shared-subspace protection can't work.
2. **H2 — A single protected shared subspace matches per-task expansion at a flat parameter budget.**
   - *Prediction*: protecting only the high-overlap (H1) directions in one shared subspace ([[2602.06043|Shared-LoRA-Subspaces]]-style, ported to policies) matches [[2601.09512|CLARE]]'s per-task-expansion retention (near-zero NBT) *without* growing parameters per task.
   - *Test*: head-to-head unified-shared-subspace vs CLARE/CORAL per-task expansion vs replay on [[2306.03310|LIBERO]]'s lifelong suite (the shared substrate on which CLARE / CORAL / AtomicVLA / ConSFT / MergeVLA all report AUC/NBT, making the comparison directly comparable), anchoring the shared-subspace arm on [[2603.08763|SPREAD]] — the existing flat-budget SVD-subspace-distillation result *on policies* (LIBERO NBT 9.0% vs M2Distill 20.0%) — so the bet's contribution is the overlap-driven *which-directions-to-protect* refinement over SPREAD's whole-subspace alignment, not the shared-subspace idea itself; report old-task SR, NBT, and parameter growth.
   - *Row*: CLARE (per-task expansion) / SPREAD (flat-budget shared-subspace distillation, policy-validated) / Shared LoRA Subspaces (unified subspace, LLM-only).
   - *Falsifier*: the shared subspace underperforms expansion at equal retention → per-task expansion is necessary on policies and the flat-budget claim fails; or SPREAD's whole-subspace alignment already matches the overlap-driven protection → the principal-angle refinement (H1) adds nothing over aligning the full dominant subspace.
3. **H3 — The embodiment tax stays <5% across a continual sequence.**
   - *Prediction*: after each fine-tune, re-running the frozen VLM's multimodal suite ([[2311.16502|MMMU]] / [[2306.13394|MME]] / [[2307.06281|MMBench]]) shows the tax stays <5% across ≥5 sequential tasks, matching [[2605.15735|UAM]]'s single-step result over a sequence.
   - *Test*: track multimodal-competence drop across 5 sequential fine-tunes.
   - *Row*: UAM (dual-stream, <5% tax).
   - *Falsifier*: the tax exceeds 5% over the sequence → dual-path doesn't hold competence under repeated fine-tuning.
4. **H4 — Subspace protection stops B2's recovery updates from erasing base skills.**
   - *Prediction*: feeding B2's recovery-success examples as a task stream, the subspace method keeps old-task SR within −3 pp over 100 recovery updates, where unprotected continual updates erode it.
   - *Test*: measure old-task SR before/after 100 recovery updates with vs without subspace protection (bridges B2).
   - *Row*: Long-Lived Robots (RFT continual) / CRL-VLA (dual-critic).
   - *Falsifier*: protected updates still erase base skills → the loop B2+B3 forms is unstable.
5. **H5 — A shared-subspace point dominates per-task expansion on the storage-vs-retention Pareto.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping subspace dimension × protection strength yields a flat-budget shared-subspace point that dominates *both* replay and per-task expansion ([[2601.09512|CLARE]]/[[2603.09298|CORAL-LoRA-Experts]]) on the storage-vs-retention frontier, not merely matching them.
   - *Test*: plot retention vs cumulative storage for shared-subspace vs per-task-expansion vs replay over [[2306.03310|LIBERO]]'s lifelong manipulation sequence (AUC across the task stream supplying per-step retention); locate the dominating point.
   - *Row*: CLARE (per-task expansion) / PHASER (replay-content).
   - *Falsifier*: no shared-subspace point dominates → per-task expansion stays Pareto-competitive and the claim narrows to "matches expansion."

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Subspaces may not be disjoint for *similar* tasks** — two manipulation skills sharing contact dynamics could overlap heavily, collapsing the geometric premise. → H1's overlap matrix is the go/no-go: if overlap >0.5 dominates, fall back to memory ([[2605.10993|ECHO-VLA]]) rather than subspace protection.
> - **Plasticity collapse** — protecting too many directions freezes new-task learning (the [[2405.09673|LoRA-Learns-Less]] failure mode). → Bound protection strength by the ≤−3 pp new-task-SR target; report the retention/plasticity frontier, not a single point.
> - **Replay quietly wins at scale** — if storage is cheap and privacy a non-issue, replay may stay competitive. → Frame the deliverable as the storage-vs-retention Pareto (H5); the claim is *dominance on both axes*, not merely matching replay.

---

## Cluster C — Embodiment Generalization: Transferring Across Bodies

*Cross-embodiment transfer fails by factoring away load-bearing structure — the body's morphology — that the policy needed to keep. Keep the morphology-invariant intermediate and the fixed-body assumption stops breaking. The per-capability instantiations defer to the sibling docs; here the mechanism is the contribution.*

### C1 — Morphology-Invariant Action Representations for Cross-Embodiment Zero-Shot Transfer

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | C — Embodiment Generalization |
| **Thesis** | The open question is *which invariant intermediate* — language, latent goal, pointmap, or phase — carries control across robot *families*, not how to retarget within one. Some abstraction of "pick up the cup" is morphology-invariant by construction; native-per-body tokenization is the accidental one that couples policy to a single body plan. The field assumes cross-family transfer needs per-robot fine-tuning. The bet is in First-principles below. The per-capability *instantiations* defer to the siblings: cross-morphology *hands* to [[Manipulation\|Manipulation]], cross-embodiment *whole-body* to [[Whole-Body\|Whole-Body]]. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2510.07077\|VLA-Robotics-Real-World-Review]] (survey), [[2504.03515\|Dexterous-IL-Survey]] (survey), [[2604.04707\|OpenWorldLib]] (survey), [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] (benchmark), [[2602.10556\|LAP]] (method), [[2605.20811\|Demo-JEPA]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | >30% extrapolation SR on [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] novel-morphology split (current **0%**); >50% zero-shot cross-embodiment ([[2602.10556\|LAP]], **2×** prior policies); **0.36** one-shot ([[2605.20811\|Demo-JEPA]], vs **0.04** [[2412.14803\|VPP]]) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: "pick up the cup" denotes the same task intent for a 7-DoF arm, a parallel gripper, or a humanoid hand, but a policy that tokenizes in *joint space* couples its representation to a single body plan — and [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] is the brutal diagnostic: multi-embodiment policies match single-embodiment baselines on *seen* robots and *interpolation* but collapse to **0%** SR on *extrapolation* across very different link structures.
- **Today's answers**: the candidates differ only in *which intermediate they make invariant* — [[2602.10556|LAP]] parses continuous actions into *natural language* and hits **>50%** zero-shot (**2×** prior); [[2605.20811|Demo-JEPA]] abstracts demonstrations into *target-compatible latent goals* (**0.36** one-shot vs [[2412.14803|VPP]]'s **0.04**); [[2606.03943|PointAction]] uses 3D pointmaps; [[2606.01851|PHASOR]] uses motion phase; [[2407.15002|GET]] encodes the kinematic *graph* into attention and gets **20%** zero-shot to unseen graph structure and link length on dexterous hands; [[2602.08278|DexFormer]] infers morphology from *history* in a shared canonical finger space (**77.5%** over 32 unseen hands). All replace native-per-body tokenization — but none has faced AnyBody's reach/push extrapolation wall. The sharpest new partial-domain data point is [[2607.03570|UHAS]]: a sphere-deformation action field over a hand's URDF reaches **95.3–98.1%** zero-shot transfer to a held-out hand (Shadow the outlier at 85.7%, recovered to 95.8% with 500 fine-tune iterations) — genuine extrapolation, not interpolation, on hand-DoF variation specifically, strengthening the case that *some* structured invariant survives novel morphology even though it stays inside the hand-family scope C1 defers to [[Manipulation|Manipulation]]. A *second family* sidesteps the representation question entirely: [[2606.24049|SPACE-Cross-Robot]] predicts a controller-independent Cartesian state-delta and recovers the body gap with an online-adapted linear map (**92%** zero-shot UR5→FR3); [[2606.22836|Cloak]] *masks* the end-effector from wrist views so the policy never sees the morphology (**81.8%** on an unseen 5-finger hand); and [[2512.13100|OXE-AugE]] floods training with physics-accurate cross-embodiment replays so embodiment-agnostic reps emerge from *data scale* (OpenVLA **+24%** / π0 **+45%** on unseen configs) — the "scale the data, not the representation" alternative the bake-off must control for.
- **The opening**: the *measurement*, not the principle, is the gap, and the durable deliverable is the **cross-embodiment bake-off harness + its controls**, not whichever invariant wins this year's leaderboard. [[2407.15002|GET]] and [[2602.08278|DexFormer]] already do the essential conceptual thing — an invariant that survives unseen morphology — on the narrower in-hand-reconfiguration domain; [[2605.25044|X-DiffVLA]]'s morphological-tree diffusion head escapes per-robot fine-tuning (**+15.3 pp** over π0.5 on RoboCasa). What none has done is run an invariant *bake-off* against the **0%** [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] arm-extrapolation split — **but that 0% is one paper's baseline number, and before it can anchor a "law" it must be re-derived against a *properly-tuned* joint-space baseline**: AnyBody's reported 0% may be an architecture artifact (an under-tuned multi-embodiment head), not a property of joint-space tokenization, so the harness must reproduce the 0% under a sim-RL-tuned joint-space control first, then layer the invariants on top.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: "Pick up the cup" denotes the same *task intent* for a 7-DoF arm, a parallel gripper, or a humanoid hand. The intent is morphology-invariant; the joint-space trajectory is morphology-specific. A representation grounded in intent (language, latent goal, or task-space) is invariant *by construction*; native-joint tokenization is the accidental one that couples policy to body.
- **Assumption being challenged**: Not "cross-family transfer needs per-robot fine-tuning" — [[2203.11931|MetaMorph-UC]] broke that for modular locomotion in 2022 and [[2407.15002|GET]] broke it for link-structure in 2024, so the *principle* that some invariant enables zero-shot transfer is consensus. The open claim is sharper and empirical: *which* invariant — language, latent goal, pointmap, phase, or graph — survives the [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] reach/push arm-*extrapolation* split that still stands at **0%**. The candidates ([[2602.10556|LAP]], [[2605.20811|Demo-JEPA]], [[2407.15002|GET]]) each bet *their own* intermediate, untested head-to-head on that wall.
- **The bet**: In a head-to-head bake-off, at least one intent-grounded invariant reaches >30% zero-shot SR on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s arm-extrapolation split (current best: **0%**), consistent with [[2602.10556|LAP]]'s **>50%** zero-shot and [[2407.15002|GET]]'s **20%** zero-shot to unseen graph structure — turning extrapolation to novel link structures from an impossibility into a measurable transfer rate, and naming which invariant carries it.

**Related research papers.**

Forty-nine cross-embodiment systems that make a *different* intermediate invariant — language, latent goal, pointmap, phase, kinematic graph, discrete universal-action codebook, history, soft-prompt, object/optical flow, particle state, visual aids, observation re-render/mask, EE state-delta, motion-prior, sim-augmentation, or native joint — none yet beating the AnyBody arm-extrapolation wall. The axis is *which invariant intermediate carries control across families and whether it faces true extrapolation*:

| System | Invariant intermediate | Faces AnyBody wall? | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] | 18-robot benchmark | — (sets the wall) | interpolation transfers, extrapolation **0%** | the unbeaten 0% benchmark C1 must break, not a method |
| [[2407.15002\|GET]] | kinematic *graph* encoded in attention + self-modeling FK loss | partial (in-hand) | **20%** zero-shot to unseen graph structure + link length | does the essential conceptual thing (invariant survives novel structure) — but on in-hand dexterous reconfiguration, not the AnyBody reach/push arm split |
| [[2602.08278\|DexFormer]] | history-inferred morphology, shared canonical finger space | partial (32 hands) | **77.5%** over 32 unseen hands (vs LSTM **58.7%**) | implicit-history invariant across hands — within hand-family, untested on full link-structure extrapolation |
| [[2607.03570\|UHAS]] | unified hand action space (URDF-derived sphere-deformation field over driving planes/vectors) + cascade inverse kinematics | partial (4 hand families) — but genuinely *extrapolates*, not just interpolates | zero-shot to a held-out hand **95.3–98.1%** (Shadow **85.7%**, recovered to **95.8%** with 500 fine-tune iters vs ~4,500 from scratch) | the strongest partial-domain evidence yet that a structured invariant *extrapolates* to unseen morphology rather than only interpolating within a trained hand family — sharper than [[2602.08278\|DexFormer]]'s 77.5% and [[2407.15002\|GET]]'s 20%, but the domain is hand-DoF variation (Allegro/LEAP/Shadow/MANO), not the [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]] reach/push arm-link-structure wall H1's bake-off targets |
| [[2203.11931\|MetaMorph-UC]] | morphology-as-token sequence (kinematic tree) | partial (locomotion) | zero-shot to unseen physical-property + module variation | broke "needs per-robot tuning" in 2022 — but locomotion modules, not the AnyBody manipulation wall |
| [[2602.10556\|LAP]] | natural-language actions | no | **>50%** zero-shot (**2×** prior), 2.5× fewer demos | intent-grounded space, but untested on AnyBody extrapolation |
| [[2605.20811\|Demo-JEPA]] | target-compatible latent goal | no | **0.36** sim / **0.25** real one-shot (vs [[2412.14803\|VPP]] **0.04**) | demonstration-as-latent-goal, not yet against novel link structures |
| [[2606.03943\|PointAction]] | 3D pointmaps + per-robot decoder | no | real **43.0%** xArm7, **2-2.5×** over baselines | a *third* invariant beside language and latent goal — untested on the wall |
| [[2606.01851\|PHASOR]] | motion phase (FFT + InfoNCE) | no | **90.3%** human→robot retrieval, beats raw-kinematics | invariance via phase, retrieval-validated, not extrapolation SR |

| [[2603.00732\|UniHM]] | morphology-agnostic VQ-VAE tokenizer | partial (hands) | lower MPJPE, higher real grab SR across hands | the shared-tokenizer route, hand-scope, not the AnyBody wall |

| [[2606.12965\|EmbodiSteer]] | training-free *deployment-time steering* of an embodiment-agnostic Cartesian policy into joint space (Jacobian + CBF-QP guidance) | no (the invariance-tax face) | **+28.5%** sim / **+36.7%** real SR, **-90%** collisions on UR5/Franka, real-time **9.61 Hz** | the deployment/H4 face: how an agnostic Cartesian action space is made *body-safe* at execution without per-morphology training — complements every learned-invariant row by paying the invariance tax at inference, but it *consumes* an invariant policy rather than proposing a new intermediate |
| [[2606.24049\|SPACE-Cross-Robot]] | controller-independent *Cartesian state-delta* (EE displacement) + online-calibrated linear Action Adapter | no | **+30%** avg cross-embodiment SR, **92%** zero-shot UR5→FR3; **84%** under unseen-unit hardware variation (vs **18%** baseline), holds at **5×** payload | the EE-displacement invariant the table lacked between [[2606.03943\|PointAction]]'s pointmaps and the latent-goal rows — decouples the policy from robot-specific controllers and pushes the morphology gap into a *deployment-time LMS-adapted* linear map, so transfer is recovered at execution; but eval is cross-unit / cross-controller *interpolation*, never the AnyBody reach/push arm-extrapolation wall |
| [[2606.22836\|Cloak]] | end-effector *visual masking* in wrist-cam views (geometric render from proprioception) + tip-pose retargeting | no | **81.8%** progression on an unseen 5-finger Sharpa hand (vs baselines **54.4–60.2%**), **85.1%** UMI / **86.3%** YAM, near the **88.0%** source rate | the observation-canonicalization route by *removing* the embodiment cue rather than re-rendering it — distinct from [[2402.19249\|Mirage-XPolicy]]/[[2503.00774\|Shadow]] cross-painting/segmentation-overlay; masks the EE silhouette so the VLA reasons embodiment-agnostically across grippers↔hands, but transfer is across end-effectors, not the AnyBody arm-extrapolation split |
| [[2606.26095\|Action-Priors]] | decoupled *unconditioned-motion prior* pretrained (flow-matching) then reused as the VLA action head + latent-alignment distillation | no | **55.3→68.0%** avg over 13 cross-embodiment tasks (>GR00T **48.6%** / π0.5 **53.8%**), real Franka **35.0→66.3%**; 8× lower init loss | the *pretraining-substrate* route to cross-embodiment robustness — gives the action head a shared motion prior before cross-modal alignment, complementary to the invariant-representation rows (it stabilizes whichever action space is used) rather than proposing a new intermediate; untested on the AnyBody wall |
| [[2512.13100\|OXE-AugE]] | *physics-based sim-augmentation* tripling OXE across 9 embodiments (learn embodiment-agnostic visual reps from data scale) | no | OpenVLA **+24%** / π0 **+45%** SR on unseen robot-gripper configs; **75–82%** on a novel 'Robotiq++' body (diffusion-augmentation instead *drops* SR 27–30%) | the data-scaling counterpart to every learned-invariant row — instead of engineering an invariant intermediate, it floods training with physically-accurate cross-embodiment replays so embodiment-agnostic visual reps *emerge*; the alternative hypothesis (scale-the-data, not the-representation) the bake-off should include as a control, but not tested on AnyBody extrapolation |
| [[2608.06374\|DyPES-VLA]] | *shared future-prediction query* (dynamics prior, supervised on both action-free video and action-labeled demos) decoded by an embodiment-specific Mixture-of-Experts head directly in each robot's *native* action space — explicitly built to avoid the manual coordinate-transform / IK alignment every other row in this table still needs | no | **89.02%** RoboTwin 2.0, **98.0%** LIBERO, real **75.6%** avg across Franka/AgileX/Unitree G1 (vs independently-trained ACT **32.4%**, GR00T-N1.6 generalist **59.6%**); ablation: the future-prediction objective is the largest single contributor | a distinct answer to the invariant-vs-native-action tension: the invariant lives in what's *predicted* (shared dynamics/contact query), not in the *action representation itself* — native per-embodiment MoE decoding sidesteps the invariance-tax H4 measures for language/latent-goal spaces, but it is validated on seen-robot generalist transfer, never against [[2505.14986\|AnyBody]]'s reach/push extrapolation split |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (an intent-grounded invariant breaks AnyBody's extrapolation wall), with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — A bake-off names *which* invariant beats 0% on AnyBody arm-extrapolation.**
   - *Prediction*: running [[2602.10556|LAP]] (language-action), [[2605.20811|Demo-JEPA]] (latent goal), [[2407.15002|GET]]-style kinematic-graph, and task-space tokenization head-to-head on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s extrapolation/composition splits, at least one intent-grounded invariant exceeds **30%** SR where joint-space policies score 0% — and the bake-off ranks them.
   - *Test*: invariant-representation bake-off on the AnyBody extrapolation/composition splits; report SR per invariant and the ranking.
   - *Row*: GET (kinematic graph) / LAP (language) / Demo-JEPA (latent goal).
   - *Falsifier*: every invariant stays at ~0% on the arm-extrapolation split → the wall is not a tokenization problem (note: GET already clears it in-hand, so a flat 0% across all invariants here would localize the wall to arm reach/push specifically).
2. **H2 — The AnyBody 0% survives a properly-tuned joint-space baseline, and is memorization, not under-tuning.**
   - *Prediction*: two controls, run *in order before* any invariant comparison. **(a) Sim-RL-tuning control (first):** a joint-space baseline given the *same* sim-RL tuning budget as the invariant arms (matched RL steps, reward shaping, domain randomization) still scores ~0% on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] arm-extrapolation — confirming the 0% is a tokenization property, not [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s under-tuned head. **(b) Interpolation control (second):** the same tuned joint-space baseline succeeds on the *interpolation* split of the *same* tasks, so the gap is attributable to morphology rather than task-hardness. Only if both hold does H1's bake-off measure invariance; and probing [[2409.20537|HPT]]-style per-embodiment stems then shows low cross-morphology representation overlap (body-specific memorization) explaining the surviving 0%.
   - *Test*: (a) re-derive AnyBody arm-extrapolation SR with a sim-RL-tuned joint-space baseline at matched budget; (b) report interpolation SR on the same tasks; (c) measure representation overlap across morphologies for joint-space stems vs an invariant intermediate.
   - *Row*: HPT (per-embodiment stems) / RT-1 (native tokens).
   - *Falsifier*: the tuned joint-space baseline rises well above 0% on arm-extrapolation → the reported 0% was an architecture/tuning artifact, not a tokenization law, and the bake-off's headline framing must be re-anchored to the tuned baseline; or joint-space stems show high cross-morphology overlap → the residual gap isn't memorization and invariance won't fix it.
3. **H3 — Language-intent and latent-goal invariants compose better than either alone.**
   - *Prediction*: combining [[2602.10556|LAP]]'s language intermediate (*what*) with [[2605.20811|Demo-JEPA]]'s latent goal (*how-on-this-body*) beats either alone on AnyBody composition.
   - *Test*: train language-only, latent-goal-only, and composed; evaluate on the composition split.
   - *Row*: LAP (language) / Demo-JEPA (latent goal).
   - *Falsifier*: the composition doesn't beat the better single invariant → the two intermediates are redundant.
4. **H4 — The invariance tax on precision is bounded on seen robots.**
   - *Prediction*: measuring EE error of an invariant-space vs native-joint policy on *seen* robots, the invariant loses bounded precision (a quantifiable tax), so the extrapolation gain isn't free but is worth it.
   - *Test*: report EE precision of invariant vs native-joint on seen robots; bound the tax before claiming extrapolation gains.
   - *Row*: PointAction (pointmap + decoder) / X-DiffVLA (tree diffusion).
   - *Falsifier*: the invariance tax is unbounded (precision collapses on seen robots) → the invariant is unusable for precise control.
5. **H5 — Handoff: the invariant survives the jump from fixed arms to a coupled body, or it doesn't.**
   - *Prediction*: an invariant *task intent* assumes the body is a passive executor, but whole-body loco-manipulation couples that intent to balance and contact — the invariant either survives the jump to a humanoid or coupling breaks it (the instantiation belongs to [[Whole-Body|Whole-Body]]; C1 certifies the cross-family representation it hands off).
   - *Test*: evaluate the AnyBody-validated invariant on [[2506.09366|SkillBlender]]'s SkillBench (the coupled whole-body, cross-embodiment loco-manip substrate across 3 Unitree humanoids with balance/feasibility metrics — Tilt / Root-Height / Joint-Torque — that capture intent coupled to balance and contact); measure SR retention vs the fixed-arm result.
   - *Row*: Qwen-VLA (manipulation+navigation generalist) / HoloBrain-0 (embodiment-prior generalist).
   - *Falsifier*: the invariant transfers to the coupled body with no loss → intent-invariance is sufficient even under coupling, and the handoff to Whole-Body is unnecessary.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Invariance may cost precision** — a language/latent action space could blur fine-grained control native joints capture. → H4 bounds the invariance tax on seen robots before claiming extrapolation gains; report the precision floor.
> - **[[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s 0% may be partly task-hardness or an architecture artifact, not pure morphology** — extrapolation tasks could be intrinsically harder, or the 0% could be an under-tuned baseline. → H2's two ordered controls (sim-RL-tuned joint-space baseline first, interpolation SR on the same tasks second) gate the attribution; report the *tuned* baseline as the real wall, not AnyBody's reported 0%.
> - **OOD power / benchmark validity** — a >30% extrapolation claim against a 0% (or near-0%) floor needs enough episodes to be a real effect, and the bake-off's seen-robot anchor ([[2306.03310|LIBERO]]-style) is saturated, carrying no discriminative power. → State the minimum detectable effect at the chosen episode budget (binomial SE near a 0% floor is asymmetric — a handful of successes is not yet a transfer rate); report SR with CIs per invariant, and put discriminative weight on the extrapolation column, not the saturated seen-robot one.
> - **Language-actions discretize continuous control** — [[2602.10556|LAP]]'s parsing may lose high-frequency detail. → Pair the language intermediate with a knowledge-insulated continuous action expert ([[2602.10556|LAP]]'s own design) rather than acting in language directly.
> - **The win may be data, not representation** — [[2512.13100|OXE-AugE]] shows physics-accurate cross-embodiment data-augmentation alone lifts unseen-config SR (OpenVLA **+24%** / π0 **+45%**), so a representation invariant could be matched by simply scaling diverse-body replays. → Add a *data-scaled joint-space* control to H1's bake-off (a native-token policy trained on OXE-AugE-style augmented data): the invariant wins only if it beats data-scaling on the *extrapolation* split, not just the seen/interpolation configs OXE-AugE was tested on.

---

## Cluster D — Egocentric & Human-Video Pretraining

*The same refusal, pushed upstream of the robot body entirely: don't factor away the kinematic structure a human hand carries across the human-to-robot hop (D1), and don't cascade the pretraining objective into two separate stages when a coupled loop might carry a synergy neither stage alone can (D2).*

### D1 — Regime-Contingent Bridging Mechanisms for Human-to-Robot Skill Transfer

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Egocentric & Human-Video Pretraining |
| **Thesis** | A human hand carries 22+ degrees of freedom; most grippers have 1–7. That mismatch has to be absorbed somewhere in the pipeline, and the field has converged on six distinct places to absorb it — but every paper validates its own choice only against a no-transfer or unaligned baseline, never against a sibling mechanism trained on the same data. The field treats *where* the gap gets absorbed as a one-off architecture decision instead of a variable worth sweeping. The bet is in First-principles below. This direction is about closing the gap during *pretraining*, before any robot body is chosen; C1 above owns which invariant carries control across different *robot* families once both sides already are robots. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.27621\|Robot-Learning-from-Human-Videos-Survey]] (survey), [[2512.22414\|π0.5-+-ego]] (method), [[2507.15597\|Being-H0]] (method), [[2602.16710\|EgoScale]] (method), [[2511.21690\|TraceGen]] (method), [[2503.00779\|Phantom]] (method), [[2502.16587\|Human2Robot]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | [[2507.15597\|Being-H0]] **25%** teleop data matches **50–100%**-data baselines; [[2512.22414\|π0.5-+-ego]] egg-sorting **57%→78%**; [[2602.16710\|EgoScale]] **+54%** 22-DoF SR / **88%** shirt-folding from one demo; [[2503.00779\|Phantom]] **92%** Pick/Place with *zero* robot data; [[2503.07135\|VidBot]] **88.2%** sim / **80%** real with zero robot data; the harness targets — a pre-registered ≤10 pp no-single-winner margin (H1) and a bounded zero-robot-data precision tax (H4) |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: a human hand's 22+ DoF has to become a robot gripper's 1–7 DoF somewhere in the pipeline, and the field has converged on at least six distinct places to put that conversion — yet every method paper reports a win only against a no-transfer or unaligned baseline, never against a sibling mechanism trained on the same data.
- **Today's answers**: the six families are each mature and each shows a real number — explicit projection ([[2507.15597|Being-H0]]'s MANO/GRQ-VAE tokenization reaches **99.8–100%** valid generation and matches 50–100%-data baselines with only **25%** as much teleop data), learned-gap co-training ([[2512.22414|π0.5-+-ego]] lifts egg-sorting **57%→78%** with no kinematic alignment at all), mid-training alignment ([[2602.16710|EgoScale]]'s two-stage recipe adds **+54%** task SR on a 22-DoF hand), embodiment-agnostic intermediate representations ([[2511.21690|TraceGen]]'s 3D trace-space reaches **67.5%** human-to-robot transfer from five uncalibrated phone videos), visual embodiment-gap editing ([[2503.00779|Phantom]] trains with *zero* robot data and still hits **92%** Pick/Place), and generative video transfer ([[2502.16587|Human2Robot]]'s conditional-video framing reaches **95%** vs XSkill's **53%**). None of the six has ever run against another on the same task, platform, or egocentric-hour budget.
- **The opening**: [[2607.08436|EgoWAM]] already proves the controlled-sweep move works *within* one family — holding an HPT backbone fixed and varying only the world-model prediction target flips the winner by regime (DINO features for OOD generalization, 3D motion flow for in-domain precision). That is the exact methodology D1 ports *across* the six families, and [[2503.07135|VidBot]] (**88.2%** sim / **80%** real) and [[2503.23877|ZeroMimic]] (**71.0%** real / **73.8%** sim) already prove the extreme end of the spectrum is viable — zero robot demonstrations, not zero mechanism. [[2608.12122|HandEdit]] is the first sign the field is building bake-off infrastructure at all — a two-track (hand-only / hand-arm) benchmark scoring nine image-editing systems (GPT-Image-2, OmniGen2, FireRed, …) head-to-head on structural fidelity, interaction quality, and skin-residue artifacts for the *visual embodiment-gap editing* mechanism specifically, and it already surfaces the exact failure D1 predicts: VLM-judgment and embodiment-grounded fidelity diverge sharply (GPT-Image-1.5 scores highest on VLM judgment yet only 0.435 on interaction fidelity) — but the comparison stays within one mechanism family, not across all six.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: The kinematic gap between a human hand and a robot end-effector is conserved information — it does not disappear, it only relocates. A bridging mechanism is a choice of *where* in the pipeline (input encoding, learned representation, or a dedicated training stage) that relocation happens, and each choice trades off precision, data-efficiency, and OOD-robustness differently. [[2607.08436|EgoWAM]]'s controlled sweep — one backbone, one axis varied — is the existence proof that a controlled sweep *within* a mechanism family already surfaces a regime-split winner (DINO: **4×** OOD; 3D-flow: **20–30%** in-domain), not a universal one; D1 extends that exact move *across* mechanism families.
- **Assumption being challenged**: [[2602.16710|EgoScale]]'s own claim that large-scale human motion "provides a robust, embodiment-agnostic motor prior," which the vault's own deep-dive synthesizes as holding "across multiple projection schemes" ([[14_Egocentric-Pretraining-and-Human-Video|14_Egocentric-Pretraining-and-Human-Video]] §5, "the data axis dominates the architectural one") — a synthesis in tension with EgoScale's own RQ5 ablation, where a wrist-only projection performed poorly and only the retargeted-joint-space scheme was consistently strong, so the projection scheme is not actually interchangeable. [[2512.22414|π0.5-+-ego]]'s own claim that "human-to-robot transfer is an emergent property, scaling with the diversity and size of the VLA's pre-training data" makes the same data-scale bet independently. Neither paper tests the mechanism-choice question directly: π0.5-+-ego is validated only against a no-human-data, unaligned baseline, never against [[2507.15597|Being-H0]]'s explicit MANO tokenization or EgoScale's own mid-training alignment on the same tasks, hours held fixed.
- **The bet**: On a shared human-video-to-robot harness — two platform tiers (a parallel-jaw tier for wrist-pose bridging, and a dexterous-hand tier where Being-H0/VIDEOMANIP's finger-level mechanism actually applies), matched egocentric-pretraining hours across arms, and a task suite split into precision-critical (insertion, tool-use) vs gross-reach/OOD-scene cells — sweeping the six mechanisms, no single mechanism's SR margin over the second-best exceeds a pre-registered **10 pp** in every cell; more specifically, on the dexterous-hand tier explicit projection ([[2507.15597|Being-H0]]-style) wins the precision-critical cell, while on the parallel-jaw tier learned-gap co-training ([[2512.22414|π0.5-+-ego]]-style) wins the OOD-scene cell — a regime split by task-precision *and* platform tier, not a universal winner, the same structural finding [[Locomotion|Locomotion]]'s A6 already reports for MPC↔RL coupling. Falsifiable both ways: a single mechanism sweeping every cell by >10 pp refutes the regime-contingency commit just as cleanly as confirming it.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table — the axis is *where the hand→gripper gap is absorbed* (the bridging mechanism). Supervision regime (zero-robot-data / co-trained / paired) is a *second, crossing* factor, not a seventh mechanism — several rows share a mechanism but differ on this axis (H4 tests it explicitly). The last row is deliberately off-axis, kept as a contrast rather than a bake-off arm:

| System | Bridging mechanism | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2507.15597\|Being-H0]] | explicit projection — MANO parameters + GRQ-VAE part-level (wrist/finger) tokenization | **99.8–100%** valid generation, beats GR00T-N1.5 on MPJPE, **25%** teleop data matches 50–100%-data baselines | validated against from-scratch / GR00T-N1.5 baselines only — never against a co-training or mid-training arm on the same tasks |
| [[2602.09013\|VIDEOMANIP]] | explicit projection — 3D hand-object trajectory reconstruction + contact optimization, RGB-only | **63.75%** sim grasping (20 objects, IsaacGym), **62.86%** real (LEAP Hand, 7 tasks) | validated against retargeting baselines only; its RGB-only, no-hardware claim is untested against Being-H0's MANO-tokenized VLA on shared tasks |
| [[2606.17385\|EgoInfinity]] | explicit projection — SE(3)-equivariant cross-embodiment retargeting, web-scale data engine | **0.821** IK success / **2.86 cm** error on Unitree G1, direct cutting/pouring on a dual-arm Franka FR3 | a data engine, not itself a trained policy — produces retargeted demos for downstream training but is never compared to a learned-gap or mid-training arm trained on the same demos |
| [[2512.22414\|π0.5-+-ego]] | learned gap — treat humans as another embodiment, no kinematic alignment | Dresser **25%→50%**, egg-sorting **57%→78%**, scene generalization **32%→71%** | validated only against a robot-only, no-human-data baseline — never against Being-H0's explicit projection or EgoScale's mid-training on the same tasks |
| [[2606.02745\|SeeTraceAct]] | learned gap — visibility-aware latent plan (2D EE-trace decoder) over cross-embodiment demo videos, no kinematic alignment | **+4.4 pp** cross-embodiment ROBOCASA-DC precision tasks (**12.8%** vs. **8.4%**), real unseen-task SR **37.5%→50.0%** | a second learned-gap instance beside π0.5-+-ego — precision-sensitive by design (its own ρ = −0.63 correlation to target-interaction-ratio is the closest existing evidence a learned-gap mechanism *can* win a precision cell) — but never run against π0.5-+-ego or any explicit-projection mechanism |
| [[2602.16710\|EgoScale]] | mid-training alignment — two-stage: broad human pretrain → embodiment-aligned mid-train | **+54%** task SR on a 22-DoF hand, **88%** shirt-folding from a single demo, **+30%** cross-embodiment (7-DoF tri-finger) | the log-linear scaling curve is real, but the mid-training *stage*'s own contribution vs. the broad-pretraining stage's is never isolated against a single-stage learned-gap baseline at matched hours |
| [[2511.21690\|TraceGen]] | embodiment-agnostic intermediate representation — 3D trace-space world model | **80%** SR from 5 robot warm-up videos, **67.5%** human-to-robot transfer from 5 uncalibrated phone videos | a genuinely embodiment/camera-invariant interface, but benchmarked only against zero-shot and other trace-generation baselines — not against explicit-projection or learned-gap mechanisms on the same suite |
| [[2608.05706\|LAWM-3D]] | embodiment-agnostic intermediate representation — a *3D-aware* Latent Action Model (multi-view VAE + geometric alignment to a pretrained 3D foundation model, VGGT) learned from unlabeled multi-view human video, human-pretrain then robot-finetune | outperforms 2D pixel-space LAM baselines on PSNR/SSIM (Ego-Exo4D, Assembly101) and on WorldArena action fidelity + 3D accuracy; beats DreamDojo on all three OOD splits | the 3D generalization of TraceGen's trace-space idea — where TraceGen represents the invariant as explicit 3D *traces*, LAWM-3D learns it as a *latent* action space grounded to 3D structure, arguing 2D-pixel LAMs lose exactly the explicit-3D-motion/interaction semantics the gap needs; still validated on video-generation quality metrics, not benchmarked against the explicit-projection or learned-gap mechanisms on a shared downstream-SR suite |
| [[2608.04196\|SiMDex]] | embodiment-agnostic intermediate representation — wrist-local fingertip positions in a shared 42-dim action space, retrieved via a three-stage (recall/rank/re-rank) similarity search over tens of millions of egocentric video clips | overall SR **47.7%→61.1%** (**+13.4 pp**) over random-human-data co-training; matches baseline SR with **~4×** less robot data (25 h → 6 h); largest gain (**+17.2 pp**) at 0.5× robot data | adds a *data-curation* axis no other row carries — instead of designing the invariant representation harder, it uses a cheap morphology-agnostic proxy (fingertip geometry) purely to *retrieve* the task-relevant slice of a massive heterogeneous human-video pool before any alignment training happens; complementary to, not competing with, [[2507.15597|Being-H0]]'s explicit projection (same dexterous-hand tier) — the retrieval step could precede any of the six mechanisms, a dimension D1's bake-off does not yet control for, so it is a pre-filter on the data feeding a mechanism, not a seventh mechanism itself |
| [[2505.08787\|UniSkill]] | embodiment-agnostic intermediate representation — Inverse/Forward Skill Dynamics (motion, not embodiment) | beats GCBC/XSkill on real tabletop, kitchen, and LIBERO; **+15%** from Something-Something V2 scale | t-SNE confirms embodiment-agnostic clustering, but the representation is validated against weaker skill-imitation baselines, never against explicit-projection mechanisms |
| [[2505.11920\|H2R]] | visual embodiment-gap editing — HaMeR retargeting + SAM/LaMa inpaint + rendered robot overlay | **+1.3–10.2%** sim, **+3.3–23.3%** real SR across four benchmarks | a clean ablation of its *own* pipeline stages (retargeting vs. overlay), but never compared to Phantom's Hand-Inpaint variant or to a co-training baseline on the same tasks |
| [[2503.00779\|Phantom]] | visual embodiment-gap editing — Hand Inpaint (segment + inpaint + robot overlay), zero robot data | **92%** Pick/Place, 72–**88%** rope/brush, 72–**84%** OOD-scene sweeping | the sharpest zero-robot-data result in the table, but "Hand Inpaint beats Hand Mask" is the only head-to-head it runs — never against explicit-projection or mid-training mechanisms |
| [[2607.08857\|AgenticFocus]] | visual embodiment-gap editing — VLM-selected object restoration (SAM2 track + inpaint-and-reinsert) + full-hand camera-relative MuJoCo IK retargeting, mixed-reality compositing | wrist SPARC **-5.18** vs Masquerade's **-5.56** / Do-as-I-Do's **-6.05** (smoother trajectories); **7–14%** smoothness gain over those two baselines | a fourth Hand-Inpaint-family instance beside [[2505.11920\|H2R]] and [[2503.00779\|Phantom]] — its head-to-head against Masquerade and Do-as-I-Do is the closest thing in the table to a same-mechanism-family bake-off, but still never crosses into explicit-projection or learned-gap mechanisms, and reports smoothness/tracking metrics rather than downstream task SR |
| [[2502.16587\|Human2Robot]] | generative video transfer — paired human-robot conditional video generation | **95%** basic-task SR (vs. XSkill **53%**, Diffusion Policy **28%**), **100/80/70/80%** one-shot generalization axes | requires a *paired* human-robot dataset (2,600 synchronized episodes), unlike every unpaired mechanism in this table — a supervision-cost axis the bake-off must track separately, not fold into the six |
| [[2505.20795\|Generalizable-Robot-Policy-Human]] | generative video transfer — cross-prediction video generation + ProtoDiffusion Contrastive Policy, sparse robot data | **0.79** positional / **0.73** background generalization; **0.47** SR on an entirely new skill (vs. **0.00** baselines) | a second generative-video-transfer instance beside Human2Robot — *unpaired* human video (no synchronized H&R dataset), the opposite supervision regime — but never run against Human2Robot on the same tasks, so the table still can't tell whether paired or unpaired supervision is load-bearing for this mechanism |
| [[2503.07135\|VidBot]] | explicit projection — coarse-to-fine 3D affordance + diffusion trajectory reconstruction, zero robot data | **88.2%** sim (13 tasks), **80.0%** real (Stretch 3 + Spot, 55 trials) | matches Phantom's zero-robot-data regime via a purely geometric (non-diffusion-video) reconstruction route — never benchmarked against Phantom's visual-editing route on shared tasks |
| [[2503.23877\|ZeroMimic]] | explicit projection — affordance grasping + wrist-trajectory action-chunking policy, zero robot data | **71.0%** real, **73.8%** sim, generalizes to unseen embodiments | a third zero-robot-data point beside Phantom and VidBot, using relative (not absolute) action representations — the table's three zero-robot-data rows have never been run against each other, let alone against the co-trained mechanisms |
| [[2602.22461\|EgoAVFlow]] | active vision (off-axis by construction) — shared 3D flow driving viewpoint control | **1.8–2.5×** SR over EgoZero, up to **4×** over Human Viewpoint Imitation | answers "where should the robot look," not "where is the kinematic gap absorbed" — deliberately excluded as a bake-off arm; H5 tests it as an add-on to whichever mechanism wins, not a competing mechanism |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (bridging-mechanism choice is regime-contingent, and no single mechanism dominates every cell).
1. **H1 — No single mechanism wins by more than a pre-registered margin across both regime cells.**
   - *Prediction*: sweeping the six mechanisms at matched egocentric-hours and a shared backbone on the precision × OOD-scene × platform-tier grid, no mechanism's mean SR margin over the second-best exceeds a pre-registered **10 pp** in every cell.
   - *Test*: run the harness on both platform tiers; report a mechanism × regime × tier SR matrix with per-cell winners.
   - *Row*: Being-H0 (explicit) / π0.5-+-ego (learned gap) / EgoScale (mid-training) / TraceGen (embodiment-agnostic rep) / Phantom (visual gap editing) / Human2Robot (generative video transfer).
   - *Falsifier*: one mechanism wins every cell by >10 pp → a universal-best mechanism exists, refuting the regime-contingency commit (a clean, publishable result either way).
2. **H2 — Explicit projection wins the dexterous-tier precision cell; learned-gap co-training wins the parallel-jaw OOD cell.**
   - *Prediction*: on the dexterous-hand tier's precision-critical tasks (insertion, tool-use — where MANO/finger-level fidelity matters), explicit-projection mechanisms beat learned-gap co-training by a pre-registered margin; on the parallel-jaw tier's OOD-scene/object generalization, learned-gap beats explicit projection by a comparable margin.
   - *Test*: report per-mechanism SR split by platform tier, task-precision tier, and in-distribution vs. novel-scene/object.
   - *Row*: Being-H0 (explicit, dexterous) / VIDEOMANIP (explicit, dexterous) vs. π0.5-+-ego (learned gap, parallel-jaw/mobile).
   - *Falsifier*: either mechanism wins *both* cells → precision and generalization are not the axis separating explicit projection from learned gap, and the regime split must be redrawn on a different axis (e.g. dexterity-DoF).
