3. **H3 — Matched egocentric-hours shrinks mechanism advantages currently confounded with data volume.**
   - *Prediction*: EgoScale's and π0.5-+-ego's own data-dominates-architecture claim predicts that once egocentric-pretraining hours are held equal across mechanisms, the SR spread between them shrinks to within H1's pre-registered margin; an unequal-hours replication of the same sweep should show a *wider* spread, confirming today's reported mechanism "wins" are partly hours artifacts.
   - *Test*: run the sweep twice — once with hours matched, once reproducing each paper's own (unequal) hour budget — and compare spread.
   - *Row*: EgoScale (names the data-dominates-architecture claim) / π0.5-+-ego (emergent-transfer framing, validated only against no-human-data baselines).
   - *Falsifier*: the matched-hours spread is not smaller than the unequal-hours spread → mechanism choice matters independent of data volume, and the field's "data dominates" consensus is the one that's wrong.
4. **H4 — Zero-robot-data supervision pays a bounded precision tax relative to co-trained supervision, at matched mechanism.**
   - *Prediction*: supervision regime (zero-robot-data / co-trained / paired) crosses the mechanism axis rather than sitting inside it — VidBot and ZeroMimic are both explicit-projection, zero-robot-data instances of the *same* mechanism family as the co-trained Being-H0 — so holding the mechanism fixed and varying only supervision regime, the zero-robot-data instances trail the co-trained instance by a *bounded* margin on in-distribution precision tasks, quantifying the "zero-robot-data tax" rather than treating zero-shot-from-human-video as free.
   - *Test*: within the explicit-projection arm, report SR of the zero-robot-data rows (VidBot, ZeroMimic, Phantom) vs. the co-trained row (Being-H0) on matched in-distribution precision tasks; bound the tax.
   - *Row*: VidBot / ZeroMimic / Phantom vs. Being-H0 (same mechanism family, different supervision regime).
   - *Falsifier*: the zero-robot-data tax is unbounded (near-total precision collapse) → the "no robot data needed" framing is not viable for precision-critical regimes, a boundary condition worth stating plainly.
5. **H5 — Active vision is a genuinely orthogonal axis, not a bridging-mechanism arm.**
   - *Prediction*: adding EgoAVFlow-style active-vision viewpoint control on top of whichever mechanism wins each H1 cell improves SR further (a positive, roughly additive effect), rather than substituting for the bridging mechanism.
   - *Test*: take the H1 winner in each cell, add an EgoAVFlow-style active-vision head, and measure the incremental SR gain over the mechanism alone.
   - *Row*: EgoAVFlow (active vision, off-axis contrast row).
   - *Falsifier*: active vision alone (paired with a trivial/no bridging mechanism) matches the best bridging mechanism → active vision is doing the kinematic-bridging work too, and the two axes are not actually separable.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **Re-implementing six mechanisms faithfully is a large engineering surface, and a weak arm fakes regime-contingency** — an under-tuned mechanism loses cells for implementation reasons, not architectural ones. → Reproduce each mechanism against its own paper's reported number on its own platform first (Being-H0's 99.8–100% valid generation, π0.5-+-ego's 78% egg-sorting, Phantom's 92% Pick/Place) as an arm-validity gate before the cross-mechanism grid.
> - **Collapsing to a single parallel-jaw platform silently strips the finger-level mechanism H2 predicts wins** — Being-H0's contribution *is* part-level wrist+finger MANO/GRQ-VAE tokenization, and VIDEOMANIP is a LEAP Hand; on a parallel jaw there is nothing for either mechanism to tokenize, so a single-platform harness would test a degenerate wrist-pose-only version of explicit projection, not the mechanism itself. → Run two platform tiers — parallel-jaw ([[2503.00779|Phantom]]/[[2505.11920|H2R]]/[[2511.21690|TraceGen]]/[[2503.23877|ZeroMimic]]'s shared Franka/xArm family) and a dexterous hand ([[2507.15597|Being-H0]]/[[2602.09013|VIDEOMANIP]]/[[2602.16710|EgoScale]]'s family) — and scope H2's precision-cell claim to the dexterous tier explicitly; treat cross-tier comparisons as out-of-scope, not bake-off data.
> - **Three of the six arms (learned gap, mid-training, generative video transfer) are thin on independent instances** — mid-training is EgoScale alone; learned gap and generative video transfer each gained a second instance in this table ([[2606.02745|SeeTraceAct]], [[2505.20795|Generalizable-Robot-Policy-Human]]), but a single losing instance still can't separate "the mechanism lost" from "that paper's instantiation lost." → Report H1's per-cell margins only for arms with ≥2 verified instances; flag EgoScale-only mid-training results as implementation-confounded until a second mid-training instance is added.
> - **The "data dominates architecture" consensus could survive even under matched hours** if hours interact non-linearly with mechanism choice, collapsing H1's grid to an hours artifact regardless of control. → H3 is the explicit test of this; report the matched-vs-unequal-hours spread comparison as the load-bearing evidence, not just point SR numbers.
> - **Precision-critical tasks need a shared, non-gameable metric across mechanisms whose action outputs differ in format** (MANO joint angles vs. Cartesian deltas vs. pixel-generated actions) — an apples-to-oranges precision comparison would fake H2's cell split. → Standardize on end-effector Cartesian tracking error regardless of each mechanism's internal action representation, following H2R's own cross-representation normalization approach.

### D2 — Objective-Coupling vs. Data-Volume Attribution in Egocentric + Video-WAM Co-Training

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | D — Egocentric & Human-Video Pretraining |
| **Thesis** | Some generalist VLAs train a video-WAM prediction objective jointly with the action objective, one coupled loop, rather than factoring egocentric pretraining into two separate stages; others reach a comparably strong recipe on the same class of egocentric video with no WAM objective at all, refusing the coupling entirely. Neither side has separated whether the joint loop's gain, where it exists, comes from the coupling itself or simply from the extra data tokens riding along with it. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.27621\|Robot-Learning-from-Human-Videos-Survey]] (survey), [[2604.15483\|π0.7]] (method), [[2604.20100\|JoyAI-RA]] (method), [[2603.16666\|Fast-WAM]] (method), [[2506.21539\|WorldVLA]] (method), [[2607.08436\|EgoWAM]] (method), [[2606.20521\|HumanScale]] (method), [[2605.00078\|Being-H0.7]] (method) |
| **Key targets** | [[2603.16666\|Fast-WAM]] video-co-training ablation: RoboTwin **91.8%→83.8%** (the existing attribution attempt H1 refines); [[2606.20521\|HumanScale]]'s matched-5,000-hr protocol: **20%** lower action loss, **90.0%** OOD-object SR (the harness precedent, a different question); [[2506.21539\|WorldVLA]]'s own action-conditioning ablation: LIBERO **62.8%→67.2%** (the H2 architecture-family + H3 action-conditioning discriminator); [[2606.03188\|GeoSem-WAM]]'s multi-signal stacking ablation: **+0.61%/+0.51%→+1.02%** combined, **0.10 pp** below the two solo gains' sum (the H4 sub-additive-vs-synergistic discriminator); [[2605.00078\|Being-H0.7]] latent-target WAM: **99.2%** LIBERO at **3–4 ms/step** (the H5 pixel-vs-latent discriminator); the bet's own pre-registered **≥5 pp** objective-coupling-beats-data-volume margin |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: [[2604.15483|π0.7]] pretrains on egocentric human video *and* a video-WAM subgoal-image objective as parallel signals on overlapping corpora and reports the recipe works, but never isolates *why*: is the gain the extra training signal the WAM objective supplies, or just the extra data tokens riding along with it? [[2604.20100|JoyAI-RA]] sharpens the question from the other side: it pretrains on the same class of egocentric video with *no* WAM objective in its architecture at all, and still reports a strong recipe — evidence the field has not even agreed a WAM objective is necessary. Notably, that team's *own next version*, [[2608.05674|JoyAI-RA 0.5]], now reverses course and adds a WAM back in (a LAC-WM), reporting **+3.1 pts** on unseen scenes from the WM module's presence alone, plus a further **+4.8 pts** on seen-task score from *how* that WM is conditioned (latent-action-conditioned vs a standard WM, both present) — the field disagreeing with itself release-to-release is exactly the unresolved-attribution symptom D2 targets, not incidental churn.
- **Today's answers**: [[2603.16666|Fast-WAM]] comes closest — ablating video co-training drops RoboTwin from **91.8%** to **83.8%** — but the ablation removes the objective and its tokens at the same time, so the drop could be either cause. The same confound shows up outside Fast-WAM's own lineage, on completely different architectures: [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]] (discrete-token autoregressive, Chameleon backbone) reports its own joint-vs-action-only ablation (LIBERO **62.8%→67.2%**) with the same data-and-objective conflation, and [[2503.00200|UVA]] (Transformer with decoupled video/action diffusion heads) reports the same qualitative pattern without even quantifying the gap. [[2606.03188|GeoSem-WAM]] stacks a *second* and *third* future-prediction signal (geometry, semantic) onto Fast-WAM itself and finds the combined gain (**+1.02%**) is actually **0.10 pp below** the sum of the two solo gains (**+0.61%+0.51%=1.12%**) — mildly sub-additive, not synergistic — but never asks whether that (redundant, not additive) pattern survives a volume-matched control either. [[2607.08436|EgoWAM]] isolates a different axis (which WM *target* — pixel, DINO, or 3D-flow — helps most) but never asks single-objective-vs-joint at matched data. [[2606.20521|HumanScale]] runs the controlled-compute protocol D2 needs, but for a different question — egocentric video vs. robot video as a pretraining *source*, not objective-coupling vs. data-volume as a pretraining *mechanism*.
- **The opening**: [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]]'s own Fig. 7 comparison sharpens *why* coupling might work: an action-*conditioned* world-model objective ($o'|o,a$) beats an action-*unconditioned* video-prediction objective ($o'|o$) at boosting the action model — a mechanistic claim, not just an existence claim, that no one has quantified at matched compute. [[2605.00078|Being-H0.7]] and [[2412.04445|Moto]] show the WAM's future-prediction objective doesn't need to be pixel-space at all — two independent latent targets (dual-branch embeddings; discretized motion tokens) each match or beat pixel-WAM baselines (**99.2%** LIBERO at **3–4 ms/step**; **52.5%** CALVIN at **1%** labeled data) — meaning the synergy, if it exists, lives in *predicting the future* as an objective, not in the video modality specifically. Together these are the wedges D2's harness needs: swap the WAM's target, swap whether it conditions on action, swap how many signals it stacks, hold everything else fixed, and read off which mechanism is actually carrying the gain.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: A video-WAM's future-prediction objective and an egocentric-VLA's action-prediction objective are two different loss functions computed over overlapping footage of the *same* underlying process — how the visible world changes under a hand's actions. Coupling them in one joint optimization, rather than cascading pretraining into a video-prediction stage and a separate action-prediction stage, should let each objective's gradient inform the other's representation — a synergy a data-volume-matched single objective cannot reach by adding capacity alone. [[2603.16666|Fast-WAM]]'s own ablation is the closest existing evidence: removing the video-co-training objective (robot data held fixed) drops RoboTwin from **91.8%** to **83.8%** — proof the objective itself, not just its data, is carrying something. [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]] sharpens *why*: its own ablation finds an action-*conditioned* future-prediction objective beats an action-*unconditioned* one at boosting the action model, because conditioning on the action forces the shared representation to resolve action-causality rather than merely track pixel motion — a mechanistic argument for *action-conditioning*, not future-prediction in general, as the lever.
- **Assumption being challenged**: [[2604.15483|π0.7]]'s own framing — that diversified context conditioning "enables a robotic foundation model to effectively learn from mixed-quality and suboptimal data without compromising high-performance objectives" — treats non-degradation as sufficient evidence the WAM-objective's subgoal-image signal is pulling its weight; it never asks whether the same token budget, spent doubling a *single* objective's data with no WAM component at all, would have gotten equally far. [[2604.20100|JoyAI-RA]] is the closest thing to that missing control in the wild: a cross-lab recipe that pretrains on the same class of egocentric video with no video-WAM objective anywhere in its architecture, and still reaches a strong recipe — undercutting, not confirming, the "the two objectives together are what's carrying it" reading. [[2603.16666|Fast-WAM]]'s ablation is the sharpest attempt on record within one architecture, but it removes the objective and its tokens together, so it cannot separate "the objective helped" from "the data helped" either. The same under-attribution repeats outside the egocentric-pretraining lineage entirely: [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]] and [[2503.00200|UVA]] each report their own joint-objective-beats-action-only ablation on two different architecture families and neither runs a volume-matched control — the field keeps re-discovering the same unablated correlation, not confirming causation.
- **The bet**: At matched total compute (mirroring [[2606.20521|HumanScale]]'s controlled 5,000-hr protocol), joint egocentric-VLA + video-WAM co-training at $N$ hours beats a single-objective egocentric-VLA baseline trained on $2N$ hours (the data-volume control) by **≥5 pp** downstream SR on held-out tasks — meaning the gain is attributable to objective-coupling, not data volume alone. And the coupling's active ingredient is action-conditioning specifically, not future-prediction in general: an action-conditioned world-model objective beats an action-unconditioned video-prediction objective at matched data and architecture, replicating [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]]'s own **62.8%→67.2%** finding — quantified at matched compute, not left as an unablated existence claim.

**Related research papers.** One comparison table — the axis is *what future-prediction target the co-training design uses, and what (if anything) the paper's own ablation isolates*:

| System | Future-prediction target (co-training design) | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| [[2312.13139\|GR-1]] | pixel-space, joint [OBS]/[ACT] tokens, **800k** Ego4D clips | **94.9%** CALVIN (vs. HULC **88.9%**), **77.8%** at 10% robot data | the lineage root proving joint video+action beats action-only, but Ego4D-pretrain-then-CALVIN-finetune never isolates whether the gain is the video objective or the extra 800k-clip token budget |
| [[2410.06158\|GR-2]] | pixel-space, cVAE action head, **38M** clips | **97.7%** multi-task tabletop, **79.0%** industrial bin-picking (vs. GR-1's **35.9%**) | scales GR-1's joint objective ~50× in clips; the jump from GR-1 could be objective refinement (cVAE) or pure data scale — never separated |
| [[2603.16666\|Fast-WAM]] | pixel-space, decoupled train-time co-training / test-time single-pass | removing video co-training drops RoboTwin **91.8%→83.8%**; **190 ms** inference, **4×** faster than imagine-then-execute | the sharpest existing attribution attempt, but it ablates the *robot*-video objective (not egocentric specifically) and removes the objective's tokens along with the objective itself — the exact confound H1 resolves |
| [[2606.03188\|GeoSem-WAM]] | pixel-space RGB + geometry (DPT depth) + semantic dense-prediction heads, all supervised on video latent tokens, discarded at test time (Fast-WAM lineage) | **98.55%** LIBERO / **92.52%** RoboTwin 2.0 (beats Fast-WAM baselines); real Franka **88.9%→95.4%**; per-signal ablation: geometry **+0.61%**, semantic **+0.51%** (sum **1.12%**), combined **+1.02%** — **0.10 pp below** the sum, mildly sub-additive | stacks a *second* and *third* future-prediction signal onto Fast-WAM and finds the combined gain falls slightly *short* of the sum of the parts, not above it — H4's discriminator, evidence against synergy rather than for additivity — but the per-signal ablation adds signals without a volume-matched control, and the **98.55%** LIBERO ceiling leaves little room to resolve a **0.10 pp** gap from noise |
| [[2506.21539\|WorldVLA]] | discrete-token autoregressive (Chameleon backbone); own controlled comparison of an action-*conditioned* world-model objective ($o'\|o,a$) vs. an action-*unconditioned* video-prediction objective ($o'\|o$) | joint WM objective lifts LIBERO **62.8%→67.2%** (**81.8%** full model); the action-conditioned objective is reported "more beneficial" than the unconditioned one (qualitative, Fig. 7) | a second, independent objective-vs-data confound on a completely different architecture family — H2's replication target — and its own action-conditioning comparison is H3's discriminator, but reported as direction, not a matched-compute margin |
| [[2503.00200\|UVA]] | Transformer joint latent + decoupled video/action diffusion heads, video head bypassable at inference | **0.90** [[2306.03310\|LIBERO]]10 (vs. π0's **0.85**), **0.88** PushT-M (+**20 pp**); own "UVA-action" (no video generation) ablation reports reduced performance | a *third* architecture family (dual-diffusion-heads) replicating the same qualitative "joint beats action-only" finding — but the ablation reports direction only, not a magnitude, so it cannot yet anchor H2's matched-compute margin, only the existence claim |
| [[2604.15483\|π0.7]] | pixel-space (WM-generated multi-view subgoal images) + egocentric action-prior, under Knowledge Insulation | matches task-specific fine-tunes out-of-the-box; "the two objectives compose cleanly, neither degrades the other" | an existence claim (co-training doesn't hurt), never an ablation against a data-matched single-objective baseline — the unablated consensus D2 challenges directly |
| [[2604.20100\|JoyAI-RA]] | multi-source (EgoLive + sim + real) under a unified camera-frame action space, **no video-WAM objective in the architecture at all** | **90.48%/89.28%** RoboTwin 2.0 Easy/Hard, **0.74** real AgiBot (vs. π0.5's **0.62**) | the sharpest evidence *against* D2's premise that a WAM objective is doing the work — egocentric video used purely as data, no parallel prediction objective, still reaches a strong recipe, so the WAM-objective's marginal contribution (if any) is exactly what the bet must isolate |
| [[2608.05674\|JoyAI-RA 0.5]] | the *same lineage's next version* — reverses 0.1's no-WAM stance and adds a frozen Latent-Action-Conditioned World Model (LAC-WM, dual implicit/explicit action alignment) between the human-video pretraining stage and the VLA stage | two distinct internal ablations, easy to conflate: (a) WM-module-vs-none — **+3.1 pts** on unseen scenes, the actual reversal-of-0.1 test; (b) latent-*action-conditioned* WM vs a *standard* (non-latent-conditioned) WM, both present — **92.1 vs 87.3 = +4.8 pts** seen-task; scaling human ego-video 10%→100% (53,000 h) lifts unseen-task score **56.9→72.4** monotonically | a natural experiment inside one team's own roadmap: 0.1 argued a WAM objective is unnecessary, 0.5 now reports a genuine WM-vs-none ablation (**+3.1 pts**, unseen only) — the real third attribution attempt for H1, smaller than the card's earlier reading — plus a separate conditioning-choice ablation (**+4.8 pts**, seen) that is a second, independent H3 discriminator (does conditioning the WM on action matter) beside [[2506.21539\|WorldVLA]]'s Fig. 7; neither runs D2's compute-matched $N$-vs-$2N$ control, so both strengthen rather than settle the objective-coupling case |
| [[2602.15922\|DreamZero]] | pixel-space, 14B autoregressive diffusion jointly predicting video+actions | **42%** relative cross-embodiment gain from 10–20 min video-only demos, **62.2%** seen-task progress in unseen envs | a joint-objective existence proof at scale reusing egocentric corpora, but no data-matched single-objective control — the gain could be model capacity as much as objective coupling |
| [[2606.30534\|Orca]] | pixel/language-space, "unconscious" dense-video + "conscious" language-conditioned learning, *zero* action labels in pretraining | **32.4** action-generation score (vs. V-JEPA-2.1's **17.0**) with a frozen backbone | the cleanest existence proof that video-only pretraining (no action objective at all) transfers to action generation — a stronger, different claim than "joint co-training beats either alone," the extreme contrast H1 must not conflate with its own bet |
| [[2607.08436\|EgoWAM]] | HPT-backbone controlled sweep of WM *target* (Pixel VAE / DINO / 3D motion flow) | DINO gives **4×** OOD generalization, 3D-flow gives **20–30%** in-domain gains | the closest existing methodology to D2's harness (fixed backbone, one axis varied), but sweeps the target representation, never data-volume vs. objective-coupling — D2 borrows the harness and adds the missing volume-matched control arm |
| [[2606.20521\|HumanScale]] | matched-5,000-hr egocentric-vs-robot WAM pretraining, controlled architecture/compute/protocol | **20%** lower action loss on unseen tasks, **90.0%** OOD-object SR (vs. **0%** baseline collapse) | answers which pretraining *source* is better (egocentric beats robot video) — a different, already-settled question from D2's, which pretraining *mechanism* (objective-coupling vs. data-volume) is better; D2 must not re-derive this finding and mistake it for its own |
| [[2412.04445\|Moto]] | latent motion tokens (unsupervised, bridging-language pretraining on unlabeled video, no pixel reconstruction) | **61.4%** SIMPLER (vs. **48.0%** no-motion-token baseline); **52.5%** CALVIN at **1%** labeled data (vs. **0%** baseline) | a *third* latent-target instance beside Being-H0.7 — predicts discretized motion tokens as a bridging representation rather than a full future-state embedding, and like the rest of the table never isolates coupling from the token-budget confound |
| [[2605.00078\|Being-H0.7]] | latent dual-branch (no pixel target at all), privileged future embeddings, training-time only | **99.2%** LIBERO, **62.1%** RoboCasa, **3–4 ms/step** inference | the sharpest evidence that the co-training synergy doesn't need pixel-space video prediction at all — directly feeds H5's discriminator (does swapping pixel target for latent target preserve most of the gain?) |

**Hypotheses & tests.** Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis of the FP bet (the co-training gain is objective-coupling, not data volume, and the coupling's active ingredient is action-conditioning, not future-prediction in general).
1. **H1 — Objective-coupling beats data-volume at matched compute.**
   - *Prediction*: (a) joint egocentric-VLA + video-WAM co-training at $N$ hours — which spends roughly double the per-step compute of a single objective, since it scores two losses per token — beats an egocentric-VLA-only baseline trained on $2N$ hours (the *actually* compute-matched single-objective arm) by **≥5 pp** downstream SR on held-out tasks. (b) Re-running [[2603.16666|Fast-WAM]]'s own RoboTwin ablation with the same volume-matched control (replacing the removed video-objective's compute with *more* robot-action-only training steps on the same tokens) recovers less than half of its reported **91.8%→83.8%** (**8.0 pp**) drop — most of that drop is the objective, not the tokens it carried.
   - *Test*: (a) a HumanScale-style 3-arm comparison — single-objective-$N$ (unmatched floor), single-objective-$2N$ (the compute-matched control), joint-$N$ (the bet) — on the same corpus, backbone, and eval protocol; report gradient steps and total tokens processed per arm so "matched compute" is auditable. (b) reproduce Fast-WAM's ablation with the added volume-matched control arm, report the recovered-gap percentage.
   - *Row*: HumanScale (matched-scale protocol) / Fast-WAM (the objective-ablation this refines and directly re-runs).
   - *Falsifier*: single-objective-$2N$ ties or beats joint-$N$ → the co-training gain π0.7 reports was compute, not objective coupling, consistent with JoyAI-RA's no-WAM recipe already reaching strong SR on data alone; *or* the volume-matched control recovers most (>50%) of Fast-WAM's 8.0 pp drop → Fast-WAM's own attribution already isolated the objective cleanly and the confound was smaller than assumed.
2. **H2 — The matched-compute coupling margin is a property of the *mechanism*, not of Fast-WAM's tokenizer.**
   - *Prediction*: the qualitative "joint beats action-only" finding [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]] (discrete-token autoregressive) and [[2503.00200|UVA]] (dual-diffusion-head transformer) each report independently replicates *at matched compute* — H1's margin (not just the confounded qualitative direction) holds, within some tolerance, across all three architecture families, evidence the effect lives in the objective-coupling idea itself rather than in Fast-WAM's specific pixel-diffusion tokenizer.
   - *Test*: re-run H1's 3-arm matched-compute protocol on WorldVLA's and UVA's own architectures and tokenizers, holding corpus and eval fixed across all three; report the margin per architecture family.
   - *Row*: WorldVLA (discrete-token autoregressive replication) / UVA (dual-diffusion-head replication) / Fast-WAM (the pixel-diffusion original).
   - *Falsifier*: the margin collapses to near-0 on WorldVLA and/or UVA even though it holds on Fast-WAM → Fast-WAM's margin (if H1 confirms it) is an artifact of that one tokenizer, not a general objective-coupling effect.
3. **H3 — The coupling's active ingredient is action-conditioning, not future-prediction per se.**
   - *Prediction*: an action-*conditioned* world-model objective ($o'\|o,a$) beats an action-*unconditioned* video-prediction objective ($o'\|o$) at matched data, architecture, and compute — replicating [[2506.21539|WorldVLA]]'s own qualitative Fig. 7 finding, and independently, [[2608.05674|JoyAI-RA 0.5]]'s **+4.8 pt** latent-action-conditioned-vs-standard-WM result, as a quantified margin rather than two separate unablated claims.
   - *Test*: on one backbone, train three arms — action-only, +action-conditioned WM objective, +action-unconditioned video-prediction objective — at matched compute; compare downstream SR.
   - *Row*: WorldVLA (Fig. 7's qualitative action-conditioning comparison) / JoyAI-RA 0.5 (the independent +4.8 pt conditioning-choice ablation) / Orca (the opposite extreme — zero action labels in pretraining, still transfers — the boundary case H3 must not conflate with its own claim).
   - *Falsifier*: the unconditioned video-prediction arm ties or beats the action-conditioned arm → future-prediction alone, not action-conditioning specifically, carries the synergy, contradicting both WorldVLA's and JoyAI-RA 0.5's qualitative claims.
4. **H4 — Stacking multiple coupled future-prediction objectives does not synergize; it is additive at best and redundant at worst.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.03188|GeoSem-WAM]]'s own per-signal ablation (geometry **+0.61%**, semantic **+0.51%**, solo sum **1.12%**, but combined only **+1.02%** — **0.10 pp** *below* the sum) generalizes — adding a third independent future-prediction signal to a co-trained WAM yields a combined gain at or below the sum of each signal's solo contribution, never meaningfully super-additive, evidence the mechanism is closer to "more independent, partially-overlapping supervision" than a single shared-representation synergy.
   - *Test*: reproduce GeoSem-WAM's geometry/semantic ablation on a **non-saturated substrate** — RoboTwin 2.0 (**92.52%** baseline, not LIBERO's **98.55%**-ceiling setting the original ablation used) or the real-Franka setting (**88.9%→95.4%**) — with H1's volume-matched control applied to *each* added signal (not just the video-vs-none contrast), then add a third independent signal; report whether the combined gain stays at-or-below the solo sum once token budget is controlled, whether the **0.10 pp** redundancy GeoSem-WAM measured on its ceiling-bound LIBERO result widens or narrows with a third signal, and episode counts with confidence intervals per arm so a sub-**0.2 pp** margin is never read as a real effect.
   - *Row*: GeoSem-WAM (the multi-signal stacking ablation) / Fast-WAM (the single-signal baseline this extends).
   - *Falsifier*: the combined gain clears the solo sum by a real (non-noise-level, >0.2 pp) margin on the non-saturated substrate once volume-matched → multiple coupled objectives *do* synergize through a shared representation, contradicting GeoSem-WAM's own two-signal result.
5. **H5 — Latent future-prediction (no pixel generation) recovers most of the co-training gain.**
   - *Prediction*: replacing the video-WAM's pixel-space future objective with a latent target — [[2605.00078|Being-H0.7]]-style dual-branch embeddings or [[2412.04445|Moto]]-style discretized motion tokens — recovers **≥80%** of the full pixel-WAM co-training's downstream SR gain.
   - *Test*: swap the video-WAM's target from pixels to each latent target on the same corpus/backbone; compare downstream SR to the pixel-WAM arm and the no-future-objective baseline.
   - *Row*: Being-H0.7 (latent dual-branch target) / Moto (latent motion-token target, a second independent latent-target instance) / DreamZero (pixel target, the full-fidelity anchor).
   - *Falsifier*: the latent-target arms recover <50% of the gain → pixel-level video prediction carries real information latent prediction discards.

> [!warning] Risks
> - **A volume-matched control for Fast-WAM's ablation isn't a perfectly clean control** — even matching token *count*, the extra robot-only steps use tokens differently than the removed objective did. → Report H1's recovered-gap fraction with confidence intervals, and treat a partial recovery as evidence *both* mechanisms contribute rather than forcing a binary verdict.
> - **WorldVLA and UVA differ from Fast-WAM in far more than "architecture family"** — different backbones, different data scale, different benchmarks (LIBERO vs. RoboTwin vs. PushT/Libero10) — so a failure to replicate the matched-compute margin in H2 could reflect any of these confounds, not architecture alone. → Re-run H2 on a *single* shared corpus and benchmark across all three architecture families, holding data and eval fixed, the way EgoWAM already controls for backbone when sweeping WM targets.
> - **WorldVLA's own action-conditioning result (Fig. 7) is reported qualitatively, with no volume-matched control of its own** — the same confound H1 flags for Fast-WAM could apply here too. → Treat WorldVLA's and JoyAI-RA 0.5's conditioning claims as priors to replicate under H1's own volume-matched harness, not settled results.
> - **GeoSem-WAM's own two-signal result was measured on LIBERO at 98.55% — near ceiling, where a ~0.1 pp gap is plausibly episode noise rather than a real redundancy-vs-synergy effect.** H4's Test above already routes around this (RoboTwin 2.0 / real-Franka substrate, >0.2 pp threshold, CIs required) rather than re-running the ceiling-bound LIBERO comparison, but the risk stands as the reason for that redesign — if the mitigation slips and H4 gets re-run on LIBERO by mistake, the falsifier reverts to unresolvable-from-noise. → Enforce the non-saturated-substrate requirement as a precondition for reporting an H4 verdict, not an optional refinement.
> - **Latent (Being-H0.7, Moto) and pixel (DreamZero) WAM targets aren't apples-to-apples architectures** — a Mixture-of-Transformers dual-branch, a discrete motion-token bridge, and a full video-diffusion backbone — so H5 risks confounding "latent vs. pixel" with "architecture family." WorldVLA's own target looked like a third case at first read (its shared vocabulary is VQ-discrete *image* tokens), but it isn't: WorldVLA's world model predicts $o_t$ — the next full image frame, tokenized through a VQ-GAN codebook (8192 entries) and reconstructable back to a 256×256/512×512 RGB frame via the VQ-GAN decoder. That is discretized pixel-space by the same "requires pixel reconstruction" criterion that puts GR-1/GR-2/Fast-WAM/DreamZero on H5's pixel side — unlike Being-H0.7's dual-branch and Moto's motion tokens, which the Related-table rows above describe explicitly as carrying **"no pixel target at all"** / **"no pixel reconstruction."** So WorldVLA correctly sits only in H2's Row (discrete-token-autoregressive vs. diffusion architecture axis) and correctly does not appear in H5's Row — the two axes are orthogonal on this paper, not confounded; H2 asks *how the tokens are generated* (discrete vs. continuous), H5 asks *what they represent* (pixel vs. privileged latent), and WorldVLA is discrete-and-pixel, not a straddle. → Implement each H5 target on the *same* backbone (swap only the prediction head/target, not the whole model), the way EgoWAM already does for its own 3-way target sweep, so the real remaining risk — Being-H0.7/Moto/DreamZero not sharing a backbone — doesn't get papered over by a false WorldVLA caveat.

---

## Cluster E — Data Generation & Demonstration Scaling: Where the Training Corpus Comes From

*The scaling wall every direction above runs into is demonstration cost, and the field's answer is to synthesize demonstrations from a small human seed. This cluster asks what that synthesis actually produces.*

### E1 — Where Does the Human's Strategy Monopoly End? Crossing Quality-Diversity Search with Demonstration Generation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Cluster** | E — Data Generation & Demonstration Scaling |
| **Thesis** | Two lineages have each solved half of robot data scaling and have never met. Quality-Diversity search *authors* genuinely new manipulation strategies — [[2604.22551\|QDTraj]]'s MAP-Elites archive finds trajectories its authors say a human demonstrator would not produce, including opening a door without touching the handle — but stops at an archive queried at runtime, never distilled into a policy. The demonstration-generation family ([[2310.17596\|MimicGen]] → [[2502.20382\|PhysicsGen]]) *does* produce policy-ready corpora at 250×, but every member multiplies the *placements* of a human-seeded strategy and by its own admission cannot leave that seed's neighborhood. So the field's operating assumption — that humans are the irreplaceable source of manipulation strategy — has been falsified in principle and never tested in the loop that matters. The bet is in First-principles below. |
| **Anchor papers** | [[2604.22551\|QDTraj]] (method, the strategy-authoring existence proof — and the archive that is never distilled), [[2410.02319\|QDGset]] (method, QD at corpus scale — 62M grasps, 40k objects — with policy learning deferred to "future work"), [[2310.17596\|MimicGen]] (method, the seed-anchored lineage's origin, whose Appendix S ran the placement arm and left the strategy arm unrun), [[2502.20382\|PhysicsGen]] (method, the closest the lineage gets to authoring — contact-schedule *adaptation*, framed by its own authors as local refinement of human global guidance), [[2605.21710\|PGDG]] (method, diversity search that is anchored and mode-preserving by construction), [[2410.24185\|DexMimicGen]] (method, its own PCA is the strategy-collapse evidence), [[2606.20871\|Geometric Entropy]] (benchmark, formalizes the strategy/placement split *and* supplies the inverted-U this direction must answer), [[2402.14606\|D3IL]] (benchmark, the human-diversity comparator), [[2406.02523\|RoboCasa]] (benchmark) |
| **Key targets** | **The boundary number, which does not exist** (H1): human-archive and QD-archive coverage fractions plus their intersection, on one task under one behavior descriptor — [[2604.22551\|QDTraj]] asserts it beats human demonstrators (hinge **27→2,944**, slider **532→3,988**) without running the comparison. **The distillation target** (H1): beat the seed-anchored pipeline where it is weakest, [[2310.17596\|MimicGen]] Square D2 **49.3%** / Three Pc. Assembly D2 **13.3%** — a step no one has taken, since [[2410.02319\|QDGset]] ships **62M** grasps on **40,353** objects and defers policy learning to "future work." **The interim results if H1 fails**: the allocation law (H2) and the asymptote (H4), against the per-unit deficit already on record — [[2406.02523\|RoboCasa]] Generated-100 **26.3%** below Human-50 **28.8%** at 2× count, overtaking only at 60× |

**Why it matters.**
- **The gap**: the generation family's headline is a multiplier — [[2310.17596|MimicGen]] turns ~200 human demos into 50K (**250×**), [[2502.16932|DemoGen]] one demo into hundreds at **0.010 s** each — and it has already reorganized how teams spend money. But a multiplier counts trajectories without asking which *factor* it multiplied. Every generator samples placements conditioned on a strategy it received and never modifies, so one axis grows while the other stays pinned at the seed count, and the field's two reported numbers (generation rate, downstream SR) are blind to the distinction — MimicGen itself notes the first does not predict the second (Gear Assembly D1: **8.2%** rate, **76.0%** SR).
- **Today's answers**: every generator preserves the demonstrated strategy by construction, and three of them prove it in their own papers. [[2502.16932|DemoGen]]'s hand command is *mathematically invariant* — `â^hand_t = a^hand_t, ∀t`, for every generated sample. [[2410.24185|DexMimicGen]]'s own PCA finds end-effector action coverage expands while "for finger joint actions, DexMimicGen primarily performs local interpolation rather than broad expansion." [[2310.17596|MimicGen]]'s Gear Assembly D1 drew **over 850 of 1000** generated episodes from just **3** source demos despite uniform sampling. [[2410.18907|SkillMimicGen]] and [[2510.18316|MoMaGen]] re-plan only free-space, base, and camera motion and replay the contact segment untouched.
- **The opening**: the decisive experiment is half-run, by the founding paper itself. [[2310.17596|MimicGen]]'s Appendix S re-collected the seed on the *wider* D2 distribution and found performance "invariant to how much the task **initializations** of the source demonstrations vary" — read by the field as *source data is saturated*. But initializations are placement, exactly what the transform already manufactures, so that null is what the factorization predicts. The strategy arm has never been run, and MimicGen concedes it: performance "could improve with more source human demos **if they are curated in an intelligent manner**, but this is left for future work." Every corpus the bet needs is public. The measurement side is open but not empty, and its shape is the gap: [[2606.20871|Geometric Entropy]] separates a near-identical-descent corpus (**2.901**) from a wide-approach-cone one (**3.667**) but is defined on *transit* and explicitly excludes fine manipulation, so it is blind to the contact segment every generator freezes; [[2603.11634|FAKTUAL]]'s whole-path entropy covers contact but conflates it with transit, so it cannot attribute a change to a factor. Neither has been pointed at a generated corpus. Measuring contact-strategy diversity in isolation is not a harder version of an existing measurement — it is an unbuilt one.

**First-principles framing.**
- **First principle**: *demonstration is the wrong primitive for acquiring strategy, and the field adopted it because teleoperation is purchasable, not because it is how strategy is found.* A demonstration transfers **what was done**; a strategy is discovered by **acting** — no infant learns to grasp by watching a thousand teleoperated demonstrations, and the one mechanism nature uses to populate a behavioral repertoire is search under selection, which is precisely what Quality-Diversity implements. The obvious alternative — mutual-information skill discovery (DIAYN, DADS, LSD) — is ruled out empirically rather than by preference: [[2410.04855|AutoTaskSkillDiscovery]] shows those methods *fail on manipulation*, with DADS collapsing to "either ignoring the object or moving it along a fixed path," which is why the skill-discovery lineage never produced a manipulation demo generator and QD is the viable substrate. The factorization is that claim's mathematical shadow: a corpus factorizes as $p(\tau) = p(g)\,p(\tau \mid g)$ — *strategies* $g$ (which approach, which grasp, which contact schedule) times *placements* given a strategy. A transform-based generator samples the second factor **only**: an SE(3) transform moves where a behavior happens and leaves the behavior untouched, so $|\text{supp}\,p(g)|$ can never exceed the seed's, whatever the multiplier. Three consequences follow with no experiment. Saturation is *predicted*, not observed. The seed sets the curve's **asymptote rather than its slope** — more generated demos approach a ceiling fixed by seed strategy count, which is a sharper and more testable claim than "returns diminish." And a fixed operator budget has an optimal split, since the factors are not substitutes: only humans currently produce new $g$, while machines produce $p(\tau\mid g)$ at **0.010 s** each ([[2502.16932|DemoGen]]). The stakes stop being academic because the factorization does not stop at generated data: [[2310.17596|MimicGen]]'s opening applies this same argument to *human* corpora (RT-1, BC-Z, Open-X) to license automating placement — so if it holds both ways, the field's headline trajectory counts are denominated in the factor that saturates, and the currency becomes operator-years. Out of scope to test here; named as the extrapolation H2 earns. And the bet runs against fashion: evolutionary search is the unglamorous branch the field walked away from when scaling teleoperation fleets became fundable.
- **Assumption being challenged**: that the human demonstrator is the irreplaceable source of *strategy*. Every member of the generation family builds this in as architecture, not hypothesis: [[2502.20382|PhysicsGen]] states that "human demonstrations can provide **global guidance**, while trajectory optimization can **locally refine** these demonstrations," and its multimodality is inherited — "behaviors **observed in the original human demonstrations**." [[2605.21710|PGDG]] concedes it "is **not designed to discover entirely new successful modes**" and that a narrow source demo means the corpus "may inherit this bias and miss alternative strategies." Quality-Diversity falsifies the premise directly — [[2604.22551|QDTraj]] reports trajectories that "would likely **not be discovered by a human demonstrator**" and "richer diversity than human teleoperation demonstrations" — but never closes the loop to a policy, so the falsification has had no effect on how corpora are built. Two weaker assumptions fall with it. First, that source data is saturated — MimicGen's two nulls both varied *placement*, and it names the factor it meant: "whether human operator time would be better spent collecting data **in new regions of the workspace**" (H2 tests the strategy arm, and states where that reading is contestable). Second, the equivalence claim. [[2310.17596|MimicGen]] reports that 200 generated demos (from 10 human) match 200 human demos across five task variants and concludes the two are substitutable; [[2410.18907|SkillMimicGen]] markets "100× Data Amplification" on the same logic. The claim is true on the spatial axis and untested on the strategy axis, and the one place a *count-controlled* comparison against genuine human data exists, the equivalence fails: on [[2406.02523|RoboCasa]]'s 24 atomic tasks, **Generated-100** — twice the demonstration count — scores **26.3%** against **Human-50**'s **28.8%**, and generated data only overtakes human data at **Generated-3000**, sixty times the count (**47.6%**). Generated demonstrations are not worthless; they are worth *less per unit*, which is exactly what a fixed strategy set and a growing placement set would predict. [[2607.13154|WANDA]] shows the same shape where the counts are far apart: **1,360** generated demos reach **16.67** where **200** official demos reach **62.22**.
- **The bet**: *the boundary is measurable, and the machine's side of it is larger than the field assumes.* On a shared task with one behavior descriptor (approach direction, grasp pose, contact schedule), a QD archive covers a **strictly larger** fraction of the viable strategy space than a 10-demo human seed, and the human's exclusive residue is small enough to name — a number nobody has, because [[2604.22551|QDTraj]] asserts the comparison without running it. Second, the distillation claim that makes it matter: an IL policy trained on that archive, count-matched against a seed-anchored corpus, wins on the broad reset distributions where the seed-anchored pipeline is weakest. Crucially the bet is about **coverage of the evaluated distribution, not raw diversity** — [[2606.20871|Geometric Entropy]] finds an inverted-U in which added strategy diversity *hurts* past a task-dependent peak, and is "approximately monotonically decreasing" for a pretrained π0.5, so the prediction is explicitly that archive-distilled data wins where the evaluation demands strategies the seed lacks and *loses* where it does not. That regime split is the falsifiable content; a uniform win would be as suspicious as a uniform loss. *Interim result:* even if distillation fails, the allocation law below stands on its own — at a matched human-operator budget of 10 source demos and a matched generation budget of 1000, a **strategy-curated** seed (distinct approach directions and grasp types, not distinct placements) beats a **placement-curated** seed of equal size by **≥15 pp** on the broad distributions where the pipeline is weakest (Square D2 **49.3%**, Three Pc. Assembly D2 **13.3%**, image-based), *and* raises the demo-count curve's **asymptote** rather than its early slope — the two arms should separate at 1000+ generated demos, not at 200. The comparison is strategy-arm-vs-placement-arm, both re-run live, because Appendix S shows the placement arm is not reliably equal to the narrow default. The double prediction that makes it falsifiable: the strategy arm must clear the placement arm by ≥15 pp *and* the placement arm must reproduce Appendix S's null on Square. The supporting measurement claim is *negative* and sharper for it: no published instrument scores the strategy factor on generated data. [[2606.20871|Geometric Entropy]] normalizes in exactly the group MimicGen's transform acts in, so its delta on transformed segments is analytically zero, not empirically null (H3); [[2603.11634|FAKTUAL]] covers contact but conflates it with transit. Building an instrument that isolates $p(g)$ is a deliverable here, not an inherited tool — which is why the allocation law is specified on downstream SR, testable *before* that instrument exists. If it holds, the operative advice inverts: human time buys strategies, machine time buys placements.

**Related research papers.**

Twenty-two generators, curation methods, and instruments on the axis that decides the bet — *what the transform varies, and what it holds fixed*. The pattern is uniform: every row varies placement, and every row holds the demonstrated behavior fixed.

| System | What the transform varies | What it holds fixed | Key result | What's missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[2604.22551\|QDTraj]] | **the strategy itself** — MAP-Elites over a 6-DoF grasp-frame genotype | nothing; no human seed ("generated automatically without priors") | hinge **27→2,944**, slider **532→3,988**; finds behavior it says a human would not, e.g. opening a door *without the handle* | the existence proof that machines author strategy, and the direction's central gap: reports **archive counts, never policy SR**, runs **no human-archive baseline** despite claiming to beat human demonstrators, and is queried at runtime rather than distilled |
| [[2410.04855\|AutoTaskSkillDiscovery]] | task *and* skill, via asymmetric self-play + automatic task generation | nothing; unsupervised | **0.83/1.00/0.52/1.00/0.98/0.98** on six unseen tasks vs skill-discovery baselines mostly under **0.20**; sim-to-real **0.83** over 103 trials | the reason this direction picks QD over mutual-information skill discovery: it documents DIAYN/DADS/LSD *failing* on manipulation (DADS "collapses to either ignoring the object or moving it along a fixed path"), so the obvious alternative substrate is excluded on evidence — but its own skills live inside an HRL orchestrator, never a demonstration corpus |
| [[2410.02319\|QDGset]] | grasp pose, via QD search transferred across object geometries | nothing; QD-native, no demonstration input | **62M** grasps on **40,353** objects (**3.5×** ACRONYM's grasps, **4.5×** its objects); transfer succeeds for **78%** of 1,304 reference objects | QD at corpus scale, proving the search is not toy — but static 6-DoF poses rather than trajectories, and it defers the load-bearing step verbatim: "leveraging QDGset for learning generalizing grasping policies for real robotics is considered for **future work**" |
| [[2310.17596\|MimicGen]] | object pose per subtask, via one constant SE(3) transform + linear interpolation | the entire contact segment, replayed open-loop | **250×** (200 human → 50K over 18 tasks); Square **11.3%→90.7%** | the seed-anchored origin, and the source of its own counter-evidence: Gear Assembly D1 drew **850 of 1000** episodes from **3** source demos, and Appendix R measures support coverage at **43.5%** (TPA D1) vs **98.8%** (Coffee D1) |
| [[2410.18907\|SkillMimicGen]] | free-space transit motion, replaced by RRT-Connect motion planning | the skill (contact) segments, transformed in the object frame and replayed | generation rate **40.7%→75.4%** avg over MimicGen; Coffee Prep D2 **0.0%→59.9%** | fixes MimicGen's collision-blind interpolation — a *connective-motion* fix that leaves the strategy axis untouched by design; with conventional teleop instead of HITL-TAMP source data its policy advantage nearly vanishes (**59.3%** generation rate, 73.8% vs MimicGen's 70.0%) |
| [[2410.24185\|DexMimicGen]] | per-arm subtask timing via a parallel/coordination/sequential taxonomy | **finger joint actions, replayed verbatim from the source demo** | **21K** demos from 60 source; Drawer Cleanup **0.7%→80.0%**, Can Sorting **0.7%→97.3%**; real GR-1 **90%** vs **0%** | its own PCA is the sharpest strategy-collapse evidence: end-effector coverage "significantly expands" while finger actions get "local interpolation rather than broad expansion" — the dexterity is not generated; SR "does not always increase" 1000→5000 |
| [[2502.16932\|DemoGen]] | object position in the point cloud, and the re-planned motion segments between skills | **the hand command, mathematically: `â^hand_t = a^hand_t, ∀t`** | 1 demo → 100–200; real 8-task avg **11.0%→74.6%**; **0.010 s**/trajectory vs MimicGen's 2.1 min | the cleanest proof of the first principle — the strategy is a literal constant of the generation map; carries a dedicated *performance-saturation* subsection, attributed to visual mismatch rather than strategy collapse (its 25-demo comparator is scripted-policy, not human) |
| [[2510.18316\|MoMaGen]] | robot base pose and head-camera pose, under reachability + visibility constraints | the manipulation contact segments | 1 demo → 1000; **63%** D0 generation rate where SkillMimicGen/DexMimicGen score **0%** on D1/D2 | extends the varied set to the base — a *placement* axis again; its failure breakdown is infrastructure (simulator instability **~35%** of failures), never behavioral variety |
| [[2502.20382\|PhysicsGen]] | joint-level actions, re-optimized under true contact dynamics | the demonstrated strategy's neighborhood (its authors' framing: humans give "global guidance," optimization "locally refines") | vs kinematic replay on identical demos and embodiment: **51% → 86%**; hardware zero-shot **26% → 74%** | the lineage's closest approach to authoring — it does change the contact schedule (one arm → both) — but a single local optimum per sample, no archive and no diversity objective: adaptation, not search |
| [[2607.13154\|WANDA]] | the whole scene, via reconstruction into render-only worlds + whole-body RRT-Connect chaining | contact segments, replayed object-relative | 1 demo → 1,360; real 5-task mobile-manip **54.8%** avg progress where 1 real demo gives **0**; **6.4×** cheaper than MoMaGen | publishes the family's starkest deficit while advocating generation: **1,360** generated → **16.67** vs the benchmark's **200** official → **62.22**, with a sublinear curve (3.6→9.28→16.67%) |
| [[2606.20871\|Geometric Entropy]] | — (measures) | — | separates **2.901** (r=0) from **3.667** (r=0.10) and **2.997** (single-mode); converges in **50–100** demos; inverted-U vs SR, monotonically *decreasing* for fine-tuned π0.5 | the instrument H3 characterizes rather than applies — its sim corpora are planner-generated, its ARX-arm corpora human (H2's reference point), and it scores *transit* only, so it cannot see the contact segment every generator freezes |
| [[2606.24078\|MinInter]] | source-demo *selection* — picks the demo needing least interpolation | everything else in the MimicGen pipeline | generation rate **+12.8%** avg across all 26 MimicGen variants (Hammer Cleanup D0 **0.476→0.977**); policy **+4.8%** | the clearest case of the field optimizing the acceptance rate as the objective — a **12.8**-point generation-rate gain buys **4.8** points of policy, and selecting the *least-interpolation* source demo is precisely the move that should narrow strategy coverage; nobody measured whether it did |
| [[2605.21710\|PGDG]] | contact schedule and risky states, via physics-grounded sampling + DPP curation + CEM relabeling | the source demo's mode of solving the task | MimicGen-style baseline → PGDG: RotateBox-Pitch **0.38→0.93**; real zero-shot **35%→82%** | states the first principle as its own limitation — generation is local to the source demo and "not designed to discover entirely new successful modes" — and independently finds larger datasets are not better "**without dataset-level curation**" |
| [[2606.25939\|DeformGen]] | the *state* distribution, via localized forces + forward simulation onto the physical manifold | the single source trajectory, warped by a KNN deformation field | 1 demo → **37–59%** SR vs **1–3%** unaugmented; 100→750 lifts ACT **19.50%→61.50%** | the scaling curve the asymptote claim must survive — monotone on averages but sharply *decelerating* (**+32.0**, **+7.2**, **+2.8**) and per-task non-monotone (ACT-Toy peaks at N=250); sim-only, 3 tasks, and its deformable-generation baseline is its own reimplementation |
| [[2606.19586\|1001DEMOS]] | camera viewpoint and obstacle configuration, via fisheye-3DGS re-rendering + trajectory optimization | contact dynamics — augmentation happens strictly *before* the detected contact event | 50 UMI episodes → **7,245** free-space + **5,000** obstacle-aware; real obstacle test **5% → 100%** | augments only pre-contact by explicit design, the most literal statement of the family's fixed point; its own ablation finds SR **plateaus at a 50° cone**, an independent inverted-U |
| [[2603.11634\|FAKTUAL]] | — (measures + curates) | — | whole-path signature-kernel entropy; curated subset **95%** on RoboMimic Can vs **86%** random; entropy–SR **r = 0.74–1.00** | the second diversity instrument, and the one that does *not* exclude contact — but scores the path undifferentiated, so it cannot say which factor shrank |
| [[2506.19121\|CUPID]] | — (curates existing demos) | — | influence-function curation lifts real SR **~38%** avg; TuckBox **100% failure → 84–88%** by removing demos that induce a brittle strategy | the closest evidence that *which* demos you keep changes which strategy a policy adopts — a $p(g)$ intervention in all but name; but it curates a training set post-hoc, never a seed fed to a generator |
| [[2607.06442\|SIEVE]] | — (curates existing demos) | — | structure-aware selection reaches **56.3%** on Bridge-V2 with **50%** of the data and steps, beating **51.8%** on the full set at 2× the budget | fixed-budget selection that explicitly allocates across *primitives and transitions* — structurally the nearest published analogue to strategy-aware seed curation, but applied to a large pre-existing corpus for training efficiency, not to the handful of demos a generator will amplify |
| [[2606.02577\|RoboDream]] | the whole observation, via a video world model painting scenes onto rendered robot-only motion | the teleoperated motion itself — the operator's trajectory is the conditioning signal | real Franka Real-50 **36.3** → Gen-Mix **62.5**; prop-free teleop **2.2×** faster at **32.5** | replaces the *simulator* rather than the transform, so its fixed point is the motion, not the contact segment — and it saturates too, at ≈**Mix-200**, attributed to retrieval diversity and domain gap |
| [[2402.14606\|D3IL]] | — (measures) | — | IL benchmark built for *diverse human* demonstrations, with an explicit Behavior Entropy metric over multi-modal solution strategies | the human-side comparator H1's coverage test needs — it scores whether a *policy* reproduces the diversity present in human demos, but never asks how much of the strategy space those human demos covered in the first place, which is the quantity the boundary number requires |
| [[2406.02523\|RoboCasa]] | — (measures) | — | the only public count-controlled human-vs-generated comparison on identical tasks: **Human-50 28.8%**, **Gen-100 26.3%**, **Gen-300 35.0%**, **Gen-3000 47.6%** (24 atomic tasks) | cuts both ways — generated *loses* at 2× count and *wins* at 60×, which is the per-unit exchange rate, not a refutation; but its human side is a single 50-demo point, so no human scaling curve exists to fit, so no human scaling curve exists to fit |
| [[2607.21588\|AXIS]] | — (infrastructure) | — | 207 tasks, versioned **25/50/100%** snapshots on a fixed held-out protocol; π0.5 **83.9** (vanilla) →84.7→85.7→**88.8** on LIBERO-Plus, "not yet saturated" on the Overall aggregate | snapshot infrastructure built to benchmark *policies* on data, not *generators*; its non-saturating curve is the counter-case the asymptote claim must beat — though the aggregate hides per-axis regression (Light **−1.7**, Language **−1.3** below vanilla at 100%) |

**Hypotheses & tests.** H1 is the primary claim — the boundary between machine-searchable and human-only strategy, and the number that decides whether the human seed is architecture or habit. H2–H7 are the interim results that hold even if H1 fails, plus the measurement work that makes it interpretable. Each item is a falsifiable sub-hypothesis, with the experiment and the Related-table row it lands on.
1. **H1 — The human's exclusive strategy residue is small and measurable, and a QD archive distilled into a policy beats a seed-anchored corpus exactly where the seed's strategies run out.**
   - *Prediction, part A (coverage)*: on one task with a fixed behavior descriptor (approach direction, grasp pose, contact schedule), a QD archive covers a strictly larger fraction of the viable strategy space than a 10-demo human seed, and the human-exclusive residue is **≤20%** of discovered strategies — the number [[2604.22551|QDTraj]] asserts without measuring. *Part B (distillation)*: an IL policy trained on that archive, count-matched against a seed-anchored [[2310.17596|MimicGen]] corpus, wins by **≥15 pp** on broad reset distributions (Square D2 **49.3%**, Three Pc. Assembly D2 **13.3%**) and *loses or ties* on the narrow D0 where the seed's single strategy suffices — a regime split, not a uniform win.
   - *Test*: run MAP-Elites on a MimicGen task with QDTraj's genotype (6-DoF grasp frame) to build the machine archive; build the human archive from N teleoperated demos scored on the same descriptor; report coverage, intersection, and each side's exclusive cells. Then distil both into IL policies at matched trajectory count and evaluate on D0/D1/D2. The residue cells are the deliverable — they name what teleoperation is still *for*.
   - *Row*: QDTraj (the archive that is never distilled, and the unmeasured human comparison) / QDGset (QD at corpus scale, policy learning deferred) / MimicGen (the seed-anchored comparator) / Geometric Entropy (the descriptor and the inverted-U constraint) / D3IL (the human-diversity comparator).
   - *Falsifier*: the human archive contains a large exclusive residue (>40%), **or** the distilled policy fails to beat the seed-anchored corpus anywhere including the broad distributions → the human seed is doing irreplaceable strategic work, the two lineages should stay apart, and the allocation law (H2) is the correct ceiling for this direction.
2. **H2 — Strategy-curated source demos beat placement-curated ones at matched operator and generation budget.**
   - *Prediction*: three 10-demo seeds — (a) standard narrow-D0, (b) placement-curated (Appendix S's D2 arm), (c) strategy-curated (distinct approach directions and grasp types at *fixed* D0 placements, so the manipulation is only on $p(g)$; **not** subtask orderings, which MimicGen's fixed-subtask-sequence assumption cannot parse) — each generating 1000 demos through the unmodified pipeline. Arm (c) beats (b) by **≥15 pp** on D2 (Square **49.3%**, Three Pc. Assembly **13.3%**); arm (b) reproduces Appendix S's null against (a) on Square.
   - *Test*: MimicGen's code and splits are public and Appendix S specifies (a)-vs-(b), so only arm (c) is new — collect it by instructing one operator to vary *method* at fixed placement, the operational inverse of Appendix S. Report all arms at **both** modalities (Appendix S is low-dim; the targets are image-based) and report generation rate alongside, since (c) may generate *worse* while training *better*. Include a **manipulation check**: confirm (c)'s seed spans more approach directions than (b)'s via [[2606.20871|Geometric Entropy]] on the *seeds*, where the metric is uncontested.
   - *Row*: MimicGen (both nulls, and the "curated in an intelligent manner" hedge) / CUPID (removes demos inducing a brittle strategy — a $p(g)$ intervention on a *training* set, never a seed) / SIEVE (fixed-budget allocation across primitives, the nearest published analogue) / MinInter (pre-empts the experimental *shape* — vary seed usage at fixed count, measure downstream SR after generation — but not the strategy-vs-placement contrast, since it re-selects among demos already collected) / PhysicsGen (the existence proof that re-solving contact yields behavior the transform cannot, **51%→86%**).
   - *Falsifier*: arm (c) matches arm (a) within 5 pp on D2 → strategy diversity in the seed is genuinely redundant, source data really is saturated, and the field's collect-few-generate-many playbook is correct as practiced.

3. **H3 — The existing diversity instrument cannot answer the strategy question on generated data, and its own construction says why.**
   - *Why this replaces a naive null*: `H_G` normalizes by endpoint anchoring, **target-frame rotation**, and displacement scaling — precisely the group MimicGen's object-centric transform acts in. A transformed segment therefore maps to the *same* descriptor as its source **analytically**, so predicting a null there is predicting a tautology. Meanwhile `H_G` scores *transit*, and MimicGen's transit is largely the linear interpolation prefix — geometry freshly set by the sampled *placement*. The instrument is blind where the thesis lives and sensitive where it does not.
   - *Prediction*: decomposing `H_G` into transformed-segment and interpolation-prefix contributions, the transformed component matches the source to **≤0.05** nats (the analytic prediction) while essentially all measured variation, including any apparent "diversity gain," rides on the prefixes and scales with reset-distribution width (D0 < D1 < D2), not with strategy.
   - *Test*: on the public MimicGen corpora, split each generated trajectory at the known transform boundaries, compute `H_G` over each part, and regress against reset width. Report the naive whole-transit `H_G` alongside, to quantify how far an off-the-shelf application would mislead.
   - *Row*: MimicGen (the interpolation prefix, Appendix N.2, and the transform whose invariance the metric shares) / DexMimicGen (same scheme) / Geometric Entropy (the instrument being characterized rather than applied).
   - *Falsifier*: the transformed component's delta materially exceeds the analytic tolerance, **or** interpolation-prefix `H_G` is flat in reset width → the metric is measuring something other than what its normalization implies, and it may be usable on generated data after all, which would make the instrument gap this direction names a non-problem.
4. **H4 — The diversity delta, not the demo count, predicts where the scaling curve flattens.**
   - *Prediction*: ranking tasks by the **decomposed** strategy-side delta from H3 (the transformed-segment component, with the placement-driven interpolation contribution removed) — not the naive whole-transit delta, which H3 predicts is dominated by placement — the tasks with the smallest delta saturate earliest in a demo-count sweep, with rank correlation ρ ≥ 0.6 between delta and saturation point — reframing [[2410.24185|DexMimicGen]]'s 1000→5000 null and [[2606.02577|RoboDream]]'s saturation at ≈Mix-200 as strategy-axis saturation rather than a volume ceiling.
   - *Test*: per task, sweep training-set size over the released generated corpus, locate the knee of the SR curve, and regress the knee against the measured `H_G` delta. Include [[2606.25939|DeformGen]]'s 100→750 curve as the discriminating case: it is monotone on averages but decelerating (**+32.0**, **+7.2**, **+2.8**) and per-task non-monotone, so H4 predicts its delta is *larger* than the rigid-transform generators' — a non-rigid registration field does author new shape — and that its knee simply sits further right. If its delta is small *and* the deceleration is no steeper than the rigid generators', H4 fails.
   - *Row*: DexMimicGen (1000→5000 null) / DeformGen (the decelerating-but-monotone counter-case) / AXIS (the "not yet saturated" counter-case on differently-provenanced data).
   - *Falsifier*: saturation point is uncorrelated with the delta (|ρ| < 0.3), or [[2606.25939|DeformGen]] shows a small delta and no saturation → volume, not strategy diversity, governs the knee.
5. **H5 — Acceptance-rate optimization narrows strategy coverage.**
   - *Prediction*: [[2606.24078|MinInter]]'s least-interpolation source-demo selection, which raises generation rate **+12.8%** for only **+4.8%** policy SR, *lowers* `H_G` of the resulting corpus relative to random source selection on the same task — because selecting the source demo nearest the target configuration systematically discards the more distant approach modes.
   - *Test*: run MimicGen generation twice on identical tasks and seeds, once with random source-segment selection and once with MinInter's selection rule; compare `H_G`, generation rate, and policy SR on a held-out reset distribution stratified by distance from the source configuration.
   - *Row*: MinInter (the interpolation-minimizing selection rule) / MimicGen (the standard-pipeline baseline — note its source-segment selection is nearest-neighbour on several tasks including Square, not uniformly random, so the selection rule must be held fixed across arms).
   - *Falsifier*: MinInter's corpus matches or exceeds random-selection `H_G` → acceptance optimization is diversity-neutral and the field's objective is not costing coverage.
6. **H6 — The generated-vs-real deficit at scale is a strategy-axis deficit, not a fidelity one.**
   - *Prediction*: on a task where a large generated corpus underperforms a much smaller real one — [[2607.13154|WANDA]]'s **1,360 → 16.67** vs the benchmark's **200** official demos → **62.22** is the reference case — mixing in a small number of *strategy-diverse* real demos (chosen to maximize marginal `H_G`) recovers more of the gap per added demo than the same number chosen at random or chosen for visual fidelity.
   - *Test*: hold the generated corpus fixed; add k real demos selected by three rules (max-marginal-`H_G`, random, max-visual-fidelity) at matched k; compare SR recovery per demo added.
   - *Row*: WANDA (the deficit) / Geometric Entropy (the selection rule) / 1001DEMOS (the fidelity-axis alternative, whose own 50°-cone plateau is a fidelity-side ceiling).
   - *Falsifier*: max-`H_G` selection performs no better than random → the deficit is not strategy-shaped, and fidelity or another axis explains WANDA's result.
7. **H7 — Machines can author new strategies, not just new placements — which is what removes the human from the binding constraint.**
   - *Prediction*: past the knee identified in H4, generating N additional demos with a *perturbed contact segment* (multiple approach modes for the same subtask, in the spirit of [[2410.18907|SkillMimicGen]]'s initiation augmentation but applied to the contact phase rather than its start pose) beats N additional standard-transform demos by ≥5 pp SR at matched count — while standard-transform demos add ≤1 pp.
   - *Test*: at the knee, fork generation into standard-transform and contact-perturbed arms at matched count and compute budget; evaluate on a held-out reset distribution. [[2502.20382|PhysicsGen]]'s trajectory optimization is the natural implementation of the perturbed arm, since it re-solves rather than replays the contact.
   - *Row*: PhysicsGen (**51%→86%** head-to-head, the existence proof that re-solving contact beats replaying it) / SkillMimicGen (initiation augmentation, the same move one phase earlier) / PGDG ("not designed to discover entirely new successful modes").
   - *Falsifier*: contact-perturbed demos match standard-transform demos past the knee → strategy diversity is not the binding constraint and the saturation has another cause. This is the hypothesis that decides whether the allocation law is a *permanent* division of labour or a temporary one: if $p(g)$ is machine-searchable — by trajectory optimization ([[2502.20382|PhysicsGen]]), RL exploration, or contact-mode enumeration — then the correct long-run move is not to spend human time better but to redirect compute from placement-multiplication to strategy-search, and H2's allocation law becomes the transitional result rather than the destination.
> [!warning] Risks
> - **More strategy diversity is not automatically better, and the field's own metric says so.** [[2606.20871\|Geometric Entropy]] finds an inverted-U — added trajectory diversity helps only to a task-dependent peak, then hurts — and for a *pretrained* π0.5 the curve is "approximately monotonically decreasing," with two of three real-robot tasks declining monotonically. A QD archive maximizes coverage by construction, so distilling one naively is a bet on the wrong side of that curve. → H1's prediction is deliberately a *regime split* (archive-distilled data wins only where the evaluation demands strategies the seed lacks, and ties or loses on narrow D0), and the strong-prior case is the sharpest test: if the effect survives on a pretrained VLA it is real, and if it vanishes there the direction is scoped to from-scratch policies, which must be stated rather than discovered at review.
> - **The instrument is blind to the axis the thesis is about.** `H_G` scores free-space transit and explicitly excludes fine manipulation, while the thesis concerns the frozen *contact* segment — so for motion-planner generators ([[2410.18907\|SkillMimicGen]], [[2502.16932\|DemoGen]], [[2510.18316\|MoMaGen]], [[2607.13154\|WANDA]]) `H_G` would score planner variety and miss the point entirely. → H3 is scoped to the two linear-interpolation generators where transit is pinned by the transform; no published instrument *isolates* the contact-strategy axis — [[2603.11634|FAKTUAL]]'s whole-path entropy includes it but cannot separate it from transit — which is a gap this direction names rather than closes, and building the isolating instrument is the honest follow-on.
> - **`H_G` was built to audit human datasets and carries an inverted-U, not a "more is better" law** — its own results show fine-tuned π0.5 *monotonically decreasing* in `H_G`, so a high-diversity corpus is not automatically a better one. → The bet is deliberately about the source→generated *delta* and its link to saturation, never about absolute diversity being good; H6 is framed as recovering a *deficit* relative to real data and H7 as beating count-scaling past the knee — neither as maximizing an entropy.
> - **MimicGen injects Gaussian action noise (σ = 0.05) during generation**, which moves a generated corpus's `H_G` for reasons unrelated to strategy authorship — and Geometric Entropy's own (r,k)=(0,0) baseline of **2.901** is non-zero precisely because it absorbs environment randomization and execution noise. → Report `H_G` for a noise-ablated generation run alongside the standard one; if the noise term alone accounts for the delta, H3's null is uninterpretable and must be re-run with noise disabled.
> - **`H_G` requires task-semantic phase boundaries** (gripper open/close events) to isolate transit segments, and the released corpora may not expose them uniformly across systems. → Verify recoverability per corpus before committing to the metric; where boundaries are unavailable, fall back to a stated alternative estimator and report which corpora were dropped rather than silently reweighting.
> - **[[2607.13154\|WANDA]]'s BEHAVIOR deficit and [[2310.17596\|MimicGen]]'s coverage-bias appendix are different generators on different benchmarks** — they converge on the same story but are not the same measurement, and fusing them would overstate the evidence. → Cite them as independent corroboration, and let H6 test the WANDA case on its own terms rather than importing MimicGen's numbers into it.
> - **Acceptance rate does not predict policy quality** ([[2310.17596\|MimicGen]] Gear Assembly D1: **8.2%** generation rate, **76.0%** policy SR; [[2410.18907\|SkillMimicGen]] states there is no strict correlation). → No hypothesis here is of the form "low acceptance → worse policy"; H5 instead predicts acceptance *optimization* costs coverage, which is a different and still-open claim.
> - **[[2606.25939\|DeformGen]]'s monotone 100→750 curve and [[2607.21588\|AXIS]]'s "not yet saturated" snapshots are live counter-cases** to a universal saturation claim. → H4 makes DeformGen the discriminating test rather than an exception to explain away; if its diversity delta is small and it still does not saturate, H4 fails honestly.

---

## Cross-Cutting Themes

> [!tip] Latent-Space Prediction Is the Default Substrate
> A1 and A2 share one substrate decision: supervise on video/pixel at training, predict in latent at deployment. A1's joint loop is tractable only because latent rollout is ~10 ms vs pixel ~150 ms; A2's latent CoT keeps step-reward supervision latency-free. The same substrate choice generalizes past fixed-base manipulation into navigation: [[2603.29165|LatentPilot]]'s in-policy Pilot Token beats external pixel-space world models on [[2004.02857|R2R-CE]] at **130 ms / 22.8 GB**, and [[2506.23468|NavMorph]]'s feature-level RSSM is the navigation analog of [[2411.04983|DINO-WM]]'s manipulation latent planner — foresight is a representation choice across mobility *and* manipulation, and external-world-model framing is a pixel-space legacy in both. [[2603.16666|Fast-WAM]] and [[2511.08544|LeJEPA]] are the shared anchors — the latent must be both cheap and non-collapsing. D2 asks the same question one stage upstream, at pretraining: [[2605.00078|Being-H0.7]]'s latent dual-branch WAM shows the co-training synergy survives dropping pixel-space video generation entirely, at **3–4 ms/step** — evidence the substrate argument A1/A2 make for deployment-time latency also holds for the *pretraining objective's* representation.

> [!tip] Step-Level Verifiable Rewards Beat Outcome-Only Signals
> A2, A3, and B2 all reject the sufficiency of terminal reward. A2 applies [[2604.22074|CIR/SR-Reasoning]]'s "outcome rewards don't guarantee causal reasoning" to latent tokens; A3 turns physical laws into per-step verifiable predicates over action sequences; B2 turns each successful recovery into a verified training example. The shared move — swap a single sparse outcome signal for a dense stream of locally-checkable predicates — is the most actionable result the three share.

> [!tip] Detection-Diagnosis-Recovery as a Unified Stack
> The integrated loop now exists ([[2604.18791|HELM]]), so B2 sharpens it toward the two faces it skips — cross-episode recurrence recognition and cause-attributed recovery — while A1, A2, and B1 each supply one face it can borrow. A1's failure-finder adversary surfaces the perturbations that should trigger detection; A2's CoT-faithfulness probe is a diagnosis instrument; B1's joint causal metric certifies recovery actually closed the loop. [[2602.04411|Self-evolving-Embodied-AI]]'s 5-module framework is the formalization all four point at, and [[2606.22449|Self-Evolving-Cognitive-Framework]] sharpens the L3-Evolver target into a *causal* one — a structural evolution operator Φ that continually revises an internal causal world model under intervention, the why-not-just-predict layer B2's recurrence-keyed recovery and B3's continual updates both reach for.

> [!tip] Efficiency and Safety Are Deployment-Blocking, Not Optional
> A1, B1, and B2 all assume real-time operation, so [[2505.04769|VLA-Concepts-Survey]]'s **3–5 Hz** AR ceiling (vs the 20–50 Hz a contact-rich loop needs) is a precondition every one of them runs inside, not around — B1's joint causal-consistency metric has to be cheap enough to compute per-step, and B2's memory+recovery loop has to fit the same control-rate budget. Safety cuts the same way: [[2605.02900|Safety-in-Embodied-AI-Survey]]'s five-layer attack taxonomy means B1's joint metric must include adversarial baselines and B2's memory loop must defend against poisoning, and [[2602.04056|Modular-Safety-Guardrails]] argues the enforcement must be a *non-bypassable runtime layer* (Decision Gate + Action Gate decoupled from the foundation model) across action / decision / human-centered dimensions — a safety budget the same control-rate ceiling must also carry. The 3–5 Hz ceiling and the Safety survey's cascade-propagation finding are the two anchors that turn "nice to have" into "blocks deployment."

> [!tip] Structure-Preserving Beats Structure-Discarding
> Don't discard the structure the data carries — the principle A1 and A3 exploit, and the one C1 extends to *bodies*. C1 keeps the *morphology-invariant* task intent that native-joint tokenization throws away ([[2602.10556|LAP]] **>50%** zero-shot, [[2605.20811|Demo-JEPA]] **0.36** one-shot vs [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s 0% wall). The same move runs through A1's "the joint $p(o',a)$ is the natural loss" and A3's "physical laws are invariant by construction" — refusing to factor away load-bearing structure, whether that is coupled dynamics, physical law, or morphology-invariant intent.
